FIXED: `decompressChunk` (depot/decompress.ts) gained a `magic === 'VS'` branch that parses the VSZa/zsv layout and decodes via `zstddec`'s `ZSTDDecoder` (added as a direct `package.json` dependency — was previously only transitive via steam-user); the existing downstream `sha1(decompressed) === expectedSha` gate (unchanged) is the integrity check, not an internal crc32 re-check. Added the requested POST-DECRYPT diagnostic: `ChunkDecodeError` now carries an optional `decryptedPreview` (first 16 bytes of the POST-DECRYPT plaintext), populated at the `unknown_container` throw site and surfaced in the existing raw-response log line as `decryptedPreviewHex=`/`decryptedPreviewLatin1=` alongside the pre-decrypt `rawPreviewHex=`/`rawPreviewLatin1=` — the next hardware run can now directly tell "decrypt is wrong" (garbage) from "decoder still incomplete" (a still-unrecognized magic) for ANY chunk that still fails after this fix. SECOND, DISTINCT BUG FIXED: the retry storm — cycle-7's PART-3 requeue guard in `downloadFileChunks` (depot.ts) unconditionally re-queued ANY chunk that exhausted fetchChunk's attempts, including a DETERMINISTIC decode-stage failure (11,063 total attempt rotations in one hardware run; 555 on the single stuck chunk alone). Fixed by threading `lastErr`'s `.code` through fetchChunk's final exhausted-attempts throw (previously discarded) and adding `isDecodeStageError` (exported, decompress.ts) — `downloadFileChunks` now rethrows immediately (no requeue) when the failure is decode-stage (deterministic), while leaving the requeue-on-network-failure path (and the sha1 integrity gate, and stallTracker/abort semantics) completely unchanged. tsc clean; steam-specific suites 16/16, 667/667 tests (656 cycle-16 baseline + 11 net-new: zstd round-trip, unknown_container `.code`+`decryptedPreview`, `isDecodeStageError` coverage, fetchChunk final-error `.code` passthrough, decode-stage-vs-network requeue-guard distinction); full suite 82/82, 1565/1565 (1554 baseline + 11 net-new) — zero regressions. # Debug: Steam native install ~30s pre-download latency (cold CM/PICS) + non-abort-aware cancel

## Symptoms

- **Expected:** Clicking Install on a native Steam depot download starts streaming bytes within ~1-2s, like the real Steam client (which keeps its CM connection + PICS cache always-warm).
- **Actual:** ~30+ seconds elapse between clicking Install and the first download bytes. Reported consistently ("downloads take quite a while to start"), across installs, on the post-rebuild build that includes Phase 23's single-flight guard + progress heartbeat.
- **Coupled symptom (same root latency):** cancelling during that pre-download window removes the item from the DownloadManager queue immediately, but the game's library button stays "Installing" with the spinner for ~30s before clearing — because the cancel/abort can't interrupt the in-flight pre-download seam mid-call.
- **Error messages:** none — this is latency, not a failure.
- **Timeline:** Observed 2026-07-18 during Phase 23 Gate 1 real-hardware UAT (macOS, Apple Silicon). The 3s install-status poller is NOT the cause (30s != 3s).
- **Reproduction:** `enableSteamNativeInstall` opt-in ON; click Install on a native Steam title; time from click to first download bytes (and separately, cancel during that window and time how long the library button stays "Installing").

## Prior/related context (READ FIRST)

- `.planning/debug/steam-cm-drop-planbuild.md` (RESOLVED, 2026-07-17): established that `buildDepotPlan` (`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts:485`) calls `SteamUser.ensureConnected()` ONCE up front, then does a long sequence of PICS/manifest network calls (`fetchAppInfo`, `getOwnedSets`, `fetchDlcInfos`, per-depot `fetchDepotPlanEntry` → `getRawManifest` + `getDepotDecryptionKey`), all now wrapped in `withPlanBuildRetry` (`PLAN_BUILD_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3`, `PLAN_BUILD_RETRY_DELAY_MS=500`) which calls `ensureConnected()` + re-resolves `getDepotClient()` on failure. That session was about a CM *drop* causing a hard failure; THIS session is about cold-connect/PICS-warmup *latency* on a healthy connection — distinct symptom, same subsystem.
- Static analysis already done (confirm, don't assume): `ensureSteamClientReady` (`clientSetup.ts:92`) is synchronous/fast (isSteamClientInstalled + hasLibraryFoldersVdf) — NOT the culprit. The latency is downstream in `resolveSteamInstallTarget` (`installLocation.ts:205` → `fetchInstalldir`, PICS appinfo) and `buildDepotPlan`.
- Known upstream issue: node-steam-user #144 — `getOwnedApps()`/PICS cache population is slow for large libraries.

## Suspected root cause (verify with timing, don't assume)

1. **`SteamUser.ensureConnected()` cold-connect cost.** The steam-user CM connection (login via stored refresh token) is likely established cold on the first install of a session (or not kept warm), so the first `ensureConnected()` + PICS warmup pays ~30s. The real Steam client keeps this always-on. PRIORITY hypothesis.
2. **`withPlanBuildRetry` backoff compounding** if early PICS calls fail because the connection isn't ready (but 3×500ms ≈ 1.5s alone can't explain 30s unless each `ensureConnected()` attempt itself blocks ~seconds).
3. **Serial PICS fetches** (`fetchInstalldir` in resolveSteamInstallTarget, then `fetchAppInfo`/`getOwnedSets`/`fetchDlcInfos`/`fetchDepotPlanEntry` in buildDepotPlan) that could be pre-warmed or parallelized.

## Investigation plan

Add lightweight timing instrumentation (log start/end durations) around, in order: `ensureSteamClientReady`; the CM connection / `SteamUser.ensureConnected()` (cold vs warm); `resolveSteamInstallTarget` / `fetchInstalldir`; and each `buildDepotPlan` step (`fetchAppInfo`, `getOwnedSets`, `fetchDlcInfos`, the per-depot `fetchDepotPlanEntry` loop) + each `withPlanBuildRetry` attempt count. Then have the user reproduce ONE native install on real hardware and read the durations from the dev log to attribute the ~30s precisely before choosing a fix. This bug requires runtime timing — it cannot be pinned from static reading alone.

## Fix goals (acceptance)

- Native install begins streaming bytes within a few seconds of the click on a warm session (target: comparable to Steam, not ~30s).
- Likely direction (pick after timing): pre-warm the CM connection + PICS cache (e.g. at app start or Steam-library load) so per-install setup is cheap; and/or reduce/parallelize the pre-download PICS work.
- **Secondary (same root, do after latency understood):** make the pre-download seams abort-aware — thread `controller.signal` into `resolveSteamInstallTarget` and the `buildDepotPlan` phase (or race against the abort) so a cancel during the pre-download window resolves promptly instead of waiting ~30s. Currently `games.ts` `runNativeDepotDownload` only checks `controller.signal.aborted` BETWEEN seams, never during them.

## Scope fence

- Do NOT touch: the Phase-23 single-flight guard (games.ts nativeInstallsInFlight), the StateFlags 4-vs-1026 completeness gate, buildid threading, or file-mode logic (all recently verified in Phase 23).
- OUT OF SCOPE: the separate "reuben.exe encountered a serious problem" CrossOver bottle crash (bottle install path, different subsystem).
- Do NOT weaken D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 semantics: a network drop must never be reported as a user cancel, and a user cancel must still cancel.
- Do NOT regress GOG/Epic/Amazon or the existing chunk-phase / plan-build retry.

## Cancel/abort + auto-resume threads (user-reported, 2026-07-19, cycle 18)

During further real-hardware testing the user surfaced THREE new/related bug threads plus one non-issue, on top of this session's existing (Cycle 17, PARKED) zstd decode/throughput thread. User decision: **track all, fix in sequence, starting with cancel/abort (Thread A).**

### Thread A — cancel/abort not clean (FIXED cycle 18, HARDWARE-VERIFIED cycle 19 — see "Cycle 18" section at bottom)

**Symptom:** user cancels a native Steam download, confirms "yes / delete files", but the game stays showing "downloading" (state never transitions), and the abort doesn't cleanly interrupt.

**Log evidence (10:17 run):**
```
[Steam] SteamGame: aborting in-flight native depot download for appId 1091500
[ERROR][Backend] Aborting not possible. Could not find a matching abort controller for 1091500
[Timing] runNativeDepotDownload: downloadSteamDepots ... took 61748ms so far ... (status=cancelled); total since click 62026ms
[DownloadManager] Installation of 1091500 aborted!
```

**Root cause (confirmed, three distinct defects — see "Cycle 18" for full detail):**
1. `aborthandler.ts`'s `callAbortController` logs the misleading "Could not find a matching abort controller" error whenever the controller IS found but already aborted — hit on every cancel because `downloadqueue.ts`'s `stopCurrentDownload()` calls `callAbortController(appName)` directly, then `SteamGame.stop()` calls it AGAIN with the same id.
2. `fetchChunk` (depot/decompress.ts) never accepted/checked an external cancel `signal` at all — a cancel arriving mid-retry had to wait out the whole call (worst case ~144s) before `downloadFileChunks`'s `signal?.aborted` check (only before/after the call, never during) could take effect. This is the ~62s hang.
3. Two "Steam: ACF poller emits the real done" badge-suppression guards (`downloadmanager/utils.ts` and `downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts`) assumed a poller would always eventually clear the badge — false for a cancelled native install, which never starts a poller at all.

**Status:** FIXED, self-verified (tsc clean, 84/84 suites, 1585/1585 tests, 20 net-new), UNCOMMITTED. **HARDWARE-VERIFIED cycle 19 (2026-07-19):** clean cancel confirmed on real hardware — button cleared to "Install" within seconds, no misleading abort-controller error, `status=cancelled`, files deleted. Still UNCOMMITTED.

### Thread B — auto-resume after close/crash (ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED this cycle, cycle 19 — see "Cycle 19" section at bottom)

**Symptom:** user reports that after closing the app or a crash, an interrupted native Steam install RESUMES automatically on next launch; expected behavior is the user must manually resume — nothing should auto-start a download on launch.

**Tension noted (now resolved):** the Steam-side startup gate LOGS the correct intent — "Steam: appId 1091500/1295660 has an interrupted install detected on startup — surfacing as resumable, NOT auto-resuming" — and that gate (`library.ts`'s `scanDownloadingAppIds`/`steamResumePending` surfacing loop) IS behaving correctly on its own. The auto-resume comes from a COMPLETELY SEPARATE, runner-agnostic mechanism it has no knowledge of and no influence over.

**Root cause (confirmed, code-read + traced):** `main.ts:570-573` calls `initQueue()` unconditionally 5 seconds after EVERY app launch (unless CLI mode). `initQueue()` (`downloadqueue.ts`) immediately calls `installQueueElement()`/`updateQueueElement()` — i.e. actually starts the download — on whatever survived at the head of the persisted DownloadManager queue (`downloadManager.get('queue')`, electron-store), regardless of runner. If the app is closed or crashes mid-download, the queue head is left with a `startTime` set and no `endTime`; nothing on either the clean-close (`app.on('before-quit')`, `handleExit()` in `utils.ts`) or crash path removes/flushes it — both leave an IDENTICAL persisted entry (confirmed: `before-quit` only stops the Steam running-game poller; `handleExit()` on a pending-operations quit kills legendary/gogdl/nile child processes + calls all abort controllers but never touches the DM queue). So on the very next launch, the unconditional `initQueue()` call restarts that exact entry — this is a mechanism entirely independent of, and invisible to, the Steam startup gate.

**Scope decision (all-stores vs Steam-only):** auto-resume-on-launch is EXISTING, INTENDED behavior for GOG/Epic/Amazon — confirmed by this same session's own prior D-UAT-05 investigation (`21-UAT.md`), which explicitly noted "gog/legendary don't hit [the wedge bug] because their resume path registers the abort controller synchronously at the top" (i.e. they DO auto-resume, they just don't wedge) and listed "(c) consider NOT auto-resuming steam native installs on startup" as a candidate fix that was NOT chosen for D-UAT-05 at the time. This bug report is the first time manual-resume-only has been stated as the EXPECTED behavior, and only for Steam. Fix is therefore **Steam-specific** (gated on `runner === 'steam'`) — GOG/Epic/Amazon auto-resume-on-launch is preserved unchanged and regression-tested.

**Deconfliction:** the concurrent `steam-1026-download-restart` debug thread owns uncommitted changes ONLY in `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/library.ts`, `games.ts`, and their test files — confirmed via `git diff --stat` before any edit. This fix touches `src/backend/downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts` (already this session's own file, extending the Thread A/cycle-18 `debug/steam-cancel-abort-thread-a` work) and `src/backend/main.ts` (untouched by any other thread). Zero file overlap — no deconfliction needed, fix applied directly.

**Fix:** `initQueue(isStartup = false)` — new optional parameter, default `false` (preserves every existing call site's behavior: `resumeCurrentDownload` (Resume button), `addToQueue`'s `isIdle()` kick, the online-reconnect auto-resume). `main.ts`'s single 5s-post-launch call is now `initQueue(true)` — the ONLY call site that ever passes `true`. Inside the while loop, when `isStartup && element.params.runner === 'steam'`, the loop defers instead of starting it: no `installQueueElement` call, the persisted entry is left untouched, `currentElement` is (re)pointed at it (so pause/cancel/resume controls target the right element, never a stale already-completed one from earlier in the same startup call), and `queueState` is normalized to `'idle'` — which the frontend (`DownloadManagerItem.tsx`: `isPaused = ['idle','paused'].includes(state)`) already renders identically to a manually-paused download: a "Resume download" button, not an active download. GOG/Epic/Amazon queue heads reached during the same `isStartup=true` call are unaffected and auto-start exactly as before.

**Status:** FIXED, self-verified (tsc clean; `pnpm test` 84/84 suites, 1595/1595 tests — 10 net-new; zero regressions), UNCOMMITTED. Awaiting hardware verify.

**PREVIOUS (2026-07-19, cycle 21 — INBOUND CRASH REPORT follow-up: unbounded WASM allocation hardening fix for the zstd decode path, self-verified, UNCOMMITTED, NOT a confirmed root-cause diagnosis of the reported crash)** — see "Cycle 21" section (bottom of file) for the full evidence and fix. Resuming this session per the objective "continue debugging steam-install-slow-start"; read the whole file top-to-bottom (frontmatter, Current Focus, D-2 REOPENED section) before acting, per this session's own resume discipline. Found, by static reading only (no hardware available in this environment), that `decompressChunk`'s zstd (`magic === 'VS'`) branch (`depot/decompress.ts`) read an UNTRUSTED, network/decrypt-derived 32-bit `decompressedSize` straight out of the chunk's own footer bytes and passed it DIRECTLY into `ZSTDDecoder.decode()`, which hands it straight to the `zstddec` WASM module's own `malloc` (`node_modules/zstddec/zstddec.ts`) with ZERO validation on either side — a plausible mechanism for an unbounded native/WASM allocation attempt that ordinary JS `try`/`catch` (already present in `decompressWorker.ts`'s `handleDecodeMessage`, which never throws by design) cannot reliably guard against, unlike a catchable `RangeError`. This is structurally exactly the class of defect the still-open "INBOUND CRASH REPORT" note (above) flagged as its prime suspect for a trace-less "hard quit-to-desktop crash" against this exact parked worker_threads/WASM path. FIXED (hardening, not a confirmed root-cause fix): threaded the already-available, TRUSTED, manifest-derived `cbOriginal` (previously only checked AFTER decompression, at `decodeChunk`'s existing `data.length !== cbOriginal` gate) one layer earlier, into `decompressChunk` itself — the zstd branch now rejects (via the existing `size_mismatch` `ChunkDecodeError` classification, `isDecodeStageError`-covered, no new error code) a chunk whose footer-claimed `decompressedSize` disagrees with `cbOriginal` BEFORE ever calling into the WASM decoder, so a corrupted/malformed chunk can never reach the unguarded allocation at all. Optional parameter (`cbOriginal?`), skipped when omitted — every pre-existing direct-call test fixture (VZ/PK/unknown_container assertions) is unaffected. **Explicitly NOT claimed as a confirmed diagnosis of the actual reported crash** — no hardware reproduction was possible in this environment, so whether this specific gap is THE cause of that incident remains unconfirmed; this is a concrete, verified-by-reading, low-risk defense-in-depth fix for a genuine gap found while investigating that lead, following the same "bound-and-recover" discipline already established in this codebase (`decompressPool.ts`'s per-task timeout, T-21-15-03). Self-verified: tsc clean; steam-specific suite (`depotPrimitives.test.ts`) unaffected count + 2 net-new (matching-cbOriginal regression guard, mismatch-rejection proof); full suite 85/85 suites, 1607/1607 tests (1605 baseline + 2 net-new) — zero regressions; eslint zero errors/zero new warnings on both touched files. Also did a full top-to-bottom static re-check of `decompressPool.ts`'s worker `'error'`/`'exit'` handling and `decompressWorker.ts`'s `handleDecodeMessage` — both are correctly guarded (every worker-side JS/WASM exception is caught and turned into a normal `DecompressWorkerResponse`, and a worker crash/exit only ever affects that ONE worker via `handleWorkerFailure`/`replaceWorker`, never the parent process) — found no OTHER static gap in this path beyond the unvalidated-allocation-size one now fixed. **Remains UNCOMMITTED, session remains PARKED — the actual "does the zstd worker path hard-crash the app" question is still open pending hardware, D-2's hardware-verify checkpoint (still outstanding, see below) is unaffected/untouched by this cycle, and Thread C's own separate hardware capture is still queued.** See the "CHECKPOINT (human-action) — 2026-07-19 (cycle 21)" section (bottom of file) for what to verify next.

hypothesis: "an interrupted native Steam install auto-resumes on next launch" is caused by `main.ts`'s unconditional 5s-post-launch `initQueue()` call restarting whatever survived at the DownloadManager's persisted queue head — a mechanism entirely separate from, and invisible to, the Steam startup gate's already-correct "surfacing as resumable, NOT auto-resuming" log/intent (library.ts's `scanDownloadingAppIds` loop only sets a passive `steamResumePending` UI flag; it has no interaction with or knowledge of the DownloadManager's own electron-store-persisted `queue` array).
confirming_evidence:
  - "main.ts:570-573 (pre-fix): `setTimeout(() => { initQueue() }, 5000)` — unconditional, every launch, no gating on runner or on whether the queue head is a fresh vs. interrupted-mid-download item."
  - "downloadqueue.ts:92-126 (pre-fix) `initQueue`'s while loop: `element = getFirstQueueElement()` then immediately `await installQueueElement(element.params)` (or `updateQueueElement`) — no check of any kind before starting the download."
  - "downloadqueue.ts's `installQueueElement` (utils.ts:102-112) calls `libraryManagerMap[runner].getGame(appName).install(...)` — for a Steam runner mid-native-depot-download, re-entering `.install()` IS the auto-resume the user observed."
  - "main.ts's `before-quit` handler (line ~679) only calls `stopRunningPoll()`; `handleExit()` (utils.ts:241-280) on a pending-operations quit kills legendary/gogdl/nile child processes + `callAllAbortControllers()` but never touches the DM queue's persisted `queue` array — confirmed neither the clean-close nor the crash path flushes/marks the entry, so both leave an IDENTICAL persisted state feeding the same unconditional restart."
  - "21-UAT.md's own D-UAT-05 investigation (this session's prior work, 2026-07-16/17) already identified this exact mechanism verbatim: 'main.ts:572 calls initQueue() at app startup, which auto-resumes queue[0]' and listed 'NOT auto-resuming steam native installs on startup' as an unchosen candidate fix — confirms this is a real, previously-known mechanism, not a new guess."
  - "DownloadManagerItem.tsx:49: `const isPaused = state && ['idle', 'paused'].includes(state)` — confirms 'idle' and 'paused' already render an identical 'Resume download' UI, so normalizing queueState to 'idle' for a deferred item requires no new frontend work."
falsification_test: "If, after the fix, a hardware close-mid-download-then-relaunch STILL shows the game auto-transition to 'downloading'/'installing' without a Resume click, OR if a GOG/Epic/Amazon interrupted download STOPS auto-resuming on launch (a regression), the fix is wrong or incomplete."
fix_rationale: "Root cause is the unconditional startup call, not the Steam gate (which was already correct) — so the fix targets the ONE call site that must never auto-start a Steam item (main.ts's initQueue(true)) while leaving every OTHER call site (explicit Resume click, a fresh install while idle, connectivity-restore auto-resume) at the default isStartup=false, which processes normally. This fixes the root cause (the unconditional launch-time auto-start) rather than papering over the symptom (e.g. hiding the badge differently) or touching the already-correct Steam gate."
blind_spots: "Not hardware-verified yet — self-verified only via tsc/jest. A queued-behind-a-deferred-Steam-item scenario (addToQueue's isIdle() kick while a Steam item sits deferred) will process that Steam item too, since isIdle() is true and the kick uses isStartup=false by design — this is an intentional, FIFO-consistent choice (an explicit user/app action, not a silent unattended one) but was not explicitly covered by the requested hardware-verify steps; documented, not fixed further, out of stated scope. onConnectivityChange's own auto-resume (`autoPaused` flag) cannot accidentally start a deferred item because `autoPaused` is only ever set true by a prior 'went offline while running' event in the SAME session, and a deferred item was never running."

**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-19, cycle 18 — Thread A [cancel/abort] ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED across three distinct defects; self-verified, UNCOMMITTED, awaiting hardware verify — NOW HARDWARE-VERIFIED, see cycle 19 entry above)** — see "Cancel/abort + auto-resume threads (user-reported)" section (below) for the full Thread A/B/C/non-issue triage, and "Cycle 18" section (bottom of file) for the full evidence, root-cause, and fix.

hypothesis: "cancelling a native Steam install doesn't cleanly abort" was actually THREE independent, compounding defects, all confirmed by reading the actual code (not guessed): (1) `aborthandler.ts`'s `callAbortController` conflated "id never registered" with "id registered but the controller is ALREADY aborted" — both fell through to the same misleading `[ERROR][Backend] Aborting not possible. Could not find a matching abort controller for <id>` log, which fires on EVERY cancel because `downloadqueue.ts`'s `stopCurrentDownload()` calls `callAbortController(appName)` directly and then ALSO calls `SteamGame.stop()`, which calls `callAbortController(this.appId)` again for the identical id — the first call succeeds and aborts; the second (redundant but harmless) call hit the misleading branch. (2) `fetchChunk` (depot/decompress.ts) NEVER accepted or checked an external cancellation `signal` at all — its only `AbortController` was purely internal, wired ONLY to `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (15s). `downloadFileChunks` (depot.ts) checked `signal?.aborted` only BEFORE and AFTER calling `fetchChunk`, never DURING it — so a cancel arriving while a chunk was mid-retry (worst case `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS`=8 attempts x (15s timeout + up to 3s backoff) ~= 144s) had to wait for that whole call to naturally exhaust or succeed first. This is the exact mechanism behind the logged `[Timing] ... downloadSteamDepots ... took 61748ms so far ... (status=cancelled)` ~62s hang. (3) Two separate "Steam: ACF poller emits the real done — suppress it here" badge-clearing guards (`downloadmanager/utils.ts`'s `installQueueElement` finally block, and `downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts`'s `removeFromQueue`) both assumed a poller would ALWAYS eventually clear the "installing"/"downloading" badge for a Steam runner — true for a successful native install (a poller starts right before `runNativeDepotDownload` returns) but FALSE for a cancelled one (`runNativeDepotDownload` returns `{status: 'abort'}` on a cancelled outcome BEFORE it ever reaches the `startInstallPolling` call) — so nothing ever cleared the badge, matching the reported "the game stays showing 'downloading'" symptom exactly.
confirming_evidence:
  - "aborthandler.ts:12-18 (pre-fix): `if (abortController && !abortController.signal.aborted) { return abortController.abort() }` falls through to the error log when the controller IS found but `signal.aborted` is already true -- read directly, not inferred."
  - "downloadqueue.ts:302-306 `stopCurrentDownload`: calls `callAbortController(appName)` THEN `libraryManagerMap[runner].getGame(appName).stop(false)`, and games.ts:1251-1270 `SteamGame.stop()` calls `callAbortController(this.appId)` again when `nativeInstallsInFlight.get(this.appId)` is truthy -- both use the identical id value (appName === this.appId for steam), confirmed by reading both call sites."
  - "decompress.ts:678-847 (pre-fix) `fetchChunk`'s parameter list had NO `signal` parameter at all; its only `AbortController` (`controller`, line ~730) was constructed fresh per-attempt and wired ONLY to `setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS)` -- confirmed by reading the full function body."
  - "depot.ts:914-929 (pre-fix) downloadFileChunks' `fetchChunk(...)` call passed 12 positional arguments, none of them a signal -- `signal?.aborted` was checked at lines 898/930/941 (before/after the call), never threaded into the call itself."
  - "games.ts:900-914 `runNativeDepotDownload`: `if (outcome.status === 'cancelled') { return { status: 'abort' } }` returns BEFORE the `startInstallPolling` call a few lines below (only reached for a successful outcome) -- confirmed by reading the function in full."
  - "downloadmanager/utils.ts:116-130 (pre-fix) `installQueueElement`'s finally block: `if (runner !== 'steam' || deferredToSetup) { sendGameStatusUpdate(...status:'done') }` -- no exception existed for `status === 'abort'`."
  - "downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts:234-240 (pre-fix) `removeFromQueue`: `if (removedRunner !== 'steam') { sendGameStatusUpdate(...status:'done') }` -- unconditional suppression for steam, no way for `cancelCurrentDownload` to force it."
falsification_test: "If, after the fix, a hardware cancel STILL shows the misleading 'Could not find a matching abort controller' log for a genuinely-still-in-flight id (not a double-call), OR the library button still takes more than ~1 attempt-cycle to clear, OR the badge stays stuck on 'downloading' after a cancel, the fix is incomplete or wrong -- return to investigation for whichever of the three still fails."
fix_rationale: "Each of the three defects is fixed at its own root, not papered over: (1) aborthandler.ts now treats 'found but already aborted' as a no-op success, not a lookup miss -- the ONLY behavior change is suppressing a misleading log, `.abort()` semantics are unchanged. (2) fetchChunk now accepts an optional trailing `signal` parameter, forwards it into the SAME internal AbortController used for the timeout (so a cancel interrupts an in-flight `fetch()` immediately, not just between attempts), makes the inter-attempt backoff sleep abort-interruptible (mirroring depot.ts's own existing `delay(ms, signal)` pattern), and — critically for D-UAT-05 — treats an external-signal abort as its OWN distinct, un-retried, never-hostHealth-recorded outcome, so a user cancel can never be misclassified as a network timeout or bias future host selection. (3) the two badge-suppression guards each gained a narrowly-scoped exception (`wasAborted`/`forceStatusUpdate`) mirroring the EXACT PATTERN Phase 17 already established for `deferredToSetup` -- no new mechanism invented, reusing an already-reviewed shape."
blind_spots: "Not hardware-verified yet -- all three fixes are unit-tested (20 net-new tests) and self-verified via tsc/jest, but the ~62s hang time and the exact log sequence were only ever observed on real hardware; a fresh hardware cancel is needed to confirm the fix collapses that window to sub-second and clears the badge promptly. The pre-existing `callAllAbortControllers`'s `for...in` over a Map iterator bug (silently never iterates) was discovered but is UNRELATED to the per-game cancel path (only reachable from app-quit) and deliberately left unfixed this cycle -- flagged in code/tests as a separately-scoped follow-up. Thread B (auto-resume) and Thread C (decode/throughput) are explicitly NOT investigated this cycle -- see the triage section below."

**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-19, cycle 17 — ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED: missing zstd container support; SECOND BUG FIXED: retry-storm on deterministic decode failures; post-decrypt diagnostic added; self-verified, UNCOMMITTED, awaiting hardware capture)** — see "Cycle 17" section (bottom of file) for the full evidence, root-cause, and fix. Supplementary cycle-16 hardware data (10:09 run) proved the `unknown_container` decode failure is DETERMINISTIC PER-CHUNK: the SAME encrypted chunk (rawPreviewHex=b0cdc0612ef3...) was fetched 555 times spread across ALL 6 hosts and failed EVERY time, with byte-identical ciphertext confirmed across hosts — this RULES OUT a concurrency race and reframes cycle 16's "host correlation" as an artifact of retry-rotation + host preference (decodable chunks succeed on the first-preferred host; undecodable chunks fail everywhere, always). ROOT CAUSE: Valve's depot chunks can ALSO be zstd-compressed (magic `VSZa`/footer `zsv`) — a THIRD container format `decompressChunk` (depot/decompress.ts) never handled (only `VZ`/LZMA and `PK`/zlib existed). Confirmed via SteamKit2's own "Add support for zstd compressed depot chunks" issue (#1503) AND, decisively, `steam-user@5.3.0` — already this project's OWN dependency — bundles its own zstd decoder at `node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn_compression.js`, proving this is a real, current container format. FIXED: `decompressChunk` gained a `magic === 'VS'` branch using `zstddec` (now a direct `package.json` dependency, previously only transitive) to decode the VSZa/zsv layout; the existing `sha1(decompressed) === expectedSha` gate (T-21-03, unchanged) remains the sole integrity check. Added the requested post-decrypt diagnostic: `ChunkDecodeError.decryptedPreview` (first 16 bytes of the POST-DECRYPT plaintext, populated at the `unknown_container` throw site) now surfaces as `decryptedPreviewHex=`/`decryptedPreviewLatin1=` in the existing raw-response log line, alongside the pre-decrypt `rawPreviewHex=`/`rawPreviewLatin1=` — splits "decrypt is wrong" (garbage) from "decoder still incomplete" (unrecognized-but-consistent magic) for any chunk that still fails after this fix. SECOND, DISTINCT BUG FIXED (the checkpoint's "retry storm"): cycle-7's PART-3 requeue guard in `downloadFileChunks` (depot.ts) unconditionally re-queued ANY chunk exhausting fetchChunk's attempts, including a fully deterministic decode-stage failure — one hardware run hit 11,063 total attempt rotations (555 on the single stuck chunk alone), collapsing throughput to 8%/1164s/2.6 MiB/s declining. Fixed by threading `lastErr`'s `.code` through fetchChunk's final exhausted-attempts throw (previously a bare `Error`, discarding it) and exporting `isDecodeStageError` (decompress.ts); `downloadFileChunks` now rethrows immediately (no requeue) for a decode-stage failure, while the requeue-on-network-failure path, the sha1 integrity gate, and stallTracker/abort semantics are all completely unchanged. Self-verified: tsc clean; steam-specific suites 16/16, 667/667 tests (656 baseline + 11 net-new); full suite 82/82, 1565/1565 tests (1554 baseline + 11 net-new) — zero regressions. next_action: **awaiting a fresh hardware capture to confirm `unknown_container` no longer fires (or, if it still does for some other chunk, read its `decryptedPreviewHex`/`decryptedPreviewLatin1` fields) and that NO retry-storm rotation counts appear — CHECKPOINT (human-action) requested.** Do NOT mark this session resolved; remains UNCOMMITTED — session stays PARKED. This cycle's own top suspect (a regression in cycle 13's uncommitted `decompressPool.ts`/`decompressWorker.ts` worker_threads changes, specifically a byteOffset/byteLength-across-worker-boundary marshalling bug) is **DEFINITIVELY RULED OUT** via `git diff 33c108a0` — every line cycle 13 touched in both files is confined to the failure/error-object path; the success path (buffer marshalling, key/encrypted copy-before-transfer, the decode call itself) is byte-for-byte identical to HEAD, and `crypto.ts`'s `steamDecrypt` has zero diff and isn't even in `git status`. `unknown_container` is CONFIRMED pre-existing at HEAD, not a cycle-13 (or any uncommitted-cycle) regression — previously masked as generic `{Error:N}`. RESULT: a fresh hardware capture (08:42-08:43, ~90s streaming) of cycle 14's `CHUNK_FETCH_HEADERS` fix showed type=CDN hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) STILL `ok=0` — throughput still stuck at percent=2%/downSpeed~16 MiB/s at 90s, no CDN win from the header fix alone. BUT every failing host's reason is now `{unknown_container:N}` (was generic `{Error:N}` before cycle 13's reason-extraction fix) — a DECODE-stage error (`decompress.ts:109`): the fetch succeeds (HTTP 200), `steamDecrypt` runs against the depot key, and the decrypted bytes' magic is neither `VZ` nor `PK`. Per-host @90s: `cache2-akl-tpwr` a=1674 ok=1377 (the ONLY host that decodes cleanly); `cache1-akl-tpwr` a=256 ok=5; `cache1-akl-edgx` a=233 ok=4; `fastly` a=169 ok=0; `akamai` a=162 ok=0; `alibaba` a=158 ok=0 — every failure on every non-working host is `{unknown_container}`. Since the SAME depot key correctly decodes 1377 chunks on `cache2-akl-tpwr`, the key + decode logic are proven correct — the failing hosts are returning DIFFERENT/corrupted bytes for the identical chunk request. **BIG REFRAME:** `unknown_container` affects MOST hosts (both SteamCache AND CDN), not just CDN, and has almost certainly been the real throughput bottleneck ALL ALONG (masked as generic `{Error:N}` before cycle 13) — the download has been confined to whichever single host happens to return decodable chunks. This is now understood as a CHUNK DECODE / RESPONSE-HANDLING problem, NOT primarily a CDN-auth problem (that thread remains a confirmed dead end per cycle 14, but is no longer the leading explanation for the throughput gap at all — cycle 14's header fix may still have helped CDN hosts get past an HTTP-level filter, going from rejected to HTTP-200, but their 200 bodies don't decode). IMPLEMENTED this cycle: a raw-response-metadata diagnostic (`describeRawChunkResponse`, `depot/decompress.ts`) wired into `fetchChunk`'s catch block, gated on "did `encrypted` get assigned" (i.e. did decode actually run) — logs HTTP status, `Content-Type`/`Content-Encoding`/`Content-Length`, and a 16-byte hex+latin1 preview of the RAW (pre-decrypt) fetched body, via `logWarning`, on every decode-stage failure (not just `unknown_container` specifically — any `ChunkDecodeError`-tagged throw). This is purely observational: no change to fetch/decrypt/retry/host-selection/decode logic itself, only a new diagnostic log line. Three competing hypotheses for the NEXT capture to distinguish: (1) failing hosts return a non-chunk HTTP-200 body (HTML error/interstitial page) — decrypting HTML produces garbage; (2) a Content-Encoding mismatch (a host gzips already-binary chunk data, or an accept-encoding handling difference corrupts bytes per-host); (3) response truncation/wrong length (less likely — would more plausibly throw `size_mismatch`, not `unknown_container`, but worth ruling out). DECISION on cycle 14's `Accept: text/html,*/*;q=0.9`/`Accept-Charset` headers (hypothesis 1 flagged these as a possible active invitation for HTML error responses): left UNCHANGED this cycle — see "Cycle 15" section's "Decision on Accept headers" subsection for the full reasoning; the raw-body diagnostic decides this first, since dropping the headers now would be a behavior change that could confound the next capture's read (can't tell "headers still needed, still working" from "headers were never the issue, coincidentally still failing for another reason"). Self-verified: tsc clean; steam-specific suites 16/16, 656/656 tests (654 cycle-14 baseline + 2 net-new: a positive test proving the diagnostic fires with the right fields on an `unknown_container` failure, and a negative test proving it does NOT fire for a network-level failure that never reaches decode); full suite 82/82, 1554/1554 tests (1552 baseline + 2 net-new) -- zero regressions. next_action: **awaiting a short fresh hardware capture to read the raw-body metadata (status/Content-Type/Content-Encoding/Content-Length/hex+latin1 preview) for a failing host (any of akamai/fastly/alibaba/cache1-akl-tpwr/cache1-akl-edgx) — CHECKPOINT (human-action) requested, no code changes until that result comes back.** Do NOT mark this session resolved; remains UNCOMMITTED — session stays PARKED. Everything below this point, including the immediately-following cycle-14 entry, is historical investigation trail, superseded by this entry and by "Cycle 15" at the bottom of the file.

**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-19, cycle 14 — MAJOR BREAKTHROUGH, DECISIVE pcap of the REAL Steam client)** — see "Cycle 14 — DECISIVE pcap: CDN needs no auth, only a Steam User-Agent" section (bottom of file) for the full evidence, root-cause reframe, and fix. RESULT: a cleartext tcpdump of the real Steam client's own chunk downloads (10,722 requests to the type=CDN `alibaba` edge) proved the CDN edges need NO auth of any kind — every captured request was a bare `GET /depot/<id>/chunk/<sha>` with no token, no cookie, no Authorization header, no query string beyond the literal path. The ONLY meaningful difference from this codebase's pre-cycle-14 request was the real client's `User-Agent: Valve/Steam HTTP Client 1.0` plus static `Accept`/`Accept-Charset` headers — this codebase sent undici's bare default UA and no Accept headers at all. hypothesis (root cause, HIGH confidence): Steam's CDN edges (CloudFront-fronted) filter on User-Agent/Accept and reject non-Steam clients, which is the true mechanism behind the ~100% type=CDN failure rate and the resulting 2.5x throughput gap — NOT auth, NOT a missing token, NOT `AuthenticateDepotID` (that log line is CM-side/logical, never an HTTP step on the wire), NOT environmental. This also RETROACTIVELY CONFIRMS cycle 12's hardware-captured `eresult=1`+empty-token finding was correct all along: there genuinely is no token to hand out for these edges, because the modern SteamPipe CDN doesn't gate on one. The entire cycle 6-13 GetCDNAuthToken RPC investigation (cycles 6-9's timeout/gating work, cycle 10's TypeError root-cause, cycle 11's manual `_send` bypass, cycle 12's decisive eresult/body diagnostic, cycle 13's decode-stage reason-extraction fix) is now understood as a CONFIRMED PHANTOM — real, carefully-executed engineering against a mechanism that was never the actual blocker. IMPLEMENTED this cycle: `CHUNK_FETCH_HEADERS` constant (`depot/decompress.ts`) — `User-Agent: 'Valve/Steam HTTP Client 1.0'`, `Accept: 'text/html,*/*;q=0.9'`, `Accept-Charset: 'ISO-8859-1,utf-8,*;q=0.7'` — threaded into `fetchChunk`'s `fetch()` call, applied to every host (CDN and SteamCache alike; harmless-and-correct for SteamCache since the real client sends the same headers everywhere). Deliberately OMITTED `x-chunk-score` (dynamic per-chunk load hint, no legitimate value computable from a debugging session) and left `Accept-Encoding` to undici's own default (functionally equivalent auto-decode). Self-verified: tsc clean; steam-specific suites 16/16, 654/654 tests (653 cycle-13 baseline +1 net-new asserting the real headers are sent); full suite 82/82, 1552/1552 tests (1551 baseline +1 net-new) — zero regressions. next_action: **awaiting a fresh hardware run to confirm the type=CDN hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) flip from ~100% err to ok>0 and aggregate throughput rises — CHECKPOINT (human-action) requested.** Do NOT mark this session resolved; remains UNCOMMITTED — session stays PARKED. Recommendation on the now-confirmed-unnecessary cycle 10-12 GetCDNAuthToken machinery: leave it DORMANT this cycle (do not revert) — see "Cycle 14" section's "Recommendation" subsection for full reasoning; this does not block the header fix, which is the actual lever. Everything below this point, including the immediately-following cycle-12/13 entry, is historical investigation trail, superseded by this entry and by "Cycle 14" at the bottom of the file.

**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-19, cycle 12 hardware validation DECISIVE + cycle 13 reason-extraction fix) — see "Cycle 12 hardware validation — DECISIVE" and "Cycle 13" sections (bottom of file) for the latest state.** RESULT: cycle 12's eresult+raw-body diagnostic is hardware-CONFIRMED decisive — `eresult=1` (OK, server accepted the request), `rawBodyBytes=8` (real, well-formed, tiny protobuf, not garbage/empty), `decodedExpirationTime` live and incrementing across the ~60s proactive refreshes (1784415459 -> 1784415519/520 -> 1784415521). This proves the decode is correct AND the server is answering with fresh, well-formed data each call — ruling out BOTH a decode-side bug and a server-side rejection. Conclusion: the modern `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1` RPC works end-to-end (encode/send/CM-round-trip/decode all correct), but Steam returns a genuinely EMPTY token for these SteamPipe CDN edges — a well-formed, tokenless business response, not a bug in this codebase's Option B implementation. URL-append-token (Option B's entire premise) therefore CANNOT unlock the type=CDN hosts via this RPC — the RPC mechanism is understood; there is no token to append. OPEN QUESTION (one line, unresolved): the real Steam client's content_log shows `AuthenticateDepotID... Success!` firing on every type=CDN host, yet this RPC yields no token for us — so either the real client authenticates CDN edges via a DIFFERENT mechanism entirely, or an empty token genuinely means "no token needed" and the CDN chunk-fetch failures have an unrelated cause. That same hardware run's chunk-stream stats showed the generic `err=N{Error:N}` breakdown for every failing host (3 CDN + 2 of 3 SteamCache), blocking the ability to tell CDN-needs-real-auth (would show 403s) apart from CDN-suffers-the-same-network-issue-as-everything-else (would show the same code as the SteamCache hosts) — root-caused THIS cycle (see "Cycle 13" below) to `decodeChunk`'s plain, code-less `Error` throw sites PLUS (the more structural half) `DecompressPool`'s worker_threads message-passing boundary reconstructing every decode-stage failure as a bare `new Error(message)`, discarding whatever code the original error carried. Fixed via `ChunkDecodeError` (tags every decode-stage throw site: `bad_footer_magic`/`unknown_container`/`sha1_mismatch`/`size_mismatch`/`decode_failed`) threaded across the worker boundary (`decompressWorker.ts`/`decompressPool.ts`, `.code` field added to `DecompressWorkerResponse`), plus a defensively-hardened `networkErrorCode` helper in `attemptFailureReason` (decompress.ts) that walks `.code` -> `.cause.code` -> `.cause.cause.code` -> the first `AggregateError.errors[].code` under either level, for genuine network-level failures. IMPLEMENTED + self-verified this cycle (tsc clean; 653/653 steam-specific tests across 16 suites [+5 net-new regression tests]; 1551/1551 full suite across 82 suites [+5 net-new]; zero regressions). next_action: **awaiting a fresh hardware run to read the real per-host reason-code breakdown — CHECKPOINT (human-action + decision) requested.** Do NOT mark this session resolved; remains UNCOMMITTED — session stays PARKED. Everything below this point, including the immediately-following cycle-11 entry, is historical investigation trail, superseded by this entry and by "Cycle 12 hardware validation — DECISIVE"/"Cycle 13" at the bottom of the file.

**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-19, cycle 11 hardware validation + cycle 12 diagnostic) — see "Cycle 11 hardware validation" section (bottom of file) for that cycle's state.** RESULT: cycle 11's manual `_send` bypass is CONFIRMED working at the transport level — the RPC now encodes/sends and the callback ACTUALLY FIRES on real hardware (stack trace shows the response arriving via `_handleMessage <- _handleNetMessage <- _processMulti`, i.e. a real CM round-trip completed); cycle 10's TypeError is gone. A NEW failure surfaced one layer deeper: every `type=CDN` host (akamai/fastly/alibaba) now fails with `GetCDNAuthToken: empty response` — the callback fired, the eresult check either passed or was skipped (undefined), but the decoded body's `token` field was empty. The fail-fast guard (PART 3) held perfectly — zero stall, degrade-to-`''` worked, no regression vs the cycle-7/8 baseline. hypothesis (cycle 12): ambiguous without more data — could be (a) a decode-side bug in the manual bypass (wrong body arg, ByteBuffer-vs-Buffer mishandling, decoding a buffer that isn't the real payload) or (b) a genuine server-side rejection/empty business response (eresult non-OK and unsurfaced, or Steam legitimately has no token to offer for this host/depot). Static re-reading of the manual decode path this cycle found NO obvious shape/wiring bug (see Evidence) — the header's `eresult` field is decoded via steam-user's own normal, unaffected `_decodeProto` machinery (independent of the RPC's missing map-entry problem), and the request/response wire-shape matches `_sendUnified`'s own mechanism byte-for-byte — which raises (without confirming) the relative likelihood of (b) over (a), but this is not proof. Added diagnostic logging (`depot/cdnAuth.ts`) that captures the raw `eresult` value + raw response body shape/byte length + decoded `expiration_time` BEFORE rejecting with "empty response" — this is the single fact the NEXT hardware run needs to resolve the ambiguity. IMPLEMENTED + self-verified this cycle (tsc clean, 640/640 steam suites, 1536/1536 full suite — matches cycle 11's own baseline exactly, zero regression). next_action: **awaiting a fresh hardware run to read the new diagnostic log line — CHECKPOINT (human-action) requested, no further code changes until that result comes back.** Do NOT mark this session resolved; remains UNCOMMITTED. Everything below this point, INCLUDING the immediately-following cycle-10 entry, is historical investigation trail, superseded by this entry and by "Cycle 11 hardware validation" at the bottom of the file.

**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-19, cycle-10 gap-closure attempt) — see "Cycle 10 gap-closure attempt" section (bottom of file).** hypothesis: CONFIRMED — cycle-10's hardware TypeError was caused by `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1`'s method-name STRING being correct (verified against the installed `.proto`) but its `_Request`/`_Response` protobuf classes never having been wired into steam-user@5.3.0's internal encode/decode lookup map (exhaustively enumerated all 294 registered map keys — zero match for this RPC under any service name; only the sibling `GetManifestRequestCode` and the unrelated legacy `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken*` are present). This disproves the RESEARCH SPIKE's "one connecting line of code" assumption for the JS-wiring specifically (the method-name/field-name research itself was correct). PART-3's fail-fast guard is CONFIRMED airtight against this exact failure (a synchronous throw inside `_sendUnified`, called inside a `new Promise` executor, auto-rejects instantly — no timeout even needed) — downloads did not stall, exactly as the hardware run showed. next_action: **STOP-for-decision returned to orchestrator/user — no code changed this cycle.** Two options presented (see bottom section): (A) revert PART 1+2 to the cycle-7/8 hardware-verified baseline, park the type=CDN throughput gap as a known larger effort [LOW risk]; (B) implement a manual `_send`-level bypass (encode/decode directly via the compiled Schema classes, skipping only the missing `_sendUnified` map entry) [MODERATE risk, unverified new code path]. Await the decision before touching any code. Do NOT mark this session resolved. Everything below this point (earlier cycles' hypothesis/next_action) is historical investigation trail, superseded by this entry and by "Cycle 10 gap-closure attempt" at the bottom of the file.

hypothesis: PIVOT #2 (2026-07-18, second hardware reproduction, WITH the uncommitted CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS=15000/CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS=8 partial mitigation applied). The prior fix tripled survival time (6min -> 17.8min) but hit the SAME failure signature ("Steam servers dropped the connection. Retry to continue."), confirming it was a MITIGATION of chunk-retry headroom, not a fix of the underlying mechanism. RECONCILED the apparent contradiction the checkpoint raised: re-traced the full call chain (downloadSteamDepots -> getContentServerHosts [ONE CM call, pre-stream] -> downloadDepotFiles -> downloadSingleFile -> downloadFileChunks -> fetchChunk) end-to-end again and confirmed structurally, a SECOND time, that NOTHING in the chunk-stream loop ever touches the SteamUser client object — `job.key` (decryption key) is captured once at plan-build time and never re-fetched, hosts are captured once before the loop and never touch the client again inside the loop. The exact string "Steam servers dropped the connection. Retry to continue." is ALWAYS generated by `classifyDepotError`'s `connectionDropped` branch (depotErrors.ts:129-144), which matches on CDN-failure text signatures (`failed after \d+ attempts` [fetchChunk's own exhausted-retries message], `CDN \d`, `ECONNRESET`, `ETIMEDOUT`, `fetch failed`, `no content servers`) — it is NOT sourced from an actual steam-user CM disconnect event; there is no code path from a real CM drop into this message during the chunk-stream phase. So hypothesis (b) from the prior session is CONFIRMED again, not refuted: this is still CDN chunk-retry exhaustion, now surfacing later (~18min vs ~6min) because the raised attempts/timeout budget bought more time before SOME chunk still burned through all 8 attempts. New leads chased this cycle: (1) `getContentServerHosts` fetches the content-server list ONCE, before the stream starts, and NEVER refreshes it across the whole multi-minute stream — a real, concrete throughput-decay mechanism (degrading/rate-limited edges are never dropped or refreshed, just retried forever) that plausibly explains the user-observed progressive slowdown and the eventual retry-exhaustion; (2) the round-robin host selection (`hosts[(fileSeed + i) % hosts.length]`) is deterministic per-file (not per-chunk-random) — every chunk within one file starts its FIRST attempt against the SAME host, concentrating load/degradation exposure rather than spreading it; (3) the chunk-retry/rotation/timeout path was completely unobserved in logs (no instrumentation existed to confirm whether the 15s timeout ever fired, how many rotations occurred, or how throughput evolved during the ~18min stream) — added comprehensively this cycle (see Fix Applied). Applied a minimal, additive, non-behavior-changing-to-existing-tests fix for lead (1): a periodic (3min) content-server-list refresh in `downloadSteamDepots` that mutates the shared `hosts` array in place (splice), so `downloadDepotFiles`/`downloadFileChunks`/`fetchChunk`'s existing round-robin selection automatically sees a refreshed host pool without any change to the selection algorithm itself (preserves the existing "rotates hosts / throws after `attempts`" test contract in depotPrimitives.test.ts, which relies on a STATIC host array).

reasoning_checkpoint_2:
  hypothesis: "The mid-stream 'Steam servers dropped the connection' failure recurring after the chunk-retry-headroom fix is STILL CDN chunk-retry-exhaustion (never a real CM drop) — the same mechanism as before, just requiring more elapsed time to manifest because the fix only raised the PER-CHUNK retry budget, not the underlying cause of chunk failures accumulating over a long stream (likely a static, never-refreshed, degrading content-server host list)."
  confirming_evidence:
    - "classifyDepotError (depotErrors.ts:129-144): the 'connectionDropped' message is reached by a text-pattern match on CDN/network failure signatures INCLUDING fetchChunk's own exhausted-retries throw text ('failed after N attempts: ...') — re-confirmed by reading the full function again this cycle."
    - "Re-traced every network/client call in the chunk-stream call chain (downloadDepotFiles L1040 -> downloadSingleFile L847 -> downloadFileChunks L785 -> fetchChunk decompress.ts L142): zero references to the SteamUser client object anywhere in this chain; the decryption `key` and `hosts` are both captured ONCE before the loop starts (from buildDepotPlan and getContentServerHosts respectively) and never re-fetched mid-stream."
    - "getContentServerHosts (depot.ts:1376) is called exactly ONCE per downloadSteamDepots invocation (depot.ts:1469), before downloadDepotFiles starts, confirmed via the single call site — no refresh mechanism existed before this cycle's fix."
    - "Host selection in fetchChunk is `hosts[(chunk.attemptSeed ?? 0 + i) % hosts.length]` where attemptSeed is set ONCE per file as `fileSeed % hosts.length` (depot.ts:808) — every chunk in the same file shares the same first-attempt host, confirmed by reading downloadFileChunks."
  falsification_test: "On the next hardware reproduction WITH the new instrumentation: if the periodic per-host stats log shows NO host with an elevated timeout/error rate over time (i.e. failure rate stays flat, not increasing, across the ~18min run) AND the mid-stream failure still recurs, the 'degrading content server' explanation is refuted for that run and the true cause is something else (e.g. genuinely low last-mile bandwidth, or a CDN-wide rate-limit unrelated to specific hosts) — return to investigation. If the failure does NOT recur and per-host stats show healthy, refreshed host rotation, this cycle's fix is supported (though still needs a clean run to fully confirm)."
  fix_rationale: "The periodic content-server refresh extends (never replaces) the existing 'resolve hosts once, round-robin forever' mechanism — it only refreshes the POOL of hosts available to the existing unchanged round-robin/backoff/rotation algorithm in fetchChunk, via in-place array mutation, so no selection-logic test (which asserts deterministic rotation over a STATIC host array) is affected. It targets the mechanism most directly explainable by static analysis (a host list that can only degrade, never recover, over an 18min window) rather than guessing at a symptom-level tweak."
  blind_spots: "No fresh hardware reproduction has been run with the new instrumentation or the content-server-refresh fix yet — the 'progressive degradation of specific hosts' explanation is a plausible mechanism given the static-list code evidence, not a captured/measured fact from a real run (the previous 2 reproductions had zero per-host visibility). The per-file deterministic host-assignment concentration (lead 2 above) is documented but NOT changed this cycle — changing fetchChunk's selection algorithm risks the existing depotPrimitives.test.ts rotation-order test contract and was deliberately left alone pending real evidence that it matters. It's also possible the throughput decay is unrelated to specific-host degradation entirely (e.g. DecompressPool backlog, or genuine link-level throttling by the ISP/CDN under sustained multi-minute load) — the new instrumentation is designed to distinguish these but hasn't been run yet."

next_action: Have the user reproduce ONE more fresh large native Steam install (same Cyberpunk 1091500 or similar) on real hardware, with this cycle's instrumentation + content-server-refresh fix in place, and capture the dev log. Read back: (a) the new `[Timing] chunk-stream stats @Ns` lines (emitted every ~15s) for per-host attempt/success/timeout/error counts and downSpeed/diskSpeed trend over the run — confirms or refutes progressive host degradation; (b) the `[Timing] downloadDepotFiles: first bytes written after Nms` line — explains the "long lag before initiated" observation; (c) the periodic `getContentServerHosts: periodic refresh replaced host list` lines — confirms the refresh is firing and whether the host list actually changes; (d) whether the mid-stream "Steam servers dropped the connection" failure recurs at all, and if so, whether classifyDepotError's `connectionDropped` branch was reached via a `failed after 8 attempts` message (confirms CDN chunk-exhaustion, not CM) — grep the raw (pre-classification) failure message if needed by temporarily checking `result.failures[0].error` before it's classified.

reasoning_checkpoint:
  hypothesis: "Two concrete, directly-observed code defects in the chunk-fetch retry path (not the setup seam) jointly explain both chunk-stream symptoms. (1) fetchChunk's CDN fetch() call (depot/decompress.ts:151) has NO timeout/AbortSignal at all -- a slow/hung content-server connection blocks that worker slot for an unbounded time, because the existing 'rotate hosts on failure' retry only ever fires on a THROWN error, never on a hang. This can produce exactly the reported 'stuck at 0%' / '~30s between graph updates' pattern. (2) downloadFileChunks (depot.ts) hard-codes attempts=4 for fetchChunk, which per the CDN's own documented ~16%-per-chunk-failure-rate-at-concurrency-8 (decompress.ts's own fetchChunk docstring) gives too little headroom for a large modern game (thousands of ~1MB chunks) -- across enough chunks the odds that AT LEAST ONE exhausts all 4 attempts is high. downloadSteamDepots (depot.ts:1465-1470) then escalates that ONE per-file failure to a whole-install status:'error' using the generic 'Steam servers dropped the connection' copy from classifyDepotError -- even though chunk fetches are plain HTTPS calls to the CDN host and never touch the authenticated SteamUser CM/session object at all, so this is NOT an actual Steam session drop (distinct from the RESOLVED steam-cm-drop-planbuild session's real CM-drop), just a mislabeled CDN chunk-retry-exhaustion event that discards the perceived success of an otherwise-mostly-completed multi-hundred-MB download (the bytes ARE safe -- finalizeToSteam already wrote the 1026 partial state before returning 'error' -- but the UX reports a hard failure)."
  confirming_evidence:
    - "decompress.ts fetchChunk: the fetch() call to the CDN host is a single-argument call, no signal/timeout option anywhere in the function body (confirmed by reading the full implementation, decompress.ts:130-164)."
    - "depot.ts downloadFileChunks calls fetchChunk(hosts, depotId, depotChunk, key, lzma, 4, decode, ...) -- the literal 4 is hard-coded at the call site, not derived from hosts.length, file size, or any named constant (unlike CHUNK_CONCURRENCY/FILE_CONCURRENCY which ARE named exported constants a few lines above)."
    - "depot.ts:1465-1470: if (result.failures.length) return { status: 'error', error: classifyDepotError(result.failures[0].error).message } -- confirmed the ENTIRE run is marked 'error' off a single failures[0] entry regardless of how many other files/depots succeeded, and finalizeToSteam() (line 1460, just above) already ran and wrote the safe partial state before this check."
    - "Traced every identifier in fetchChunk's CDN request path -- it references only host/depotId/sha (plain strings), never the SteamUser client/session object -- confirming a genuine CM/session disconnect structurally CANNOT surface through this code path; it can only be a CDN HTTP failure, yet classifyDepotError's message reads as a Steam-connection-level drop."
    - "getContentServerHosts (depot.ts:1363, a live client.getContentServers CM round-trip) executes AFTER the fully-instrumented buildDepotPlan (measured 4.1s total) and BEFORE downloadDepotFiles's first byte, yet has ZERO [Timing] instrumentation -- confirmed via grep across the steam store manager, which shows every buildDepotPlan/resolveSteamInstallTarget/ensureConnected seam covered but nothing for getContentServerHosts -- even though the SAME reproduction run that measured the 4.1s setup ALSO reported 'stuck at 0% a while'."
  falsification_test: "On the next hardware reproduction with getContentServerHosts now instrumented: if click-to-first-byte is still ~4.1s (getContentServerHosts adds negligible time) AND per-chunk fetch latencies stay healthy with no long individual stalls even without a timeout ever firing, hypothesis (1) is refuted for that run and the ~30s gaps have a different cause (bandwidth, decode-pool backlog) -- return to investigation. If a large install completes with zero per-file failures across every chunk, hypothesis (2) is weakened for that run (though a single clean run doesn't fully disprove a low residual per-chunk failure probability at scale)."
  fix_rationale: "Both changes EXTEND (never replace) the existing retry-across-content-servers mechanism, exactly as the scope fence permits for the CM-drop sub-thread: (a) an AbortController-bound per-attempt timeout makes a hung/slow content-server edge fail FAST and rotate to the next host within a predictable window instead of the OS/undici default (unbounded from this code's perspective) -- targets the MECHANISM (unbounded per-attempt latency), not just the symptom; (b) raising the attempts budget (still bounded, still backed off, still host-rotating) gives genuinely transient per-chunk CDN failures the headroom a multi-thousand-chunk install statistically needs, reducing the odds ANY single chunk exhausts its budget and cascades into the misleading whole-install 'dropped connection' failure. NOT touching the failures.length -> 'error' escalation logic itself or FILE_CONCURRENCY/CHUNK_CONCURRENCY -- those are broader behavior/UX and throughput-tuning questions outside a minimal, evidence-backed fix, and risk regressing the existing D-06 Retry-UI contract already covered by tests."
  blind_spots: "No fresh hardware reproduction has been run WITH these fixes yet -- the throughput/stall claim is inferred from a directly-observed code defect (no timeout exists, which is a fact, not a guess) plus the qualitative user report, not from a captured hang. The real ~30s gaps could instead (or also) be caused by DecompressPool worker-thread backlog under load, genuinely low link bandwidth, or getContentServerHosts latency itself (now instrumented but unconfirmed until the next repro). Not changing FILE_CONCURRENCY(8)/CHUNK_CONCURRENCY(4) -- if those bounds are simply too low for achievable bandwidth, this fix won't address that. The ~16%-failure-rate compounding-probability reasoning is a rough independence-assumption estimate, not validated real-world telemetry from this codebase."

next_action: Apply the two chunk-retry fixes (bounded per-attempt fetch timeout in fetchChunk; raise the hard-coded attempts=4 to a higher named constant at depot.ts's fetchChunk call site) plus getContentServerHosts [Timing] instrumentation (closes the last unmeasured setup-to-stream gap for the next repro). Run tsc + the 4 steam test suites + full jest suite to confirm no regression, then request a human-verify checkpoint on real hardware (fresh large native install) to confirm faster/steadier progress and no more mid-stream "dropped connection" hard-fail.

reasoning_checkpoint_3:
  hypothesis: "Plain round-robin host selection in fetchChunk gives a persistently-failing content-server host (0% success across hundreds of attempts) the SAME share of attempts -- and the same exponential-backoff SLEEP time between attempts -- as a genuinely-healthy host. Across dozens of concurrent chunk-fetch worker slots, this collapses the aggregate attempt rate toward zero during a long stream (workers sleeping through backoff between doomed attempts against bad hosts, never hung on any single request) even though zero timeouts fire -- explaining both the throughput decay AND the eventual retry-exhaustion 'Steam servers dropped the connection' failure. Health-aware selection (deprioritize/skip proven-bad hosts, concentrate attempts on proven-good ones, cap the backoff ceiling) directly targets this mechanism."
  confirming_evidence:
    - "Third hardware reproduction (fresh Cyberpunk 1091500, macOS, Auckland/NZ) with cycle 2's per-host stats instrumentation showed, at time of failure (~908s): alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com a=258 ok=0 err=258 (0% success, ~57ms avg -- fast-fail, never once succeeded across 258 attempts) and cache1-akl-edgx a=293 ok=36 err=257 (~12% success) coexisting in the SAME 6-host round-robin pool as cache1/cache2-akl-tpwr (94%/93% success, ~285-298ms avg) for the entire run."
    - "timeouts=0 for the whole run despite a ~7min stall with totalAttempts nearly flatlined (~340 attempts / ~0.8 per second) -- confirms workers were not hung on any single in-flight request (the 15s CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT never engaged); the only mechanism that explains a near-zero attempt rate with zero timeouts is workers spending most of their time asleep in the exponential backoff between attempts, which fires on every non-timeout failure too."
    - "fetchChunk's pre-cycle-3 backoff was uncapped (`200 * 2**i`) -- at CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS=8, a single chunk that lands mostly on bad hosts could sleep up to ~25.4s cumulative (200+400+800+1600+3200+6400+12800ms) doing zero useful work, and the per-file deterministic `attemptSeed = fileSeed % hosts.length` means the FIRST attempt for a given file is fixed to whichever host that file's seed lands on -- giving every host, good or bad, statistically equal representation as a first pick."
    - "rotations=1328 recorded by cycle 2's own instrumentation over the run -- confirms the retry-across-hosts mechanism WAS firing constantly (workers kept retrying), consistent with 'wasting effort cycling through known-bad hosts' rather than 'stuck/hung'."
  falsification_test: "On the next hardware reproduction with hostHealth's per-host stats now tagged UNHEALTHY/healthy in the periodic [Timing] chunk-stream stats log: if a 0%-or-near-0%-success host (like alibaba/cache1-akl-edgx) is STILL receiving a comparable attempt share to the two good hosts after this fix (i.e. the UNHEALTHY tag is present but attempts keep flowing to it at the same rate as before), the scoring/blacklist mechanism is not actually suppressing bad-host attempts and this hypothesis's proposed fix has failed even if the diagnosis itself was correct. If totalAttempts/sec stays healthy through what was previously the ~7min stall window AND the two good hosts' ok= counts keep climbing (no freeze) for the whole run, the fix is supported. If the install still hard-fails with the same signature despite bad hosts being visibly deprioritized, the diagnosis was incomplete (a cause besides host-selection/backoff-starvation is also at play, e.g. genuine last-mile bandwidth ceiling or DecompressPool backlog) and this cycle's fix should be treated as a partial mitigation only, same as cycle 1."
  fix_rationale: "Host-scoring EXTENDS fetchChunk's existing retry-across-hosts mechanism rather than replacing its shape: on a cold start (no attempt history for any host) the new HostHealthTracker's stable-sort ordering is byte-for-byte identical to the old plain round-robin (verified by a dedicated test) -- deprioritization only emerges as real per-host history accumulates, which is exactly the condition the diagnosis showed developing over the run. Capping the backoff ceiling (200,400,800,1600,3000,3000,3000... vs uncapped 200,400,800,1600,3200,6400,12800) bounds the worst-case per-chunk time-wasted-in-backoff from ~25.4s to ~12s regardless of which hosts a chunk happens to land on, addressing constraint #3 (retry/backoff must not collapse the attempt rate to ~0) even in the residual case where health-aware selection hasn't yet fully learned a host is bad. Removing the disproven cycle-2 periodic host-list refresh (Steam returned an IDENTICAL 6-host list on every refresh in the cycle-2 hardware run, per its own '(6 -> 6)' log line) eliminates a mechanism that could never have helped and simplifies the fix to the one that addresses the actual, evidenced mechanism."
  blind_spots: "No fresh hardware reproduction has been run WITH this cycle's fix yet -- the throughput-improvement claim is inferred from a directly-observed code defect (plain round-robin treats all hosts equally regardless of proven track record, which is a fact) plus a real per-host stats snapshot from the SAME failure mode this fix targets, not from a captured clean run. The 'good-host freeze' (ok= stopped climbing on cache1/cache2-akl-tpwr around 480s) is explained by this fix's mechanism (attempts being wasted on bad hosts instead of flowing to good ones) but NOT independently confirmed as the sole cause -- an alternative explanation (e.g. the CDN itself rate-limiting a client IP after sustained load, independent of which hosts are selected) remains untested and would NOT be fixed by host-scoring alone; the directive's own REALISM constraint acknowledges this ('cannot manufacture bandwidth the region's CDN doesn't provide'). The composite score's latency-divisor (LATENCY_SCORE_DIVISOR_MS=50000) and the unhealthy thresholds (MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES=5, MIN_SAMPLES_FOR_UNHEALTHY=5, MIN_SUCCESS_RATE_FOR_HEALTHY=0.35) are reasoned choices consistent with the exact per-host numbers observed in the diagnosis (would correctly flag alibaba 0/258 and cache1-akl-edgx ~12% as unhealthy, while NOT flagging fastly's ~70%-but-slow host as unhealthy, only deprioritizing it via latency) -- not independently tuned against a live run yet."
