Read back: (a) whether the mid-stream "Steam servers dropped the connection" failure recurs at all, and if so at what elapsed time (compare to the ~908s/~18min-then-~15min pattern across cycles 1-3); (b) the `[Timing] chunk-stream stats @Ns` lines -- confirm previously-bad hosts (alibaba, cache1-akl-edgx) are now tagged UNHEALTHY and receiving a visibly smaller attempts= share than the good Auckland caches; (c) whether totalAttempts/sec stays healthy through what was previously the failing window (no ~0.8/s flatline); (d) whether the two good hosts' ok= counts keep climbing for the whole run (no freeze). This is the next and expected FINAL verification checkpoint for this session -- if confirmed, proceed to archive_session; if the failure still recurs, treat this as a further partial mitigation and continue investigating (candidates: last-mile bandwidth ceiling, DecompressPool backlog, or a CDN-side per-IP rate limit independent of host selection).

## Evidence

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — User report (Phase 23 Gate 1 UAT, macOS): native installs consistently take ~30+s before download starts, vs Steam starting instantly; cancel during that window leaves the library button on "Installing" ~30s. No error. Static analysis eliminated ensureSteamClientReady (synchronous) and pointed at the CM/PICS-dependent resolveSteamInstallTarget + buildDepotPlan path.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/user.ts` `connectSteamUserClient()` (line ~210) — confirmed `new SteamUserLib({ enablePicsCache: false })`. found: PICS caching is explicitly disabled for the whole steam-user client instance, so every `getProductInfo` call anywhere in the codebase is a live, uncached CM network round-trip, every time, forever (not just on cold-connect). implication: this is a candidate root cause for the "across installs" (not just first-install) recurrence — a warm CM connection does NOT make subsequent installs cheap, because PICS results are never cached locally.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/user.ts` `connectSteamUserClient()` — found: on a cold/disconnected client, `client.logOn({ refreshToken })` waits for the `loggedOn` event with a 15000ms fallback timeout (resolves with placeholder username on timeout). implication: worst-case a cold `ensureConnected()` alone could consume up to 15s; needs runtime timing to see actual (non-timeout) logOn latency.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/installLocation.ts` `fetchInstalldir()` (line 138) vs `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts` `fetchAppInfo()` (line 344) — found: BOTH independently call `client.getProductInfo([numericAppId], [], true)` for the exact same appId, back-to-back in the same install (`resolveSteamInstallTarget` runs before `buildDepotPlan` in games.ts `runNativeDepotDownload`, games.ts:835→depot.ts:1385). implication: at minimum one redundant full PICS round-trip per install when the CM connection is already warm (fetchInstalldir's `getClient()` check only skips this call when the client is NOT yet connected).
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts` `buildDepotPlan()` (line 485-553) — found: the call sequence is fully serial: `ensureConnected()` → `withPlanBuildRetry(fetchAppInfo)` → `withPlanBuildRetry(getOwnedSets)` → `withPlanBuildRetry(fetchDlcInfos)` → a `for` loop calling `withPlanBuildRetry(fetchDepotPlanEntry)` ONE DEPOT AT A TIME (no `Promise.all`, unlike the chunk-download phase's `CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`/`FILE_CONCURRENCY` bounded parallelism). implication: total pre-download latency scales linearly with depot count, each step paying its own uncached network round-trip; `getOwnedSets` in particular (`client.getProductInfo([], packageIds, true)` over the FULL owned-package list) is the exact operation node-steam-user issue #144 (already noted in session) flags as slow for large libraries.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/games.ts` `runNativeDepotDownload()` (line 811-879) — found: confirms exact top-level call order (`ensureSteamClientReady` → `resolveSteamInstallTarget` → `downloadSteamDepots` which internally calls `buildDepotPlan` then the chunk-download loop); `controller.signal.aborted` is checked only BETWEEN these three top-level awaits, never during them — confirms the coupled stuck-cancel symptom's mechanism (same seams, not abort-aware mid-call) exactly as suspected in the session's secondary objective.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: added lightweight `[Timing]` duration-logging instrumentation (no behavior change) at every seam above: `SteamUser.ensureConnected`/`connectSteamUserClient` (cold vs already-connected fast path), `resolveSteamInstallTarget`/`fetchInstalldir`, `buildDepotPlan`'s `withPlanBuildRetry` wrapper (now logs a label + duration + attempt count per step: fetchAppInfo, getOwnedSets, fetchDlcInfos, and each per-depot `fetchDepotPlanEntry:<depotId>`), and `runNativeDepotDownload`'s three top-level awaits. All logs use `logInfo` with `LogPrefix.Steam` so they show up in the existing dev log without any new log channel.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx jest src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/{depot,installLocation,user,games}.test.ts` 287/287 pass; full suite `npx jest` 1459/1459 pass, 80/80 suites. found: instrumentation is additive-only (one new optional `label?: string` param on the private `withPlanBuildRetry`, all other changes are `logInfo` calls) — no existing behavior, GOG/Epic/Amazon paths, or abort semantics affected. implication: safe to ship for the reproduce-and-observe checkpoint; a follow-up commit will strip this instrumentation once the ~30s is attributed (or convert the most useful lines to permanent debug-level logs, TBD after runtime evidence).

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 (Gate 1 fresh Cyberpunk 1091500 install, macOS Apple Silicon, warm session ~8min post-launch) — REPRODUCTION DATA (satisfies the checkpoint). `[Timing]` breakdown click→stream: ensureSteamClientReady 0ms; SteamUser.ensureConnected "already connected (fast path, 0ms)" (CM warmed at app startup — startup cold-connect was only 1598ms/545ms); resolveSteamInstallTarget/fetchInstalldir getProductInfo 285ms; buildDepotPlan TOTAL 3804ms = fetchAppInfo 200ms + getOwnedSets 265ms + fetchDlcInfos 235ms + fetchDepotPlanEntry[1460472] 1705ms + [2060314] 659ms + [2224089] 733ms. **TOTAL setup (click→first stream) ≈ 4.1s.** found: the pre-download setup is fast on a warm session — nowhere near ~30s; getOwnedSets was 265ms (NOT the #144 slowness). implication: the perceived slowness is NOT the setup seam the session instrumented; it is downstream in the chunk-stream phase (uninstrumented).
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — NEW FAILURE, distinct from RESOLVED [[steam-cm-drop-planbuild]]: after ~363s of streaming the install errored — `runNativeDepotDownload: downloadSteamDepots (buildDepotPlan + stream) took 362847ms (status=error)` → `depot install failed for appId 1091500: Steam servers dropped the connection. Retry to continue.` → DownloadManager marked it failed. App did NOT crash (deferred native-decompress fatal did not fire; PID stayed alive). This CM drop is MID-STREAM (~6min in), not plan-build (~3s); the chunk loop's retry-across-content-servers did not recover it. User-observed during this run: stuck at 0% a while, ~30s between graph updates, overall much slower than the native Steam client → points to low chunk throughput.

## CHECKPOINT (human-action) — SATISFIED 2026-07-18

Reproduction timing captured (see Evidence). The pre-download-latency hypothesis is disproven; investigation PIVOTS to the chunk-stream phase (throughput + mid-stream CM-drop). Session remains "investigating" under the new hypothesis.

## Eliminated

- hypothesis: "ensureSteamClientReady (clientSetup.ts:92) hangs" — ELIMINATED via static read: it only does synchronous isSteamClientInstalled + hasLibraryFoldersVdf checks and returns immediately; no CM wait.
- hypothesis: "the 3s install-status poller causes the delay" — ELIMINATED: 30s is ~10 poll cycles, not one interval; the latency precedes any download/poll activity.
- hypothesis: "the ~30s is the pre-download SETUP seam (cold-connect cost / getOwnedSets #144 / serial-uncached buildDepotPlan)" — ELIMINATED by 2026-07-18 reproduction timing: warm-session setup is ~4.1s total (ensureConnected 0ms, resolveTarget 285ms, buildDepotPlan 3804ms incl. getOwnedSets 265ms). Even the startup cold-connect was ~1.6s. The setup is NOT the source of the perceived slowness — it is the chunk-stream phase. (The `enablePicsCache:false` redundant-round-trip finding remains a real minor inefficiency but is not the ~30s.)

## Fix Applied (2026-07-18, pending human hardware re-verification)

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: `fetchChunk`'s CDN `fetch()` call previously had NO timeout/AbortSignal at all — a slow/hung content-server connection could block a chunk-worker slot for an unbounded time, since the existing "rotate hosts on failure" retry only ever fires on a THROWN error, never on a hang. Added `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000` and wired an `AbortController` into the `fetch()` call so a stuck attempt now aborts, falls into the existing catch/backoff/host-rotation path, and rotates within a bounded window. Extends the existing mechanism — same attempt loop, same backoff schedule, same host rotation, just bounded per attempt.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: raised the hard-coded `attempts=4` passed to `fetchChunk` (in `downloadFileChunks`) to a new named `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS = 8` constant. `fetchChunk`'s own docstring documents a ~16% per-chunk transient failure rate at `FILE_CONCURRENCY=8` without retry; at `attempts=4` a large modern game's thousands of ~1MB chunks made it near-certain SOME chunk would exhaust every attempt, and `downloadSteamDepots` (depot.ts:1465-1470) escalates ANY single per-file failure to a whole-install `status:'error'` using the misleading "Steam servers dropped the connection" copy — even though chunk fetches are plain HTTPS CDN calls that never touch the authenticated SteamUser CM/session object (confirmed by tracing every identifier in the request path), so this is NOT a real Steam session drop, unlike the RESOLVED `steam-cm-drop-planbuild` session. Doubling the retry budget (still bounded, still backed off, still host-rotating) makes exhausting all attempts for a single chunk far less likely without changing the retry mechanism's shape.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: added `[Timing]` instrumentation to `getContentServerHosts` (a live, previously-unmeasured `client.getContentServers` CM round-trip that runs AFTER the fully-instrumented `buildDepotPlan` and BEFORE `downloadDepotFiles`'s first byte) — closes the only remaining unmeasured gap between the measured 4.1s setup and the start of streaming, for the next hardware reproduction to confirm/deny.
- Deliberately NOT changed: `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` → whole-install `status:'error'` escalation logic itself (broader UX/behavior question, outside a minimal fix, risks the existing D-06 Retry-UI test contract); `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY` (throughput-tuning question, not confirmed as the bottleneck without a fresh timed repro).

**Self-verification (2026-07-18):** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 4 steam suites + `depotPrimitives.test.ts`: 5 suites, 311/311 pass (the `global.fetch` jest mocks in `depotPrimitives.test.ts` ignore the new second `{ signal }` argument, so the existing "rotates hosts / throws after `attempts`" test — which explicitly passes its OWN literal `4` to `fetchChunk`, independent of the production call site — is unaffected). Full suite: 80/80 suites, 1464/1464 tests pass. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression; no change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched); no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate.

**NOT yet verified:** the actual real-world effect on throughput/stall symptoms and on whether the mid-stream hard-fail recurs — this requires a fresh hardware reproduction (a large native Steam install, ideally the same Cyberpunk 1091500 or similarly sized title) since it depends on live Steam CDN behavior that cannot be simulated in unit tests.

## Resolution

root_cause: Two code-level defects in the chunk-fetch retry path of `downloadDepotFiles`/`downloadFileChunks`/`fetchChunk` (depot.ts + depot/decompress.ts), NOT the pre-download setup seam (already eliminated). (1) `fetchChunk`'s CDN `fetch()` call had no timeout/AbortSignal, so a slow/hung content-server connection could block a chunk-worker slot for an unbounded time — the existing "rotate hosts on failure" retry only triggers on a thrown error, never on a hang. (2) The hard-coded `attempts=4` gave too little retry headroom for a large game's thousands of chunks given the CDN's documented ~16% per-chunk transient-failure rate at concurrency 8; a single chunk exhausting its retries gets escalated by `downloadSteamDepots` into a whole-install `status:'error'` with the misleading "Steam servers dropped the connection" message — a CDN chunk-retry exhaustion, not an actual Steam CM/session drop (chunk fetches never touch the SteamUser client object).
fix: Added a 15s per-attempt `AbortController` timeout to `fetchChunk`'s CDN fetch (decompress.ts, `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`); raised the hard-coded fetchChunk `attempts` from 4 to a named `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS = 8` constant at the depot.ts call site; added `[Timing]` instrumentation to the previously-unmeasured `getContentServerHosts` CM round-trip to close the last gap in the setup-to-stream timeline for the next reproduction. Did not change the failures-escalation logic or FILE_CONCURRENCY/CHUNK_CONCURRENCY.
verification: Self-verified only (tsc clean; 4 steam suites + depotPrimitives 311/311; full suite 80/80 suites, 1464/1464 tests; no GOG/Epic/Amazon or abort-semantics regression). Real-hardware re-reproduction still required to confirm the throughput/stall symptom and mid-stream hard-fail are actually resolved — awaiting human verification.

  - src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts
  - src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts

**SUPERSEDED (2026-07-18, cycle 2):** the hardware re-reproduction below shows this fix EXTENDED survival (6min -> 17.8min) but did NOT resolve the underlying symptom — the same failure signature recurred. See the cycle-2 Evidence/Fix Applied entries below. This Resolution block is left as the historical cycle-1 record; the session is NOT resolved.

## CHECKPOINT (human-verify) — 2026-07-18

**Cycle-2 hardware re-reproduction result (2026-07-18):** PARTIAL MITIGATION, not resolution. Fresh Cyberpunk 1091500 native install on macOS with the cycle-1 fix applied ran ~17.8min then failed: `runNativeDepotDownload: downloadSteamDepots (buildDepotPlan + stream) took 1067067ms (status=error)` → `depot install failed for appId 1091500: Steam servers dropped the connection. Retry to continue.` App did not crash. Progress percent was user-observed as MONOTONIC (on-disk `du` fluctuation 9→4.2→6.1→7.0GB was benign decompress/staging churn, not a re-download loop). UI updates were slow (~30s apart); download speed slowly decreased over the run before failing; long lag before first visible progress despite buildDepotPlan being only ~3.5s.

### Evidence (cycle 2, 2026-07-18)

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: re-traced the full chunk-stream call chain a second time end-to-end (`downloadSteamDepots` depot.ts:1546 → `getContentServerHosts` depot.ts:1504 [ONE CM call] → `downloadDepotFiles` depot.ts:1040 → `downloadSingleFile` depot.ts:847 → `downloadFileChunks` depot.ts:785 → `fetchChunk` decompress.ts:142). found: zero references to the SteamUser client object anywhere in this chain after the single `getContentServerHosts` call — the depot decryption `key` is captured ONCE in `buildDepotPlan` (plan-time) and passed through as a plain `Buffer`; `hosts` is captured ONCE before `downloadDepotFiles` starts. implication: RECONCILES the checkpoint's apparent contradiction — the exact string "Steam servers dropped the connection. Retry to continue." structurally CANNOT originate from a real SteamUser CM/session drop during the chunk-stream phase; it is always reached via `classifyDepotError`'s `connectionDropped` branch (depotErrors.ts:129-144), which pattern-matches CDN/network failure text INCLUDING fetchChunk's own `failed after N attempts` exhausted-retries message. Cycle 1's root-cause claim ("chunk fetches never touch the SteamUser CM/session") is CONFIRMED again, not refuted — the recurrence at ~18min (vs ~6min pre-fix) means the raised attempts/timeout budget bought more time before SOME chunk still exhausted all `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS=8` attempts.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `getContentServerHosts` (depot.ts:1504) call sites — confirmed it is called exactly ONCE per `downloadSteamDepots` invocation (depot.ts:1653, pre-fix), before `downloadDepotFiles` starts, and never again for the rest of the (potentially 18min+) stream. found: no mechanism existed to refresh, prune, or deprioritize individual content-server hosts as they degrade over a long-running stream. implication: candidate mechanism for progressive throughput decay — a static host list can only get worse (individual edges rate-limiting/degrading under sustained load) over an 18min window, never better, and `fetchChunk`'s round-robin (`hosts[(attemptSeed + i) % hosts.length]`) keeps cycling degraded hosts back into rotation forever.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `downloadFileChunks` (depot.ts:785) host-selection — `attemptSeed: hosts.length ? fileSeed % hosts.length : 0` is set ONCE per FILE (depot.ts, in the chunk-loop body), so every chunk within the same file shares the same first-attempt host. found: this concentrates retry/degradation exposure per-file rather than spreading it randomly per-chunk. implication: documented as a real design finding (Current Focus lead 2) but NOT changed this cycle — altering `fetchChunk`'s selection order risks the existing `depotPrimitives.test.ts` "rotates hosts... 4 attempts, 4 distinct hosts" test contract, which asserts deterministic round-robin over a STATIC host array; left alone pending real evidence from the next hardware run that it materially matters.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: the chunk-fetch retry/rotation/timeout path had ZERO log output anywhere — confirmed via grep across depot.ts + depot/decompress.ts, no `logInfo`/`logWarning` call existed inside `fetchChunk`'s attempt loop or `downloadFileChunks`. found: cycle 1's 15s timeout + 8-attempt fix produced no log evidence of firing during the ~18min cycle-2 reproduction, because nothing logged attempts/timeouts/rotations at all. implication: added comprehensive instrumentation this cycle (see Fix Applied) before requesting the next hardware reproduction, so a single next run can distinguish "static host list degrading" vs "genuinely low bandwidth" vs "DecompressPool backlog" per the falsification test in `reasoning_checkpoint_2`.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: added `onAttempt` observability hook to `fetchChunk` (decompress.ts) — reports host/attempt-index/outcome(success|timeout|error)/ms for EVERY attempt, purely additive (optional trailing param, default undefined, zero behavior change). Threaded through `downloadFileChunks` → `downloadSingleFile` → `downloadDepotFiles`, which aggregates PER-HOST stats (attempts/successes/timeouts/errors/avg-ms) in a `Map` and logs a `[Timing] chunk-stream stats @Ns: ...` summary (percent, downSpeed/diskSpeed, total attempts/rotations/timeouts, worst 6 hosts by timeout+error count) every 15 heartbeat ticks (~15s) by extending the EXISTING `PROGRESS_HEARTBEAT_MS` `setInterval` callback rather than starting a second interval — preserves the `depot.test.ts` "registers a ... setInterval ... exactly once" test contract. Also logs a final stats snapshot unconditionally after the download settles (even if it ends on a non-multiple-of-15 tick). Also logs `[Timing] downloadDepotFiles: first bytes written after Nms` on the very first `disk>0` byte, addressing the "long lag before it initiated" observation independently of the already-measured ~3.5s buildDepotPlan.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: `getContentServerHosts` now also logs `hosts=N` (host count) alongside its existing round-trip timing.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: added `startContentServerRefresh` in `downloadSteamDepots` — a `setInterval` (new `CONTENT_SERVER_REFRESH_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000` constant) that periodically re-calls `getContentServerHosts` and mutates the SAME `hosts` array IN PLACE (`splice`, never reassigns the reference) so `downloadDepotFiles`/`downloadFileChunks`/`fetchChunk`'s existing round-robin selection transparently sees a refreshed pool with NO change to the selection algorithm itself. Guards `signal?.aborted` before each refresh tick; a failed refresh is logged via `logWarning` and the existing host list is kept (never throws into the download). Always stopped via a `finally` wrapping the `downloadDepotFiles` call in `downloadSteamDepots`, on every exit path (success/failure/abort) — never left running past the download's lifetime. This is a DRAFT fix for lead #2 (progressive throughput decay from a never-refreshed, potentially-degrading host list) — concrete and safe, but NOT yet confirmed against real CDN behavior.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 4 steam suites + `depotPrimitives.test.ts`: 5 suites, 311/311 pass (unchanged pass count from cycle 1 — the new `onAttempt`/host-refresh code paths are exercised in production but not directly asserted on by existing tests, since `fetchChunk` and `../depot/decompress` are fully `jest.mock`'d in `depot.test.ts`, and `depotPrimitives.test.ts`'s two `fetchChunk` calls simply don't pass the new optional trailing arg). Full suite: 80/80 suites, 1464/1464 tests pass (identical counts to cycle 1 — no regression). found: a pre-existing (NOT introduced by this cycle) leaked-timer warning from `games.test.ts`'s `startInstallPolling`/`pollInstallOnce` fires during worker teardown when running suites together — confirmed pre-existing by re-running the identical 5-suite command with this cycle's changes `git stash`'d out; the warning appears identically either way. implication: safe to ship for the next hardware-reproduction checkpoint; no test contract broken, no GOG/Epic/Amazon regression, no change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched), no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate, no change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard.

### Fix Applied (cycle 2, 2026-07-18, pending human hardware re-verification)

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: added `ChunkAttemptOutcome`/`ChunkAttemptEvent`/`OnChunkAttempt` types and an optional trailing `onAttempt` param to `fetchChunk`, reporting host/attempt/outcome/ms for every attempt (success, timeout [AbortError from `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`], or other error). Purely additive observability — no change to attempt count, backoff schedule, or host-rotation order.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: threaded `onAttempt` through `downloadFileChunks` and `downloadSingleFile`. `downloadDepotFiles` now aggregates per-host attempt/success/timeout/error/avg-ms stats and logs a periodic `[Timing] chunk-stream stats @Ns: ...` summary (piggybacked on the existing heartbeat interval, not a new one) plus a `[Timing] ... first bytes written after Nms` marker on first byte. `getContentServerHosts` now also logs the resolved host count. Added `CONTENT_SERVER_REFRESH_MS` + `startContentServerRefresh` in `downloadSteamDepots`: periodically refreshes the content-server host pool via in-place array mutation, so a degrading/rate-limited host is not stuck in rotation for the entire multi-minute stream — extends (never replaces) the existing round-robin/backoff/rotation mechanism in `fetchChunk`.
- Deliberately NOT changed this cycle: `fetchChunk`'s host-selection algorithm itself (per-file deterministic `attemptSeed`, documented as a real concentration-of-exposure finding but risky to change without breaking `depotPrimitives.test.ts`'s rotation-order assertions and without real evidence it's the dominant factor); `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` → whole-install `status:'error'` escalation logic (same reasoning as cycle 1); `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY` (unchanged, same reasoning as cycle 1).

**Self-verification (cycle 2, 2026-07-18):** see Evidence entry above — tsc clean, 311/311 (5 suites), 1464/1464 (80 suites, full jest), no regressions, pre-existing unrelated leaked-timer warning confirmed not introduced by this cycle.

**NOT yet verified:** whether the periodic content-server refresh actually prevents the eventual retry-exhaustion failure, and whether the new per-host stats log confirms or refutes "progressive host degradation" as the throughput-decay mechanism — both require one more hardware reproduction with this cycle's instrumentation, which is expected to be the next checkpoint.

## CHECKPOINT (human-verify) — SATISFIED 2026-07-18 (cycle 3 diagnosis)

**Cycle-3 hardware reproduction result (2026-07-18, fresh Cyberpunk 1091500, macOS, Apple Silicon, Auckland/NZ):** install died at status=error after 908s (~15min); throughput decayed 16→0 MiB/s by ~480s then sat at 0 for ~7min before erroring; on-disk reached ~8.7GB/~11%. Cycle 2's per-host stats instrumentation produced the DEFINITIVE diagnosis (see Evidence below) — the user reviewed it directly and DECIDED on a host-scoring/health-aware-selection fix rather than requesting further investigation. This satisfies the checkpoint cycle 2 left open; the session proceeded straight to fix_and_verify (cycle 3) per that decision.

### Evidence (cycle 3, 2026-07-18)

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — DEFINITIVE DIAGNOSIS from the cycle-2-instrumented third hardware reproduction (fresh Cyberpunk 1091500, macOS, Auckland/NZ). Per-host final counts (a=attempts ok=success err=error avgMs; timeouts ALWAYS 0 for the entire run): `cache1-akl-tpwr.steamcontent.com` a=4622 ok=4354 err=268 avgMs=285 (GOOD, but FROZE — ok stopped climbing after ~480s, never recovered); `cache2-akl-tpwr.steamcontent.com` a=3888 ok=3640 err=248 avgMs=298 (GOOD, also froze mid-stream); `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` a=258 ok=0 err=258 avgMs=57 (100% FAILURE, fast-fail, never once succeeded); `cache1-akl-edgx.steamcontent.com` a=293 ok=36 err=257 (~88% failure); `steampipe.akamaized.net` a=788 ok=541 err=247 avgMs=326 (~31% failure); `fastly.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` a=786 ok=548 avgMs=3995, degrading ~2800→~4000ms over the run (SLOW, worsening). rotations=1328, hosts stayed 6 the whole time; the periodic refresh logged "replaced host list (6 -> 6)" — SAME hosts every time, no help (see Eliminated below). found: totalAttempts nearly flatlined in the final 7min (~340 attempts ≈ 0.8/s) despite timeouts=0 — the retry-across-hosts mechanism was firing constantly (rotations=1328) but burning attempts+backoff-sleep on the SAME proven-bad hosts (alibaba, cache1-akl-edgx) at the same rate as the two genuinely good hosts, because plain round-robin gives every host an equal share regardless of track record. implication: this is CONFIRMED (not just hypothesized) as workers starved in retry/backoff, never hung on any single request — the exact mechanism a health-aware host-scoring fix targets directly.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `fetchChunk`'s host selection (`decompress.ts` pre-cycle-3) was `hosts[(attemptSeed + i) % hosts.length]` — a pure round-robin with ZERO memory of any host's prior success/failure history across the whole run. found: confirms structurally (not just statistically) why a 0%-success host received the same ~1/6 share of every file's rotation as a 94%-success host — there was no code path by which a host's track record could influence selection. implication: root cause of the throughput collapse is a MISSING mechanism (health-aware selection), not a bug in the existing retry/rotation logic, which was working exactly as designed — it just had no way to know some of what it was rotating into was hopeless.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `fetchChunk`'s exponential backoff (`sleep(200 * 2 ** i)`, pre-cycle-3) was UNCAPPED. found: at `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS=8`, a single chunk exhausting every attempt could sleep up to ~25.4s cumulative (200+400+800+1600+3200+6400+12800ms) doing zero useful work, entirely independent of which hosts it happened to land on. implication: even with perfect host-scoring, an unlucky chunk (e.g. hitting the slow/degrading `fastly` host on an early attempt) could still consume a large, uncapped amount of a worker slot's time purely in backoff sleep — a secondary, complementary lever to bound the worst case (constraint #3: retry/backoff must not collapse the attempt rate to ~0).
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action (fix implementation, see Fix Applied below): created `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/hostHealth.ts` (`HostHealthTracker`) implementing recent-success-rate + latency composite scoring with a fast consecutive-failure circuit-breaker; threaded an optional `hostHealth` parameter through `fetchChunk` → `downloadFileChunks` → `downloadSingleFile` → `downloadDepotFiles` (one tracker instance per download run, shared across every file/depot in that run); capped the exponential backoff ceiling at `CHUNK_FETCH_MAX_BACKOFF_MS = 3000`; REMOVED the disproven cycle-2 periodic content-server refresh (`CONTENT_SERVER_REFRESH_MS`/`startContentServerRefresh`) since the same hardware run that produced this diagnosis also proved it does nothing (identical 6-host list every refresh); tagged the existing `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` log's worst-hosts line with an `UNHEALTHY` marker sourced from the live tracker, for direct visual confirmation on the next hardware run.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 4 steam suites + `depotPrimitives.test.ts` + the new `hostHealth.test.ts` + `decompressPool.test.ts`: 7 suites, 454/454 pass. Full suite: 81/81 suites, 1477/1477 tests pass (1464 cycle-2 baseline + 13 new: 11 in `hostHealth.test.ts`, 2 added to `depotPrimitives.test.ts`'s `fetchChunk` describe block). Confirmed via `git stash`/`git stash pop` re-run of the identical 6-suite command that the pre-existing leaked-timer warning (games.test.ts's `startInstallPolling`/`pollInstallOnce` firing during worker teardown when suites run together) is byte-for-byte identical with or without this cycle's changes — not introduced by this cycle. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression; no change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched); no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate; no change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard; `depotPrimitives.test.ts`'s original two `fetchChunk` tests (the rotation-order/distinct-hosts assertion and the SHA1-match-success assertion) pass UNMODIFIED — cold-start (no `hostHealth` argument) behavior is byte-for-byte identical to the pre-cycle-3 plain round-robin, verified by a dedicated `hostHealth.test.ts` test asserting this explicitly.

### Eliminated (cycle 3)

- hypothesis: "the cycle-2 periodic content-server refresh (`CONTENT_SERVER_REFRESH_MS`) would relieve progressive host degradation by re-resolving the host pool" — ELIMINATED by direct observation in the cycle-3 hardware run: the refresh fired repeatedly and logged "replaced host list (6 -> 6)" every time — Steam's `getContentServers` returned the IDENTICAL 6 hostnames on every call for the whole run. The CDN assigns a fixed edge set per app/region; re-resolving it changes nothing. REMOVED this cycle (see Fix Applied) rather than left in as harmless-but-useless — replaced by health-aware selection WITHIN the fixed pool.
- hypothesis: "the mid-stream failure is a hung/unbounded request (a true 'stuck' worker)" — ELIMINATED again, more strongly, by this cycle's data: timeouts=0 for the ENTIRE ~908s run despite ~7min of near-zero attempt throughput. The 15s `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (cycle 1) is confirmed working as designed (no request is silently hanging past it) — the throughput collapse is a SELECTION/backoff-starvation problem, not a hang.

### Fix Applied (cycle 3, 2026-07-18, pending fourth hardware re-verification)

- **NEW FILE** `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/hostHealth.ts`: `HostHealthTracker` — per-download-run (never a module-level singleton) host-health tracker. `record(host, outcome, ms)` updates per-host attempt/success/consecutive-failure/latency stats. `pickHost(hosts, seed, attemptIndex)` partitions hosts into healthy/unhealthy buckets (unhealthy = `consecutiveFailures >= MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES(5)` OR `attempts >= MIN_SAMPLES_FOR_UNHEALTHY(5)` AND `successRate < MIN_SUCCESS_RATE_FOR_HEALTHY(0.35)`), sorts each bucket by a composite score (success rate dominant, latency as a small tiebreak via `LATENCY_SCORE_DIVISOR_MS=50000`), and returns `[...healthy, ...unhealthy][attemptIndex % length]` — unhealthy hosts are NEVER excluded, only deprioritized to the back, so a transient CDN-wide outage still has a fallback candidate. On a cold start (no history for any host) this ordering is proven byte-for-byte identical to the original `hosts[(seed+i) % hosts.length]` round-robin (Array.prototype.sort's ES2019+ stability guarantee + a dedicated test) — deprioritization only emerges as real attempt history accumulates against specific hosts.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: `fetchChunk` gained an optional trailing `hostHealth?: HostHealthTracker` parameter. When supplied, each attempt's host comes from `hostHealth.pickHost(...)` instead of the plain round-robin formula, and every attempt's outcome is reported to `hostHealth.record(...)` (alongside the existing, unchanged `onAttempt` logging hook). Omitting the parameter (every pre-cycle-3 call site/test) leaves behavior completely unchanged. Also added `CHUNK_FETCH_MAX_BACKOFF_MS = 3000`, capping the exponential backoff sleep (`Math.min(200 * 2**i, 3000)`) — bounds a single chunk's worst-case cumulative backoff-sleep from ~25.4s to ~12s across 8 attempts, addressing constraint #3 (retry/backoff must not collapse the attempt rate to ~0) as a complement to host-scoring, not a replacement for it.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: threaded `hostHealth` through `downloadFileChunks` and `downloadSingleFile` (both gained an optional trailing `hostHealth?: HostHealthTracker` parameter, forwarded to `fetchChunk`). `downloadDepotFiles` creates ONE `HostHealthTracker` per download run (shared across every file/depot in that run — host health is a property of the CDN edge, not of any single file) and passes it through. The existing `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` log's worst-hosts line now appends ` UNHEALTHY` for any host the live tracker currently deprioritizes, for direct visual confirmation on the next hardware run. REMOVED (disproven, see Eliminated) the cycle-2 `CONTENT_SERVER_REFRESH_MS` constant and `startContentServerRefresh` function, and its call site in `downloadSteamDepots` — replaced with a comment explaining why and pointing at `hostHealth.ts`.
- Deliberately NOT changed this cycle: the per-file deterministic `attemptSeed = fileSeed % hosts.length` derivation itself (still used as the tie-breaking rotation seed feeding into `pickHost`'s cold-start ordering — changing it further is unnecessary now that scoring handles the actual concentration-of-exposure problem); `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` → whole-install `status:'error'` escalation logic (same reasoning as cycles 1-2); `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY` (unchanged, same reasoning as cycles 1-2) — the directive's own REALISM constraint acknowledges this fix cannot manufacture bandwidth the region's CDN doesn't provide, only stop wasting effort on dead hosts and stabilize use of the good ones.

**Self-verification (cycle 3, 2026-07-18):** see Evidence entry above — tsc clean; 7 targeted suites (4 steam + depotPrimitives + hostHealth + decompressPool) 454/454; full suite 81/81 suites, 1477/1477 tests; pre-existing leaked-timer warning confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged via git-stash comparison; existing `depotPrimitives.test.ts` rotation-order/distinct-hosts test passes UNMODIFIED (cold-start behavior proven identical); no GOG/Epic/Amazon or abort-semantics regression; no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate or the Phase-23 single-flight guard.

**NOT yet verified:** the actual real-world effect on the mid-stream retry-exhaustion failure and on whether the two good Auckland hosts keep receiving (and succeeding on) attempts through what was previously the ~7min stall window — this requires a fourth hardware reproduction, expected to be the next and hopefully final checkpoint for this session. Per the objective, this session is NOT marked resolved regardless of self-verification outcome — a hardware reproduction is mandatory before any resolution.

## Diagnostic cycle 4 (2026-07-18) — NEW LEAD (a): server-ranking/cellID, additive logging only

User-approved diagnostic to run BEFORE the next full host-scoring hardware verification: is the real Steam client getting a better server set/ranking than we are, because (1) we never set a `cell_id` on logOn, and (2) `getContentServerHosts` discards the directory's own load/weight/preferred-server ranking and keeps only hostnames? This cycle is diagnostic logging ONLY — no selection/scoring/retry behavior changed, no new fix logic. The cycle-3 host-scoring fix (hostHealth.ts + decompress.ts + depot.ts, still uncommitted) is untouched.

### Evidence (cycle 4, 2026-07-18)

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked (graphify + `node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn.js` `getContentServers`, and `components/09-logon.js`): confirmed `getContentServers(appid, cb)` calls `GetServersForSteamPipe` with `{cell_id: this.cellID || 0}` — if `client.cellID` is unset/0 at call time, the directory request uses cell 0 (a generic/default region), not our actual geographic cell. `client.cellID` is set by steam-user itself during `logOn`'s CM-list/logon handshake (`this.cellID = body.cell_id` in `09-logon.js:738`) and persisted to a local `cellid-<machineID>.txt` file for reuse on future cold connects — we never pass an explicit `cell_id` in `logOn({ refreshToken })` (confirmed: no `cellID`/`cell_id` reference anywhere in `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/user.ts`), so we rely entirely on whatever steam-user resolves/persists on its own; unconfirmed whether that resolution is fast/accurate for our region without a real logon.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked (`cdn.js` `getContentServers`'s own `servers.map()`): confirmed the FULL field set the directory actually returns per server: `type`, `sourceid` (source_id), `cell` (cell_id), `load`, `weightedload` (weighted_load), `preferred_server`, `NumEntriesInClientList`, `Host`, `vhost`, `https_support`, optional `allowed_app_ids`. Our `SteamUserDepotExtras.getContentServers` type declaration (depot.ts) only declared `Host?`/`vhost?` — the rest were silently dropped by our own type, not by steam-user. implication: `load`/`weightedload`/`preferred_server` are exactly the directory's own ranking signals the real Steam client can use to prioritize among an identical host set — we've never even looked at them.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `getContentServers(appid, callback)` — confirmed no count/limit/max-servers parameter exists anywhere in steam-user's `cdn.js`; the only input is the optional `appid` filter (used to exclude servers whose `allowed_app_ids` doesn't include ours). We already pass `numericAppId`, so we're already using the one narrowing lever steam-user exposes — there is no "request a bigger pool" option to note as unused.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: widened the `SteamUserDepotExtras.getContentServers` type declaration (depot.ts) to include all fields `cdn.js` actually returns (`type`, `sourceid`, `cell`, `load`, `weightedload`, `preferred_server`, `NumEntriesInClientList`, `https_support`, `allowed_app_ids`) — type-only change, no runtime behavior.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: in `getContentServerHosts` (depot.ts), added a one-time `logInfo('[Timing] getContentServerHosts RAW for appId ...: ' + JSON.stringify(servers))` immediately after the `getContentServers` call resolves and BEFORE the existing `.map((s) => s.Host ?? s.vhost)` reduction — captures every field for every returned server, once per download run (same call site/frequency as the existing timing log right below it, unchanged).
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: in `connectSteamUserClient`'s `loggedOn` handler (user.ts), added a one-time `logInfo('[Timing] SteamUser.loggedOn: cellID=... publicIP=...')` reading `client.cellID`/`client.publicIP` (both typed in `@types/steam-user/index.d.ts:46,51`, confirmed valid post-logon fields) — fires once per cold logOn (the "already connected fast path" in `ensureConnected()` does not re-fire `loggedOn`, so this won't spam on every install, only on the first cold connect of a session, matching the existing cold-connect-only `[Timing]` instrumentation in `ensureConnected`).
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 7 targeted suites (4 steam + depotPrimitives + hostHealth + decompressPool): 454/454 pass — identical count to cycle 3's baseline, confirming the type widening + two added log lines are behavior-neutral. Full suite: 81/81 suites, 1477/1477 tests pass — identical to cycle 3's full-suite baseline. Pre-existing leaked-timer warning (unrelated `library.ts`/`pollInstallOnce` teardown issue) still present, unchanged. No change to selection/scoring/retry logic, host-scoring fix, abort semantics, StateFlags gate, or single-flight guard.

### Fix Applied (cycle 4 — diagnostic only, no fix logic)

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: widened `SteamUserDepotExtras.getContentServers`'s declared return type to include the full field set steam-user's `cdn.js` actually returns; added a one-time RAW JSON dump log in `getContentServerHosts` before the existing hostname-only reduction.

### How to read the next dev-log capture

A ~30s Install-then-cancel run is enough (no full download needed) — the two new log lines both fire once, near the very start of the pre-download seam:
1. `[Timing] SteamUser.loggedOn: cellID=<N> publicIP=<x.x.x.x>` — fires ONCE per app session, only on a cold logOn (skip this line if the session was already warm from a prior install in the same run; if so, it will have fired earlier in the same dev-log — search backward). `cellID=0` (or `undefined`) would mean we're querying the directory with the generic/default cell, a concrete, actionable finding.
2. `[Timing] getContentServerHosts RAW for appId <id>: [{...}, {...}, ...]` — fires once per install attempt, right after the click. Compare each server's `load`/`weightedload`/`preferred_server`/`cell` against which hosts cycle 3's host-scoring later found to be good (Auckland `cache1-akl-tpwr`/`cache2-akl-tpwr`) vs bad (`alibaba`, `cache1-akl-edgx`) — if the directory's own `load`/`weightedload` already ranked alibaba/cache1-akl-edgx worse than the Auckland caches at request time, that's direct evidence the directory pre-ranks servers and we could honor that ranking directly instead of (or alongside) learning it empirically via `hostHealth.ts`.

**NOT yet verified against real data** — this cycle is additive logging only; the ~30s capture described above is the next checkpoint. No hardware run has happened with this instrumentation yet.

## Cycle 5 (2026-07-18) — RESULT + FIX: weightedload-aware host selection

**Cycle-4's ~30s directory capture came back decisive** (cell-22 directory, Auckland, appId 1091500) and the user gave the go-ahead to implement a real fix this cycle, layered on cycle 3's `hostHealth.ts`, keeping the RAW dump + cellID diagnostic logging.

### Evidence (cycle 5, 2026-07-18)

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — CAPTURED DIRECTORY EVIDENCE (satisfies cycle 4's checkpoint): `cellID=22, publicIP=203.211.111.62` — the cell ID is CORRECT for Auckland. The "wrong cell ID" hypothesis from diagnostic cycle 4 is ELIMINATED (see Eliminated below). The directory returned 6 servers WITH a ranking we were discarding, sorted by `weightedload` (lower=better): `cache1-akl-edgx.steamcontent.com` type=SteamCache cell=22 load=16 weightedload=20; `cache1-akl-tpwr.steamcontent.com` cell=22 load=37 weightedload=37; `cache2-akl-tpwr.steamcontent.com` cell=22 load=39 weightedload=48; `steampipe.akamaized.net` type=CDN cell=- load=0 weightedload=130; `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` type=CDN weightedload=130; `fastly.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` type=CDN weightedload=130. implication: CONFIRMS the root cause — `getContentServerHosts` did `servers.map(s => s.Host)` and round-robinned EVENLY, so the 3 global CDN fallbacks (weightedload=130, including alibaba which went 0/258 in cycle 3's hardware run) got the SAME traffic share as the 3 local Auckland SteamCache edges (weightedload 20-48) — Steam's own client weights by weightedload and pushes nearly all traffic to the local caches, treating the 130s as last-resort. We were flattening Steam's own ranking.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: cross-referenced the cycle-3 hardware diagnosis's per-host empirical results against this cycle's weightedload ranking — `cache1-akl-edgx` had the BEST weightedload (20) yet failed ~88% empirically in the cycle-3 run, while `cache1-akl-tpwr`/`cache2-akl-tpwr` (weightedload 37/48, worse prior) were the reliable ~94%/~93% workhorses. implication: weightedload is a good PRIOR (correctly ranks the 3 local caches far above the 3×130 CDN fallbacks) but is NOT a perfect reliability predictor at the fine-grained level — the fix must let empirical evidence override a good prior once a host proves itself unreliable, never let the prior alone determine long-run selection.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action (fix implementation, see Fix Applied below): extended `depot/hostHealth.ts`'s `HostHealthTracker` with an optional `weightedLoads: ReadonlyMap<string, number>` constructor argument that seeds each host's cold-start PRIOR score (`priorScoreFromWeightedLoad`, calibrated so the 3 Auckland locals — weightedload 20/37/48 — score ~0.51–0.71 vs the 3×130 CDN fallbacks' ~0.28). The composite score blends this prior with the pre-existing empirical (success-rate + latency) score, with the prior's weight fading from 100% at 0 attempts to 50% at `PRIOR_HALFLIFE_SAMPLES` (=`MIN_SAMPLES_FOR_UNHEALTHY`=5) attempts and increasingly small beyond — while the pre-existing, entirely prior-INDEPENDENT `isUnhealthy` circuit breaker (consecutive-failure streak / low success rate) still demotes a good-prior-but-flaky host (edgx) to the back of the queue exactly as before, regardless of its prior. A host with NO known weightedload (map entry absent) falls through to the exact pre-cycle-5 neutral behavior (prior=1 at cold start, pure empirical once attempts>0) — never penalized, never given a synthetic ranking the directory didn't actually supply.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: extracted `depot.ts`'s directory-response reduction (previously an inline `servers.map((s) => s.Host ?? s.vhost)` that discarded everything else) into a standalone, exported, directly-testable pure function `reduceContentServers(servers): { hosts: string[]; weightedLoads: Map<string, number> }` — threads `weightedload` alongside each hostname via a parallel `Map`, omitting a host from the map entirely (not defaulting to a synthetic worst-case) when the directory doesn't report a finite `weightedload` for it. `getContentServerHosts` now returns `{ hosts, weightedLoads }` (was `string[]`); `downloadSteamDepots` threads `weightedLoads` through `DownloadDepotFilesOpts.hostWeightedLoads` into `downloadDepotFiles`, which seeds its per-run `HostHealthTracker(opts.hostWeightedLoads)`. The RAW directory dump log and the `cellID`/`publicIP` log from diagnostic cycle 4 are UNCHANGED and kept.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: extended the existing `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` per-host worst-hosts log line with a `wl=<weightedload>` field (when known) alongside the existing `a=/ok=/to=/err=/avgMs=` counters and `UNHEALTHY` tag — so the next hardware run's log directly shows, side by side, each host's directory prior AND its live empirical outcome (confirms/denies that the 130-weightedload CDN fallbacks are now receiving little/no traffic while the low-weightedload locals absorb the bulk of attempts, per the fix directive).
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 8 targeted suites (hostHealth, depotPrimitives, depot, installLocation, user, games, library, decompressPool): 8 suites, 478/478 pass (467 baseline + 11 new: 5 `reduceContentServers` tests in depot.test.ts + 6 weightedload-aware tests in hostHealth.test.ts). Full suite: 81/81 suites, 1488/1488 tests pass (1477 cycle-3/4 baseline + 11 new). The pre-existing, unrelated `readAcfState`/`pollInstallOnce` leaked-timer teardown warning still fires identically (not introduced by this cycle — same as every prior cycle's confirmation). No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression; no change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched); no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate; no change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the existing `depotPrimitives.test.ts` cold-start rotation-order test and `hostHealth.test.ts`'s original cold-start/circuit-breaker tests pass UNMODIFIED (both call `new HostHealthTracker()`/`fetchChunk(...)` with no weightedload data, so the new prior-blend logic is provably inert for every pre-cycle-5 caller — confirmed by dedicated new regression test `omitting weightedLoads entirely ... reproduces the exact cycle-3/4 neutral cold-start order`).

### Eliminated (cycle 5)

- hypothesis: "we're querying the content-server directory with the wrong/default cell ID (cellID=0), giving us a worse server set than the real Steam client" — ELIMINATED by direct observation: the cycle-4 `[Timing] SteamUser.loggedOn` capture showed `cellID=22, publicIP=203.211.111.62` — a correct, real Auckland-region cell, not 0/undefined. The directory response itself was never the problem; discarding its ranking was.

### Fix Applied (cycle 5, 2026-07-18, pending fifth hardware re-verification)

reasoning_checkpoint_5:
  hypothesis: "The content-server directory already ranks its returned hosts via `weightedload` (lower=better) in a way that closely tracks real-world reliability at the local-vs-global-fallback granularity (3 Auckland locals at 20-48 vs 3 global CDN fallbacks at 130 each) — discarding that ranking and treating all 6 hosts as equally-likely round-robin candidates is precisely why cycle 3's plain-round-robin (and cycle-3's neutral-cold-start health scoring) let a 0%-success CDN fallback (alibaba) and a ~12%-success local edge (cache1-akl-edgx) receive the same initial attempt share as the two genuinely reliable Auckland caches. Seeding HostHealthTracker's cold-start PRIOR from weightedload, blended with (never replacing) the existing empirical scoring, directly fixes this without discarding cycle 3's demonstrated ability to demote a good-prior-but-flaky host."
  confirming_evidence:
    - "Cycle-4's captured directory response (cell 22, Auckland) shows weightedload 20/37/48 for the three hosts cycle-3's OWN hardware run separately proved were the two reliable ones (tpwr1/tpwr2, weightedload 37/48) plus one unreliable one (edgx, weightedload 20) — vs weightedload=130 for all three hosts cycle-3 separately proved were unreliable-to-dead (steampipe.akamaized.net ~69%, fastly ~70%-but-degrading, alibaba 0%)."
    - "Re-read `HostHealthTracker.isUnhealthy` (hostHealth.ts, unchanged this cycle): purely a function of `consecutiveFailures`/`successes`/`attempts` — no reference to any prior/weightedload input — confirming a good-prior host (edgx) is demoted to the unhealthy bucket by the SAME mechanism cycle 3 already proved works, regardless of this cycle's changes."
    - "New regression test `omitting weightedLoads entirely ... reproduces the exact cycle-3/4 neutral cold-start order` passes byte-for-byte against the pre-cycle-5 assertions — confirms this cycle's change is provably inert for every caller/test that doesn't supply weightedload data."
  falsification_test: "On the next hardware reproduction with the `wl=` field now in the `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` log: if the 130-weightedload CDN fallbacks (alibaba, fastly, steampipe.akamaized.net) STILL receive a comparable a= (attempts) share to the two Auckland tpwr caches through the whole run, the weightedload-seeding is not actually biasing selection as intended and this cycle's fix has failed even if the diagnosis was correct. If the mid-stream 'Steam servers dropped the connection' failure still recurs despite the 130s visibly receiving little traffic and the good locals visibly absorbing the bulk of attempts, the diagnosis was incomplete (a cause besides host-selection is also at play — e.g. genuine last-mile bandwidth ceiling, independent of which hosts are selected, per the fix directive's own REALISM constraint) and this cycle's fix should be treated as a further partial mitigation, same pattern as cycles 1-2."
  fix_rationale: "The weightedload PRIOR is threaded as an ADDITIVE seed to HostHealthTracker's existing composite score, never a replacement for the empirical/circuit-breaker mechanism cycle 3 already validated against real per-host data — this directly satisfies the fix directive's central nuance (weightedload is a good prior but not a perfect predictor; combine, don't let either dominate). The prior's influence explicitly fades with sample count (PRIOR_HALFLIFE_SAMPLES) so it cannot permanently entrench a bad host, and the unhealthy circuit breaker remains completely prior-independent so a flaky good-prior host (edgx) is demoted on the same real-failure evidence as before. Extracting `reduceContentServers` as a pure function (rather than inlining the weightedload-threading directly in the CM-calling `getContentServerHosts`) keeps the actual selection-relevant logic unit-testable without a live client, matching this module's existing testability discipline (buildDepotPlan/downloadDepotFiles are similarly decomposed).
  blind_spots: "No fresh hardware reproduction has been run with this cycle's fix yet — the throughput/reliability-improvement claim is inferred from (a) the cycle-4 captured directory ranking (a real fact, not a guess) correctly separating the 3 known-good hosts from the 3 known-bad hosts at the weightedload granularity, and (b) unit tests proving the blending/demotion mechanics behave as designed in isolation — not from a captured clean run with THIS fix active. WEIGHTEDLOAD_PRIOR_DIVISOR=50 and PRIOR_HALFLIFE_SAMPLES=5 are reasoned calibration choices consistent with the exact weightedload/reliability numbers observed across cycles 3-4, not independently tuned against a live run. It remains possible (per the directive's own REALISM constraint, and per cycle-3's blind spot which is still open) that a residual throughput ceiling is a genuine last-mile-bandwidth or CDN-side rate-limit effect independent of which hosts are selected, in which case this fix — even if it works exactly as designed — would improve steadiness/reliability without necessarily eliminating a bandwidth ceiling."

- **NEW behavior** `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/hostHealth.ts`: `HostHealthTracker` constructor now accepts an optional `weightedLoads?: ReadonlyMap<string, number>`. New exports `WEIGHTEDLOAD_PRIOR_DIVISOR`, `PRIOR_HALFLIFE_SAMPLES`, `priorScoreFromWeightedLoad`. `score()` now blends a host's directory-weightedload PRIOR with its empirical success/latency score, fading the prior's weight as real attempts accumulate; `isUnhealthy()` is UNCHANGED (still purely empirical, still demotes a good-prior host that flakes).
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: new exported pure function `reduceContentServers` (+ exported `RawContentServer` type) replaces the inline `servers.map((s) => s.Host ?? s.vhost)` reduction, now also building a hostname→weightedload `Map`. `getContentServerHosts` returns `{ hosts, weightedLoads }` instead of `string[]`. `DownloadDepotFilesOpts` gained `hostWeightedLoads?: ReadonlyMap<string, number>`, threaded from `downloadSteamDepots` into `downloadDepotFiles`'s `new HostHealthTracker(opts.hostWeightedLoads)`. The `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` worst-hosts log line gained a `wl=<weightedload>` field per host (when known). RAW directory dump log (diagnostic cycle 4) is UNCHANGED and kept.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/user.ts`: UNCHANGED this cycle (cycle 4's `cellID`/`publicIP` log kept, already confirmed correct — see Eliminated above).
- New tests: `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/hostHealth.test.ts` gained a `weightedload-aware selection (cycle 5)` describe block (cold-start-prefers-local-cache, edgx-flakes-despite-good-prior, prior-fades-with-samples, `priorScoreFromWeightedLoad` monotonicity, and a no-weightedLoads regression test). `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/depot.test.ts` gained a `reduceContentServers` describe block (5 tests: Host/weightedload mapping, vhost fallback, missing-host skip, missing/NaN-weightedload omission, order preservation).
- Deliberately NOT changed: `fetchChunk`'s attempt-count/backoff/timeout mechanics (cycles 1/3, unchanged); the per-file deterministic `attemptSeed` derivation (unchanged, still feeds `pickHost`'s seed); `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` → whole-install `status:'error'` escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; the cellID passed to `logOn` (still self-resolved by steam-user, confirmed correct this cycle).

**Self-verification (cycle 5, 2026-07-18):** see Evidence entry above — tsc clean; 8 targeted suites 478/478; full suite 81/81 suites, 1488/1488 tests; no regressions; pre-existing unrelated leaked-timer warning confirmed not introduced by this cycle; every pre-cycle-5 test (hostHealth's original 10 tests + depotPrimitives's `fetchChunk` tests) passes UNMODIFIED, proving the weightedload-seeding is inert when no weightedload data is supplied.

**NOT yet verified:** the actual real-world effect on host selection and throughput/reliability — this requires a fifth hardware reproduction (same Cyberpunk 1091500 or similarly sized title, same Auckland/NZ network) with this cycle's `wl=` field in the `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` log, to directly confirm the 130-weightedload CDN fallbacks receive little/no traffic while the local caches absorb the bulk of attempts, and that throughput holds steadier / the mid-stream retry-exhaustion failure no longer recurs (or recurs later/less severely, in which case treat as a further partial mitigation per the falsification test above). Per the objective, this session is NOT marked resolved — a hardware reproduction remains the mandatory next checkpoint.

## Cycle 6 (2026-07-18) — RESULT + FIX: CDN auth token support (highest-leverage fix)

**New root cause + user-approved fix directive (2026-07-18):** correlating the cycle-5 hardware run's per-host outcomes against each host's `type` field (not just its `weightedload`) revealed the ACTUAL mechanism behind the cycle-3/5 failures: every `type=CDN` host (steampipe.akamaized.net, alibaba, fastly — all `weightedload=130`) failed ~100% of attempts (alibaba 0/258, akamai ~1/207, fastly ~5/264), while every `type=SteamCache` host (the local Auckland edges) worked. This is not a weightedload/prioritization problem (cycle 5 already fixed that) — it is that `fetchChunk` (decompress.ts) built chunk URLs with **no CDN auth token**, and nothing in this codebase ever called steam-user's `getCDNAuthToken`. Global SteamPipe CDN edges (`type=CDN`) require a per-depot token; local SteamCache edges apparently do not enforce it, which is why they "worked" despite being few and overloaded. The real Steam client uses all six servers via `GetCDNAuthToken`; this codebase was locked out of the CDN half of the pool entirely, explaining why cycle 3/5's health-aware/weightedload-aware selection could only ever converge onto the 2-3 SteamCache hosts and why those then rate-limited/exhausted under concentrated load.

### Evidence (cycle 6, 2026-07-18)

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — VERIFIED API against the INSTALLED `steam-user@5.3.0` source (`node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn.js:144-165`, NOT assumed — same Pitfall-5 discipline already used for `getDepotDecryptionKey`/`getRawManifest`/`getContentServers`): `getCDNAuthToken(appID, depotID, hostname, callback)` → `callback(err, { token: string, expires: Date })`. Internally sends `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken` with `{ app_id, depot_id, host_name }` and resolves `{ token: body.token, expires: new Date(body.expiration_time * 1000) }`. steam-user ALSO keeps its own internal cache keyed by `depotID + '_' + hostname`, reused while >1hr remains before expiry — CONFIRMS per-depot+host granularity (the request itself is keyed by all three of `app_id`/`depot_id`/`host_name`), not per-app or per-depot-only.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `CMsgClientGetCDNAuthTokenResponse`'s protobuf schema (`node_modules/steam-user/protobufs/steammessages_clientserver_2.proto:201-205`) declares `token` as a plain `optional string` — the protobuf schema itself carries no format guarantee. found: the TOKEN FORMAT (whether it needs a `?`/`&` prefix) is determined by how steam-user's OWN consumers of this same API use the result, not by the schema. implication: read `cdn.js`'s own `getRawManifest`/`downloadChunk` (the only two in-tree call sites) to resolve this rather than assuming.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `cdn.js`'s `getRawManifest` (line ~251: `` `${urlBase}/depot/${depotID}/manifest/${manifestID}/5${manifestRequestCode}${token}` ``) and `downloadChunk` (line ~337: `` `${urlBase}/depot/${depotID}/chunk/${chunkSha1}${token}` ``) both append the resolved `token` DIRECTLY to the URL with **no separator inserted by the caller**. found: this is only correct if `body.token` (the raw string Steam's CM sends back) already contains its own leading `?` — consistent with Steam's CDN auth token historically being a full query-string fragment (`?...`), per both SteamKit2's CDNClient and node-steam-user's own usage pattern here. implication: `depot/cdnAuth.ts`'s `getToken()` returns the token STRING UNMODIFIED (never inserts its own `?`/`&`) for the caller to append verbatim, mirroring steam-user's own discipline exactly — confirmed by reading the actual installed library's usage, not guessed.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action (fix implementation, see Fix Applied below): implemented `depot/cdnAuth.ts`'s `CdnAuthTokenCache` — a per-download-run (never module-level) in-memory cache keyed by `${depotId}_${host}` (mirroring steam-user's own internal cache key), with proactive refresh (`PROACTIVE_REFRESH_BUFFER_MS = 5min` before reported expiry) and reactive refresh (an explicit `invalidate(depotId, host)` called by `fetchChunk` on any 401/403 response, so the NEXT attempt against that exact depot+host re-fetches instead of repeating a rejected token). Concurrent callers for the SAME depot+host are coalesced onto a single in-flight fetch via a `pending` map — never one CM round-trip per chunk. A token-fetch failure is caught, logged (redacted), and degrades to `''` (token-less request) rather than throwing — a content server that doesn't actually need a token, or a transient CM hiccup, must never become a hard chunk-fetch failure by itself; fetchChunk's own existing retry/host-rotation mechanism remains the single place a genuine chunk failure is decided.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: threaded `cdnAuth?: CdnAuthTokenCache` through `fetchChunk` (decompress.ts, new optional 11th positional param, after `hostHealth`) → `downloadFileChunks` → `downloadSingleFile` → `downloadDepotFiles`'s `DownloadDepotFilesOpts.cdnAuth`. `downloadSteamDepots` constructs ONE `CdnAuthTokenCache(client, Number(appId))` per run (where the authenticated `client` is available — same "stays decoupled from the SteamUser client for testability" discipline `hosts`/`hostWeightedLoads` already established) and passes it through. `fetchChunk`'s chunk URL becomes `` `https://${host}/depot/${depotId}/chunk/${sha}${token}` `` where `token` is `''` when `cdnAuth` is omitted (every pre-cycle-6 caller/test) — byte-for-byte unchanged for every existing call site.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: `getCDNAuthToken` added to `SteamUserDepotExtras` (depot.ts) with the exact verified signature, documented inline with the same Pitfall-5 discipline as its siblings.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: SECURITY — every log line in `depot/cdnAuth.ts` reports only `depot`/`host`/`tokenLen`/`expiresInMs` on success, or `depot`/`host`/the thrown error on failure — the token VALUE is never passed to `logInfo`/`logWarning` anywhere in this module. Verified by a dedicated test (`cdnAuth.test.ts`, "SECURITY: the token value is NEVER present in any logInfo/logWarning call") that plants a distinctive secret token string and asserts it never appears in any logged call's serialized arguments. Never persisted to disk; lives only in the in-memory, per-run `CdnAuthTokenCache` instance.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 11 targeted suites (depot, installLocation, user, games, library, decompressPool, hostHealth, depotPrimitives, cdnAuth [NEW], reconcile, clientSetup): 11 suites, 515/515 pass (28 new: 13 in the new `cdnAuth.test.ts` [cache hit/miss/expiry/refresh/concurrent-dedup/fetch-failure-degrades/fetch-failure-not-cached/invalidate/invalidate-scoping/no-op-invalidate/2 SECURITY-redaction tests], 5 new in `depotPrimitives.test.ts`'s new "with a CdnAuthTokenCache (cycle 6)" describe block [URL-construction, once-per-depot+host-across-chunks, cdnAuth-omitted-unchanged, 401-invalidate-and-refetch, token-fetch-failure-degrades-gracefully], 1 new in `depot.test.ts` proving `downloadSteamDepots` threads a REAL `CdnAuthTokenCache` bound to the actual client + numeric appId into `fetchChunk`). Full suite: 82/82 suites (+1, the new `cdnAuth.test.ts`), 1507/1507 tests (1488 cycle-5 baseline + 19 new: 13+5+1). The pre-existing, unrelated `readAcfState`/`pollInstallOnce` leaked-timer teardown warning still fires identically — confirmed present in the SAME form across every prior cycle's self-verification, not introduced by this cycle. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression; no change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched); no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate; no change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate (T-21-03, `decodeChunk`) is completely untouched — the token only changes the URL a chunk is fetched FROM, never the decrypt/decompress/verify path; every pre-cycle-6 `fetchChunk`/`downloadDepotFiles`/`downloadSteamDepots` test passes UNMODIFIED, proving `cdnAuth` is provably inert when omitted.

### Fix Applied (cycle 6, 2026-07-18, pending sixth hardware re-verification)

reasoning_checkpoint_6:
  hypothesis: "fetchChunk builds chunk URLs with no CDN auth token, and nothing in this codebase calls steam-user's getCDNAuthToken. The cycle-5 hardware run's failures correlate EXACTLY with content-server `type`: every type=CDN host (weightedload=130, requires a token) failed ~100%, while every type=SteamCache host (token-less) worked — locking this codebase out of the CDN half of the six-host pool entirely and explaining why cycle 3/5's selection logic could only ever converge onto 2-3 overloaded local caches. Adding CDN auth token support (fetched once per depot+host, cached, refreshed proactively and on 401/403) directly restores the missing capability the real Steam client already has via GetCDNAuthToken."
  confirming_evidence:
    - "Cycle-5's own per-host data, re-read through the `type` field this cycle actually looked at: alibaba (type=CDN, wl=130) 0/258; steampipe.akamaized.net (type=CDN, wl=130) ~1/207; fastly (type=CDN, wl=130) ~5/264 — three-for-three CDN hosts failing near-100%, vs the SteamCache locals (cache1/cache2-akl-tpwr, cache1-akl-edgx, all type=SteamCache) which had real (if uneven) success rates in the SAME run."
    - "Directly reading the INSTALLED steam-user@5.3.0 source (cdn.js) confirms `getCDNAuthToken` exists, is never called anywhere in this codebase (confirmed via grep across storeManagers/steam before this cycle), and that steam-user's OWN `getRawManifest`/`downloadChunk` call sites append its resolved token to a CDN URL — i.e. steam-user's own authors treat SOME servers as requiring this token for exactly this kind of request."
    - "`cdn.js`'s `getRawManifest`/`downloadChunk` only fetch a token when `contentServer.usetokenauth == 1` — a per-server flag our own `RawContentServer`/`SteamUserDepotExtras.getContentServers` declaration never captured (widened in diagnostic cycle 4 to include weightedload/load/etc, but not `usetokenauth`, since cycle 4 predates this cycle's directive). This cycle's fix applies the token to EVERY host uniformly (both CDN and SteamCache) rather than gating on `usetokenauth`, per the fix directive's own guidance (\"appending uniformly to both CDN and SteamCache hosts is standard\") — a SteamCache host that doesn't need a token is expected to simply ignore the extra query-string fragment; the next hardware run's per-host stats will directly confirm whether the previously-working SteamCache hosts still succeed with a token appended, or whether this uniform-append assumption needs to become conditional on `usetokenauth` in a future cycle."
  falsification_test: "On the next hardware reproduction with the `[Timing] CDN auth token acquired` log lines now present: if the type=CDN hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) STILL fail near-100% despite a token being acquired and appended, that is important NEW evidence — either the token granularity/format is wrong (e.g. needs per-request-not-per-depot+host, or needs a different separator than steam-user's own bare-append convention), or CDN auth tokens are not actually the gating factor for this specific title/region, and the investigation must continue rather than treating this as resolved. If the SteamCache hosts (cache1/cache2-akl-tpwr) start FAILING once a token is appended to their URLs too, the uniform-append assumption is refuted for at least some SteamCache edges and the fix should gate token-appending on `usetokenauth` (or per-host observed behavior) in a follow-up cycle."
  fix_rationale: "This is a genuinely new capability (calling an API this codebase never called before), not a tuning of the existing retry/selection/backoff mechanisms cycles 1-5 already built and validated — all of which are explicitly retained unchanged (hostHealth's scoring/circuit-breaker, the weightedload prior, the capped backoff, the bounded per-attempt timeout, the full instrumentation). The token cache's design (per-depot+host, proactive+reactive refresh, coalesced concurrent fetches, graceful degradation on failure) directly targets the fix directive's explicit constraints: never fetch per-chunk, never let a token-service hiccup become a hard chunk failure, never log the credential value."
  blind_spots: "No fresh hardware reproduction has been run with this cycle's fix yet — the 'CDN hosts will now succeed' claim is inferred from (a) steam-user's own source treating GetCDNAuthToken as the mechanism gating access to `usetokenauth` servers (a directly-read fact), and (b) the cycle-5 hardware run's own type=CDN-vs-type=SteamCache failure correlation (a directly-observed fact from THIS codebase's own prior reproduction) — not from a captured run with a token actually being sent. The uniform-append-to-every-host decision (rather than gating on the undeclared `usetokenauth` field) is the fix directive's own explicit guidance, but is unverified against this specific region/title's SteamCache hosts — flagged above as the falsification test's second branch. It remains possible (unlikely per the directive's own reasoning, but not eliminated) that the type=CDN hosts fail for an UNRELATED reason (e.g. genuine regional routing/blocking) and a token changes nothing, in which case this cycle's fix would be a correct, safe, well-tested capability addition that nonetheless doesn't move the needle on THIS specific symptom — the hardware run is the only way to know."

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/cdnAuth.ts` (NEW FILE): `CdnAuthTokenCache` — verified steam-user API, per-depot+host in-memory cache, proactive (5min-before-expiry) + reactive (`invalidate()` on 401/403) refresh, concurrent-fetch coalescing, graceful `''`-on-failure degradation, redacted logging. `CDNAuthTokenClient` interface documents the exact verified `getCDNAuthToken` signature.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: `fetchChunk` gained an optional trailing `cdnAuth?: CdnAuthTokenCache` parameter (after `hostHealth`). Each attempt appends `await cdnAuth.getToken(depotId, host)` to the chunk URL verbatim; a 401/403 response calls `cdnAuth.invalidate(depotId, host)` before falling into the existing retry/backoff/rotation path unchanged. Omitting `cdnAuth` (every pre-cycle-6 caller/test) leaves the URL byte-for-byte identical to before.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: `SteamUserDepotExtras` gained the verified `getCDNAuthToken` declaration. `cdnAuth?: CdnAuthTokenCache` threaded through `DownloadDepotFilesOpts` → `downloadFileChunks` → `downloadSingleFile` → each `fetchChunk` call site. `downloadSteamDepots` constructs ONE `CdnAuthTokenCache(client, Number(appId))` per run (alongside the existing `hosts`/`hostWeightedLoads` resolution) and passes it into `downloadDepotFiles`'s opts.
- Deliberately NOT changed this cycle: `hostHealth.ts`'s scoring/circuit-breaker/weightedload-prior logic (cycles 3/5, unchanged and still the tiebreak across the — now hopefully fully-functional — six-host pool); `fetchChunk`'s attempt-count/backoff/timeout mechanics (cycles 1/3, unchanged); the sha1 chunk-integrity gate (T-21-03, `decodeChunk` — untouched, the token only changes the fetch URL, never the decrypt/decompress/verify path); `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` → whole-install `status:'error'` escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; gating token-append on the undeclared `usetokenauth` field (per the fix directive's own guidance to append uniformly and let the hardware run confirm/refute — see blind_spots above).

**Self-verification (cycle 6, 2026-07-18):** see Evidence entry above — tsc clean; 11 targeted suites 515/515; full suite 82/82 suites, 1507/1507 tests; no regressions; pre-existing unrelated leaked-timer warning confirmed not introduced by this cycle; every pre-cycle-6 test passes UNMODIFIED, proving `cdnAuth` is inert when omitted; a dedicated SECURITY test proves the token value never reaches any log call.

**NOT yet verified:** the actual real-world effect on the type=CDN hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) — this requires a sixth hardware reproduction (same Cyberpunk 1091500 or similarly sized title, same Auckland/NZ network) with this cycle's `[Timing] CDN auth token acquired` log lines and the retained `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` per-host `ok=`/`wl=`/`UNHEALTHY` fields, to directly confirm the type=CDN hosts now show `ok>0` instead of near-100% failure, that the SteamCache hosts still succeed with a token appended, and that aggregate throughput/survival improves toward Steam-parity. Per the objective, this session is NOT marked resolved — a hardware reproduction remains the mandatory next checkpoint.

## Cycle 7 (2026-07-18) — DISPROOF (cycle 6) + consolidated FIX: steam-user URL/token parity + completion robustness

**Cycle-6 hardware run result (2026-07-18, reported by user):** WORSE than cycle 5. The dev app quit almost immediately after starting the install ("dev app is DOWN (broken cycle-6 build quit)" per the cycle-7 directive). Root cause: cycle 6 called `getCDNAuthToken` UNCONDITIONALLY whenever `cdnAuth` was supplied, for EVERY host on EVERY chunk-fetch attempt. steam-user's own `downloadChunk`/`getRawManifest` (installed steam-user@5.3.0 source, `node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn.js`) only requests a token `if (contentServer.usetokenauth == 1)` — and `getContentServers`'s own reduction (same file, ~L67) has that exact field HARD-CODED commented out (`//usetokenauth: '1'`), so it is NEVER actually populated on a real directory response. The CM never answers a token request for a server that never asked for one; cycle 6's unconditional fetch blocked EVERY single chunk behind a stuck token round-trip, strangling throughput to ~0 — strictly worse than cycle 5, including on the previously-healthy Auckland caches. This DISPROVES the cycle-6 hypothesis ("appending a token uniformly to every host unlocks the type=CDN hosts") as implemented — the underlying idea (steam-user supports a CDN auth token mechanism) was correct, but the trigger condition (append unconditionally) was wrong; the real gate is `usetokenauth == 1`, a field this codebase never even declared until now.

### Eliminated (cycle 7)

- hypothesis: "appending a CDN auth token to EVERY chunk request, unconditionally, whenever a `CdnAuthTokenCache` is available, unlocks the type=CDN hosts (steampipe.akamaized.net/alibaba/fastly) without regressing the type=SteamCache hosts" — ELIMINATED by direct hardware observation: the dev app quit almost immediately after the cycle-6 build's install started; the unconditional token fetch blocked every chunk (including on the SteamCache hosts, which never needed a token and previously worked fine) behind a CM round-trip that never resolves for a non-`usetokenauth` server. Re-reading the INSTALLED steam-user@5.3.0 source (`node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn.js` lines ~238, ~314-337) confirms steam-user's OWN reference client only fetches a token `if (contentServer.usetokenauth == 1)`, with the maintainer's own comment: "I'm not sure that any servers use token auth anymore." `getContentServers`'s own server-reduction has `usetokenauth` commented out entirely — it is structurally never present on a real response. REPLACED (this cycle) by gating strictly on a real, directory-sourced `usetokenauth` flag (which in practice stays false/absent for every host observed so far, matching steam-user's own comment) — the cache/mechanism itself is retained (not deleted), just correctly dormant.

'https://' : 'http://') + server.Host` and only fetch a token `if (contentServer.usetokenauth == 1)`, appending the resolved token DIRECTLY (`${urlBase}/depot/${depotID}/chunk/${chunkSha1}${token}`, no separator inserted). implication: the cycle-6 "uniform append" fix directive guidance is now understood to have been based on an incomplete reading of `cdn.js` — the gate exists and this codebase must honor it exactly, both for the token AND for the URL scheme (this codebase hardcoded `https://` unconditionally pre-cycle-7, which is ALSO wrong per this same source: e.g. `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com`'s directory-reported `https_support: 'unavailable'` per the cycle-5 hardware diagnosis means steam-user's own reference client would request it over PLAIN HTTP, never https — a second, independently-disprovable defect this cycle also closes).
