'https://' : 'http://'` (exact steam-user parity — only an explicit `'mandatory'` selects https; a MISSING hostMeta entirely still defaults to `https://` for backward compatibility with every pre-cycle-7 caller/test) and `token = meta?.usetokenauth && cdnAuth ? await cdnAuth.getToken(...) : ''`. This unlocks `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` (`https_support: 'unavailable'` per the cycle-5 hardware diagnosis) to be requested over plain http, matching what the real Steam client would do.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — SECURITY re-confirmed: an http request for a host the directory marks non-https is unencrypted chunk transport, but the `sha1(decompressed) === chunk.sha` gate (T-21-03, enforced inside `decodeChunk`/the injected `decode`, completely untouched this cycle) already verifies decrypted CONTENT regardless of transport — tampering in transit is still caught. steam-user's own reference client does exactly this for `https_support` values other than `'mandatory'`.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked (design decision, documented for the record): chose a SIDE-CHANNEL metadata map (`hostMeta: ReadonlyMap<string, ContentServerHostMeta>`) threaded ALONGSIDE the existing bare-hostname `hosts: string[]`/`HostHealthTracker` selection, rather than widening `hosts` itself into an array of richer `{host, https_support, usetokenauth, weightedload}` records end-to-end. Both approaches deliver the identical observable behavior (per-host scheme/token parity, hostHealth/weightedload selection preserved). The side-channel map was chosen because it is purely ADDITIVE (an optional trailing parameter, exactly like every other cycle's new capability in this file) and requires ZERO changes to `hostHealth.ts`/`hostHealth.test.ts` (pickHost continues to operate on plain hostnames, its ~15 existing tests are completely unaffected) or to the ~25 `downloadDepotFiles` test call sites that share a single `HOSTS` fixture constant — versus a full type change, which would force every existing `fetchChunk`/`pickHost` test in `depotPrimitives.test.ts` and `hostHealth.test.ts` to be rewritten to pass/expect host RECORDS instead of bare strings, for no behavioral gain. This keeps the change minimal and low-blast-radius, consistent with every prior cycle's discipline in this file.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action (PART 3, completion robustness): **NEW FILE** `depot/stallTracker.ts` — `StallTracker`, a per-download-run (never module-level) forward-progress clock: `recordProgress()` resets a `lastProgressAt` timestamp whenever ANY bytes land on disk for ANY file in the run; `hasStalled()` is true once `STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (3 minutes — chosen well above the ~7min near-zero-but-nonzero-throughput window cycle 3's diagnosis observed, so a merely-slow run is never mistaken for a genuinely wedged one) has elapsed with zero recorded progress anywhere. `downloadDepotFiles` constructs ONE `StallTracker` per run (same discipline as `HostHealthTracker`/`CdnAuthTokenCache`) and threads it through `downloadSingleFile`/`downloadFileChunks` alongside the existing optional params.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: `downloadFileChunks` (depot.ts, now EXPORTED for direct unit testing) wraps its `await fetchChunk(...)` call in a try/catch. On success it calls `stallTracker?.recordProgress()` (resetting the WHOLE-RUN clock, not just this file's). On a caught exception (fetchChunk exhausted its `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS` budget): if `signal?.aborted`, returns immediately (D-UAT-05/06 unaffected — a user cancel still cancels promptly, even mid re-queue-eligible failure); else if a `stallTracker` was supplied AND `!stallTracker.hasStalled()`, the FAILED CHUNK IS RE-QUEUED (`queue.push(chunk)`) and the worker's `while` loop continues — this chunk gets another pass later (by whichever worker picks it up next) instead of throwing out of this `Promise.all` and killing every OTHER in-flight chunk-worker for this same file; else (genuinely stalled, or `stallTracker` omitted entirely) the error propagates as a real, honest failure — appending "(download stalled: no forward progress for Nms across the whole run)" to the message when a stallTracker was present, so the classified error text still matches `classifyDepotError`'s existing `failed after \d+ attempts` connection-dropped pattern. Omitting `stallTracker` (every pre-cycle-7 caller/test) preserves the EXACT pre-cycle-7 behavior: immediate rethrow, no retry, no requeue.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the steam-specific suites: 16 suites, 624/624 pass (598 cycle-6 baseline + 26 new: 4 in `reduceContentServers`'s new `usetokenauth`/`hostMeta` describe additions, 4 in `depotPrimitives.test.ts`'s new "URL scheme parity via hostMeta" describe block + 2 new "PART 1 REVERT REGRESSION GUARD" tests, 4 in the new `downloadFileChunks` completion-robustness describe block in `depot.test.ts`, plus the pre-existing cycle-6 cdnAuth-threading test updated in place [not a net-new test] and several existing cdnAuth tests updated to supply `hostMeta` so they still exercise a REAL token fetch under the new, correct gate). Full suite: 82/82 suites, 1520/1520 tests pass (1507 cycle-6 baseline + 13 net-new — some cycle-6 tests were updated in place rather than added). Confirmed via `git stash`/`git stash pop` re-run of the identical steam-suite command that the pre-existing, unrelated `readAcfState`/`pollInstallOnce` leaked-timer teardown warning is byte-for-byte identical with or without this cycle's changes (598 baseline tests either way) — not introduced by this cycle. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression; no change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 — explicitly re-tested this cycle with a dedicated "user cancel still exits promptly even while a chunk would otherwise be re-queued" test); no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate; no change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate (T-21-03, `decodeChunk`) is completely untouched — hostMeta only changes the URL a chunk is fetched FROM (scheme + optional token), never the decrypt/decompress/verify path. Every pre-cycle-7 `fetchChunk`/`downloadFileChunks`/`downloadDepotFiles`/`hostHealth`/`reduceContentServers` test that predates this cycle passes UNMODIFIED or was updated ONLY where cycle 6's now-reverted unconditional-token assumption made the update necessary (documented per-test above) — `hostHealth.ts`/`hostHealth.test.ts` are BYTE-FOR-BYTE untouched this cycle (the side-channel `hostMeta` design's whole point).

### Fix Applied (cycle 7, 2026-07-18, pending seventh hardware re-verification)

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: `fetchChunk` gained a new optional trailing `hostMeta?: ReadonlyMap<string, ContentServerHostMeta>` parameter (new exported `ContentServerHostMeta` type: `{ httpsSupport?: string; usetokenauth?: boolean }`). URL scheme is now `!meta || meta.httpsSupport === 'mandatory' ? 'https://' : 'http://'` (was hardcoded `https://`). The CDN auth token is now fetched ONLY when `meta?.usetokenauth && cdnAuth` (was: whenever `cdnAuth` was merely supplied — REVERTED). The 401/403 `cdnAuth.invalidate(...)` call is now also gated on `meta?.usetokenauth`. Omitting `hostMeta` (every pre-cycle-7 caller/test) preserves the exact pre-cycle-7 URL.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: `RawContentServer` gained `usetokenauth?: number`. `reduceContentServers` now ALSO returns `hostMeta: Map<string, ContentServerHostMeta>` (mapping `usetokenauth === 1` to `true`, exact steam-user parity, never a loose truthy check) alongside the existing `hosts`/`weightedLoads`. `getContentServerHosts` returns `{ hosts, weightedLoads, hostMeta }`. `DownloadDepotFilesOpts` gained `hostMeta?: ReadonlyMap<string, ContentServerHostMeta>`, threaded through `downloadFileChunks`/`downloadSingleFile`/`downloadDepotFiles`/`downloadSteamDepots` the same way `hostWeightedLoads`/`cdnAuth` already are. The `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` worst-hosts log line gained `scheme=`/`usetokenauth` fields per host (reads `opts.hostMeta`), so the next hardware run's log directly shows the scheme/token-gate state per host alongside the existing `wl=`/`UNHEALTHY` fields.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`: **NEW behavior (Part 3)** — `downloadFileChunks` (now `export`ed for direct unit testing) gained an optional trailing `stallTracker?: StallTracker` parameter. A chunk that exhausts `fetchChunk`'s `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS` budget is now RE-QUEUED (not thrown) as long as `stallTracker` is supplied and the whole download run hasn't stalled (`!stallTracker.hasStalled()`); a genuine give-up (stalled, or `stallTracker` omitted) still throws, preserving the classified `failed after \d+ attempts` message text. `downloadSingleFile` forwards the new param. `downloadDepotFiles` constructs ONE `StallTracker` per run and passes it through; every successful chunk write calls `stallTracker.recordProgress()`.
- **NEW FILE** `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/stallTracker.ts`: `StallTracker` class + `STALL_TIMEOUT_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000` constant — see doc comment for the full root-cause writeup. `now` is an explicit, injectable parameter on every method for deterministic unit testing (no real sleeping required).
- Deliberately NOT changed this cycle: `hostHealth.ts`'s scoring/circuit-breaker/weightedload-prior logic (cycles 3/5, completely untouched — `pickHost` still operates on plain hostnames); `fetchChunk`'s attempt-count/backoff/timeout mechanics (cycles 1/3, unchanged); the sha1 chunk-integrity gate (T-21-03); `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` → whole-install `status:'error'` escalation logic (still the final reporting step — Part 3 changes what happens BEFORE that point, not the escalation itself); `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; `depot/cdnAuth.ts` itself (retained, unchanged, now correctly gated by its caller).

## Cycle 8 (2026-07-18) — FINAL: cycle-7 hardware confirmed + weightedLoads=0 bug fixed + session closed out

**Cycle-7 hardware run result (2026-07-18, reported by user):** cycle 7's mechanics work as designed — no `[Timing] CDN auth token acquired` spam (Part 1's revert holds), `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` correctly showed `scheme=http` (Part 2's URL/token parity holds), and the `StallTracker`-backed re-queue kept the run alive well past cycle-5's earlier death point (no premature single-chunk-failure abort). The remaining slowness/failure is judged ENVIRONMENTAL, not a code defect: the previously-reliable Auckland SteamCache edges were fast-rejecting (~78-90ms, ~100% fail) — the signature of a CDN-side rate limit, almost certainly self-inflicted from ~8-9GB pulled repeatedly across ~4 hours of iterative hardware testing on cycles 1-7. Per the user's explicit direction this cycle, this environmental condition is NOT chased further.

**Real bug found and fixed this cycle:** the `[Timing] getContentServerHosts ... hosts=6, weightedLoads=0` log line (present in every cycle-5/6/7 hardware run) showed the weightedload-aware selection built in cycle 5 was structurally inert on every real run — `reduceContentServers`'s `weightedLoads` map was always empty despite the directory returning a real ranking.

reasoning_checkpoint_8:
  hypothesis: "reduceContentServers's `if (typeof s.weightedload === 'number' && Number.isFinite(s.weightedload))` guard (depot.ts) silently drops every real directory response, because the directory actually returns `weightedload`/`load` as STRINGS (confirmed by the cycle-4/5 RAW JSON dump, e.g. `\"weightedload\": \"130\"`) — `typeof '130' === 'number'` is false, so the entry is skipped and the whole `weightedLoads` map for every real host stays empty (`weightedLoads.size === 0`), matching the exact `weightedLoads=0` observed in every hardware log across cycles 5-7. This makes cycle 5's weightedload-aware HostHealthTracker prior permanently inert on real hardware even though its unit tests (which only ever used numeric fixtures) all pass."
  confirming_evidence:
    - "Cycle-5's own captured directory RAW dump (Evidence, cycle 5): the JSON shown for e.g. `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` includes `weightedload: 130` rendered without quotes in the session's prose summary, but re-reading the actual RAW JSON string logged by `[Timing] getContentServerHosts RAW for appId ...` (the literal JSON.stringify output steam-user's directory returns) shows the field serialized as a quoted string — consistent with steam-user's CM/protobuf layer returning these directory fields as strings, not numbers (the same pattern already independently confirmed for `usetokenauth` needing an `=== 1` numeric-strict check, not a loose truthy one, in cycle 7)."
    - "Every hardware-run log line across cycles 5, 6, and 7 that reports `getContentServerHosts: ... hosts=6, weightedLoads=0` (0, not 6) — directly observed, not inferred — despite `reduceContentServers`'s own unit tests (which pass numeric fixtures like `{ weightedload: 37 }`) asserting `weightedLoads.get(...)` returns the expected value. This is the exact 'tests used numeric fixtures, production data is stringly-typed' gap the fix directive named."
    - "Reading `RawContentServer`'s declaration (depot.ts, pre-cycle-8): `weightedload?: number` — a TypeScript-level assumption about the shape of an external, undocumented (Pitfall 5) API response that was never actually verified against the real wire format, unlike `usetokenauth`'s `=== 1` gate which WAS independently re-verified against the installed steam-user source in cycle 7."
  falsification_test: "After the fix, the next hardware log line should read `weightedLoads=6` (matching `hosts=6`) instead of `weightedLoads=0` for a directory response that supplies weightedload for every host. If it still reads 0, either the coercion itself is wrong (e.g. the field is nested/renamed differently than assumed) or the RAW dump's actual field name/shape differs from what cycles 4-7 documented — return to investigation rather than trusting this fix blind. The new depot.test.ts regression test (string-valued weightedload fixtures) already exercises the exact shape observed in the RAW dumps, so tsc+jest passing is a real (if not hardware-confirmed) signal, not just a tautology."
  fix_rationale: "Coercing via `Number(...)` (NaN-guarded) rather than tightening/loosening the `typeof` check preserves reduceContentServers's existing, deliberate design invariant (a host with a missing or genuinely non-numeric weightedload is OMITTED from the map, never given a synthetic worst-case value) while accepting the real-world string encoding. This is the smallest change that fixes the actual defect: the field-presence/finite-value semantics are unchanged, only the type-coercion step is added. `RawContentServer.weightedload`/`load`'s declared type widened from `number` to `number | string` so the type system reflects the verified real shape rather than an unverified assumption."
  blind_spots: "No fresh hardware reproduction has been run with this specific fix — cycle 7's hardware run happened BEFORE this bug was fixed, so `weightedLoads=0` was still true during that run; the ENVIRONMENTAL rate-limiting conclusion for cycle 7 was reached independently of this bug (via per-host latency/fail-rate signature, not via weightedload). This fix is expected to make cycle 5's weightedload PRIOR start actually influencing cold-start host selection on the NEXT clean run, but that improvement is unconfirmed against real CDN behavior — the user has explicitly directed that no further hardware reproduction be chased this cycle due to the self-inflicted rate-limiting, so this fix is landing without its own dedicated hardware verification. It is a small, well-tested, low-risk, additive-coercion-only change with an exact regression test matching the real observed data shape."

### Evidence (cycle 8, 2026-07-18)

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked (`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts:1717-1741`, `reduceContentServers`): confirmed the guard `if (typeof s.weightedload === 'number' && Number.isFinite(s.weightedload))` requires the RAW value to already be a JS `number` — a real directory response's `weightedload`/`load` fields are STRINGS (per the cycle-4/5 RAW dump evidence), so this guard is `false` for every real server, leaving `weightedLoads` permanently empty. found: this exactly matches every hardware log's `weightedLoads=0` (vs `hosts=6`) across cycles 5-7. implication: cycle 5's weightedload-aware `HostHealthTracker` prior has never actually been exercised with real data on any hardware run to date — it degraded to the pre-cycle-5 neutral cold-start ordering every single time, silently.

Changed `reduceContentServers`'s weightedload handling to coerce via `Number(s.weightedload)` (guarding `undefined`/`null` first, then `Number.isFinite` on the coerced result) before `.set(host, numWeightedload)` — a non-numeric string (e.g. malformed data) is still omitted from the map exactly as before, never stored as `NaN` or a synthetic default.

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 8 steam-specific suites most relevant to this fix (depot, depotPrimitives, hostHealth, cdnAuth, installLocation, user, games, library): 8 suites, 498/498 pass (496 cycle-7 baseline + 2 new regression tests). ### Fix Applied (cycle 8, 2026-07-18, FINAL — committed, hardware-completion-verification parked)

- `StallTracker`'s re-queue mechanism kept a run alive well past cycle-5's earlier death point (completion-robustness works).

**What is NOT hardware-confirmed:**
- A full install reaching StateFlags=4 completion on a healthy (non-rate-limited) CDN session.
- The weightedLoads=0 fix (this cycle) improving real host selection — landed via self-verification (tsc + 1522/1522 jest) only, no dedicated hardware run, per explicit user direction not to chase the environmental condition further this cycle.

**Honest caveat:** the code fixes across cycles 1-8 are correct, self-verified, and committed. They are NOT independently proven to resolve the original ~30s-latency/mid-stream-failure symptom end-to-end on real Steam CDN infrastructure. A clean-network completion run (ideally after the Auckland caches' rate-limit window has expired — likely hours, not verified) remains the outstanding confirmation step for this session. Re-open this file (do not start a new session) when that run is available.

## Resolution (FINAL, cycle 8) — supersedes the cycle-1 Resolution block above

root_cause: The ~30s pre-download-latency framing (session trigger) was ELIMINATED early (cycle 1 hardware reproduction: warm-session setup is ~4.1s, not the bottleneck). The REAL, multi-layered root cause discovered across cycles 1-8, in the chunk-stream phase (`downloadDepotFiles`/`downloadFileChunks`/`fetchChunk`, depot.ts + depot/decompress.ts + depot/hostHealth.ts + depot/cdnAuth.ts + depot/stallTracker.ts):
  1. (cycle 1) `fetchChunk`'s CDN fetch had no per-attempt timeout, and the hard-coded `attempts=4` gave too little retry headroom for a large game's thousands of chunks.
  2. (cycle 3) Plain round-robin host selection gave a 0%-success content-server host the same attempt share (and backoff-sleep time) as genuinely healthy hosts, collapsing aggregate throughput via retry/backoff starvation even with zero hung requests.
  3. (cycle 5) The content-server directory's own `weightedload` ranking (favoring local SteamCache edges over global CDN fallbacks) was discarded entirely by the pre-existing hostname-only reduction.
  4. (cycle 6, then reverted in cycle 7) CDN auth tokens were fetched unconditionally instead of being gated on the directory's per-host `usetokenauth` flag, which strangled throughput to near-zero on a broken build.
  5. (cycle 7) URL scheme was hard-coded to `https://` regardless of a host's directory-reported `https_support`, causing 100% failure on HTTP-only edges (e.g. `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com`); a single exhausted chunk also escalated to a whole-install hard failure with no forward-progress-aware retry.
  6. (cycle 8, THIS cycle) `reduceContentServers`'s `weightedload` guard required the raw directory value to already be a JS `number`, but the real directory response returns `weightedload`/`load` as STRINGS — silently dropping every real-world entry and leaving `weightedLoads` permanently empty (`weightedLoads=0` on every hardware run across cycles 5-7), making fix #3 (weightedload-aware selection) inert on real hardware despite passing its own numeric-fixture unit tests.
  Residual (NOT a code defect, confirmed via cycle-7 hardware run's per-host latency/fail-rate signature): the Auckland test network is currently CDN-rate-limited (~78-90ms fast-reject, ~100% fail on previously-reliable local caches), almost certainly self-inflicted from ~8-9GB pulled repeatedly across ~4 hours of iterative hardware testing across cycles 1-7. This is explicitly NOT chased per user direction — no code change can fix a temporary, self-inflicted rate limit.
fix: Cumulative, cycles 1-8 (see each cycle's "Fix Applied" section above for full detail): bounded per-attempt chunk-fetch timeout + capped exponential backoff + raised retry budget (cycle 1/3); health-aware host selection with a directory-weightedload-seeded prior, empirical success/latency scoring, and a consecutive-failure circuit breaker (cycle 3/5, `depot/hostHealth.ts`); a dormant, `usetokenauth`-gated CDN auth token cache (cycle 6/7, `depot/cdnAuth.ts`); per-host URL-scheme parity via directory `https_support` metadata (cycle 7); forward-progress-aware chunk re-queueing via `StallTracker` instead of single-chunk-failure whole-install abort (cycle 7, `depot/stallTracker.ts`); and, this cycle, `Number(...)`-coercion (NaN-guarded) of the directory's string-typed `weightedload` field in `reduceContentServers`, which had silently made the cycle-5 weightedload prior inert on every real hardware run to date.
verification: tsc --noEmit clean. Steam-specific suites: 8 suites, 498/498 pass. Full suite: 82/82 suites, 1522/1522 tests pass. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression; D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 abort-vs-drop semantics unaffected; `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate unaffected; Phase-23 single-flight guard unaffected; sha1 chunk-integrity gate (T-21-03) unaffected. Hardware-verified (cycle 7, pre-dates this cycle's weightedLoads fix): no CDN-auth-token spam, correct `scheme=http` on HTTP-only hosts, StallTracker re-queue survives past prior death points. NOT hardware-verified: a full clean-network install completion to StateFlags=4, and this cycle's specific `weightedLoads` fix's effect on real host-selection behavior — both parked pending a non-rate-limited test window (see FINAL STATUS above).

### Resolution update (cycle 12, 2026-07-19) — Option B's RPC mechanism now UNDERSTOOD (not fixed further)

The FINAL Resolution above (cycle 8, committed 33c108a0) remains the shipped baseline — nothing in this update changes it. This note records what cycles 9-12's re-opened investigation (diagnostic cycle 9 → RESEARCH SPIKE → cycles 10-12, "Option B") established about the residual 2.5x throughput gap the cycle-8 FINAL STATUS explicitly left open (CDN hosts unreachable without depot-level auth):

- **Mechanism confirmed working end-to-end, hardware-validated (cycle 12):** the manual `_send` bypass around steam-user's missing `_sendUnified` map entry for `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1` (cycle 11) correctly encodes, transmits, round-trips through Steam's CM, and decodes the response — `eresult=1` (OK), a real non-trivial `rawBodyBytes` (8), and a `decodedExpirationTime` that is live and increments correctly across repeated proactive-refresh calls (1784415459 → 1784415519/520 → 1784415521). This rules out BOTH a decode-side bug and a server-side rejection.
- **But the token field itself is genuinely, consistently empty** for every `type=CDN` host tested (akamai/fastly/alibaba) — a well-formed, server-accepted, correctly-decoded business response that simply contains no token. Option B's entire premise (fetch a token, append it to the chunk-fetch URL) has no token to append — **this specific RPC cannot unlock the CDN hosts**, regardless of how correctly it's called.
- **Open, unresolved question:** Steam's own `content_log.txt` (cited in the original RESEARCH SPIKE) shows the real client's `AuthenticateDepotID... Success!` firing on every `type=CDN` host — so either the real client authenticates CDN edges via a mechanism this RPC doesn't correspond to, or an empty token is itself the expected/correct response and the CDN chunk-fetch failures have a cause unrelated to authentication (e.g. the same environmental/rate-limit factor documented since cycle 8, or a real per-edge access rule). Cycle 13 (below) fixes the chunk-fetch failure-reason extraction specifically so the NEXT hardware run's real per-host reason codes (403 vs ECONNRESET vs a SteamCache-matching code) can settle this — see that section for the recommended path once that data exists.
- **Not changed by this update:** the RPC call itself (cdnAuth.ts), the `type==='CDN'` gate (PART 2), or PART-3's fail-fast/degrade-to-`''` guard — all remain exactly as cycle 10/11 left them, hardware-confirmed not to stall or regress the cycle-7/8 baseline.

### Resolution update (cycle 18, 2026-07-19) — Thread A (cancel/abort not clean): NEW, distinct symptom fixed; session title/scope now covers this too

Cycle 18 addresses a NEW, user-reported symptom distinct from this session's original throughput/latency root cause above — "cancels a native Steam download... but the game stays showing 'downloading'" — surfaced during further real-hardware testing alongside Thread B (auto-resume, recorded/scoped only) and Thread C (this session's original decode/throughput thread, still PARKED). See "Cancel/abort + auto-resume threads (user-reported)" section (near the top of this file) and "Cycle 18" section (bottom of file) for the full triage, root cause, fix, and test coverage.

root_cause (Thread A only): three distinct, compounding defects — (1) `aborthandler.ts`'s `callAbortController` logs a misleading "not found" error on the second of two harmless, idempotent same-id abort calls (`downloadqueue.ts`'s `stopCurrentDownload` + `SteamGame.stop()` both call it); (2) `fetchChunk` (depot/decompress.ts) never accepted or checked an external cancel signal, so a cancel arriving mid-retry had to wait out the whole call (worst case ~144s) — the hardware-observed ~62s hang; (3) two "Steam: ACF poller emits the real done" badge-suppression guards (`downloadmanager/utils.ts`, `downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts`) assumed a poller always eventually fires, which is false for a cancelled native install (no poller is ever started on that path).
fix (Thread A only): `aborthandler.ts` treats "found but already aborted" as a no-op, not a miss; `fetchChunk` gained an optional trailing `signal` parameter threaded from `downloadFileChunks`, forwarded into the same internal per-attempt `AbortController`, checked before hostHealth/onAttempt recording (protects D-UAT-05/06), and threaded into an abort-interruptible backoff sleep; `installQueueElement`/`removeFromQueue` each gained a narrowly-scoped exception (`wasAborted`/`forceStatusUpdate`) mirroring Phase 17's existing `deferredToSetup` pattern.

### Resolution update (cycle 19, 2026-07-19) — Thread A HARDWARE-VERIFIED; Thread B (auto-resume after close/crash): ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED

Thread A (cycle 18's fix) is now HARDWARE-VERIFIED: clean cancel confirmed on real hardware (button cleared to "Install" within seconds, no misleading abort-controller error, `status=cancelled`, files deleted). See the "CHECKPOINT — SATISFIED" entry (bottom of file) for the full record.

root_cause (Thread B only): `main.ts:570-573`'s unconditional 5s-post-launch `initQueue()` call restarts whatever survived at the DownloadManager's persisted queue head (electron-store `queue`), for any runner, with zero gating — a mechanism entirely separate from, and invisible to, the already-correct Steam startup gate (`library.ts`'s `scanDownloadingAppIds` surfacing loop, which only sets a passive `steamResumePending` UI flag). Neither the clean-close path nor the crash path flushes/marks the persisted queue entry, so both feed this same unconditional restart identically.
fix (Thread B only): `initQueue(isStartup = false)` — `main.ts`'s sole startup call becomes `initQueue(true)`; every other call site (Resume button, `addToQueue`'s idle kick, connectivity-restore auto-resume) keeps the default `false`. When `isStartup && element.params.runner === 'steam'`, the loop defers (no `installQueueElement` call), re-points `currentElement` at the deferred item, and lets `queueState` normalize to `'idle'` — already rendered by the frontend identically to `'paused'` (a "Resume download" affordance). GOG/Epic/Amazon are explicitly unaffected (scope decision: auto-resume-on-launch is intended, pre-existing behavior for those stores per this session's own prior D-UAT-05 investigation).
verification (Thread B only): tsc clean; 84/84 suites, 1595/1595 tests (1585 cycle-18 baseline + 10 net-new); zero regressions. Self-verified only — NOT yet hardware-confirmed (see the CHECKPOINT at the bottom of this file).

## Diagnostic cycle 9 (2026-07-18) — RE-OPENED: failure-reason capture, additive only, UNCOMMITTED

**Re-open scope (per explicit user directive):** cycle 8's `weightedLoads=0` fix is committed (33c108a0) and the session was closed out (21999b12). This cycle re-opens the SAME session for a tiny, additive, diagnostic-ONLY change — no behavior change, no new fix — to finally capture WHY chunks fail (HTTP status / error name-or-code), because every prior cycle's per-host stats (`[Timing] chunk-stream stats`) only ever recorded COUNTS (attempts/successes/timeouts/errors), never the underlying reason. This matters because the cycle-8 hardware run showed the previously-reliable Auckland caches fast-rejecting (~78-90ms, ~100% fail) and the session's own FINAL STATUS called this "environmental" (self-inflicted CDN rate-limiting) WITHOUT ever having observed an actual HTTP status/error code for those fast rejections — that conclusion was inferred from latency/fail-rate shape alone, not from a captured reason. This cycle closes that observability gap so the NEXT hardware run can distinguish rate-limit (429) vs auth (401/403) vs overload (503) vs connection-reset (ECONNRESET) vs something else, rather than guessing.

### Evidence (cycle 9, 2026-07-18)

- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked (graphify-first orientation, then `depot/decompress.ts`'s `fetchChunk` + `depot.ts`'s `onAttempt`/`HostStat`/`logChunkStreamStats`): confirmed `fetchChunk`'s catch block already computes `outcome` (`success`/`timeout`/`error`) and reports it via the existing `onAttempt` hook (cycle 2 instrumentation), but NEVER captured the underlying HTTP status or error code/name — for an HTTP non-ok response it threw a bare `new Error(\`CDN ${res.status}\`)` (status folded into a message string, never structured); for a thrown/network error it only checked `err.name === 'AbortError'` to set `outcome`, discarding `err.code`/`err.cause.code` entirely. `depot.ts`'s `HostStat`/`onAttempt` aggregator (built cycle 2, extended cycles 5/7 with `wl=`/`scheme=`/`usetokenauth`) had no field at all for a failure reason. implication: every prior cycle's per-host stats could show a host was failing, but never WHY — exactly the gap the FINAL STATUS's unverified "environmental rate-limit" inference fell into.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: added a small `ChunkHttpError` class (decompress.ts) carrying `res.status` (+ folds `res.statusText` into the message when present — cheap, already resolved synchronously on `res`, no extra I/O) so the catch block can read the exact status back instead of re-parsing text; added a pure `attemptFailureReason(err, timedOut)` helper that returns `String(status)` for a `ChunkHttpError`, `'AbortError'` for the bounded-timeout case, else `err.code ?? err.cause?.code ?? err.name ?? 'Error'` for a thrown network error. `ChunkAttemptEvent` gained an optional `reason?: string` field, populated in the existing catch block alongside the existing `message` field — the `CDN ${res.status}` throw site is now `new ChunkHttpError(res.status, res.statusText)` (message text unchanged in shape: still matches `classifyDepotError`'s `/CDN \d/i` pattern, confirmed — appending statusText after the status digit doesn't affect the regex, which has no end-anchor). Zero change to attempt count, backoff, host-rotation, or selection — `reason` is purely an additional field on the SAME event the existing hook already reports.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — action: `depot.ts`'s `HostStat` gained a `reasons: Map<string, number>` field, incremented in the existing `onAttempt` handler ONLY for `outcome === 'error'` attempts with a `reason` present (timeouts are already unambiguously `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` firing via the existing `timeouts` counter, so they are not broken down further — matches the directive's own example format). `logChunkStreamStats`'s existing worst-hosts rendering gained a compact `errBreakdown` (top 4 reasons by count, comma-joined `reason:count` pairs) folded into the EXISTING `err=` field as `err=<N>{<breakdown>}` (e.g. `err=427{429:400,ECONNRESET:20,503:7}`) — reuses the exact existing `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` line/interval/cadence (piggybacked on the pre-existing heartbeat, per cycle 2's design), no new log channel, no new interval.
- timestamp: 2026-07-18 — checked: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx jest` on the 9 steam-specific suites (depot, depotPrimitives, hostHealth, decompressPool, installLocation, user, games, library, cdnAuth): 9 suites, 515/515 pass (3 new regression tests in `depotPrimitives.test.ts`'s new "onAttempt reason reporting (diagnostic re-open, cycle 9)" describe block: HTTP-429-status reason, thrown-ECONNRESET-code reason, bounded-timeout AbortError reason). Full suite: 82/82 suites, 1525/1525 tests pass (1522 cycle-8 baseline + 3 new). The pre-existing, unrelated `readAcfState`/`pollInstallOnce` leaked-timer teardown warning fires identically to every prior cycle's confirmation — not introduced by this cycle. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression; no change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched); no change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate; no change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate (T-21-03, `decodeChunk`) completely untouched — this cycle only changes what gets REPORTED about a failed attempt, never the decrypt/decompress/verify path, never selection/retry/backoff/scheme/abort behavior. Every pre-cycle-9 `fetchChunk`/`onAttempt`/`HostStat` test/caller passes UNMODIFIED (the new `reason` field is additive-optional on an existing event shape).
