Status remains `fix_committed_hardware_verification_parked` — this diagnostic is NOT a resolution and does NOT change that status.

### How to read the next dev-log capture (cycle 9)

A short Install attempt is enough to see failures start accumulating — no need to run to completion. Every ~15s (the existing `[Timing] chunk-stream stats @Ns` cadence), each of the worst 6 hosts now renders as:

```
<host>[a=<attempts> ok=<successes> to=<timeouts> err=<errors>{<reason>:<count>,...} avgMs=<avg> wl=<weightedload> scheme=<https|http><usetokenauth?> <UNHEALTHY?>]
```

The NEW piece is the `{...}` breakdown immediately after `err=<N>` — up to the 4 most frequent failure reasons for that host, most-frequent first, as `reason:count` pairs summing to (at most) that host's `err=` count. A `reason` is either a plain HTTP status number (`429`, `403`, `503`, `404`) or a thrown error's code/name (`ECONNRESET`, `ECONNREFUSED`, `ETIMEDOUT`, `UND_ERR_SOCKET`, etc.) — never a full message/stack. Timeouts (`to=`) are NOT broken down further (an `AbortError` there is unambiguously `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` firing, already distinguished from `err=`).

**What to look for on the previously-reliable Auckland caches (`cache1-akl-tpwr`/`cache2-akl-tpwr`) if they're still fast-rejecting (~78-90ms, ~100% fail):**
- `429` dominant → genuine rate-limiting (the FINAL STATUS's inferred cause, now actually confirmable).
- `401`/`403` dominant → an auth/token rejection (unexpected — cycle 7 keeps CDN auth dormant for non-`usetokenauth` hosts; would be a new, surprising finding worth re-opening investigation over).
- `503`/`500` dominant → server-side overload, not a client-side rate-limit — a different mitigation would apply (e.g. more backoff headroom) than what a 429 would suggest.
- `ECONNRESET`/`ECONNREFUSED` dominant → the edge is actively refusing/dropping connections, closer to a network-level block than an HTTP-level rate-limit.
- No `err=` entries at all (all `to=`, i.e. timeouts) → NOT what cycle 8's "~78-90ms fast-reject" description implies (that's fast, not a 15s timeout) — would itself be a surprising, actionable finding.

This is purely observational data collection — no code path changed as a result of what is observed; a human decides what (if anything) to do with the breakdown once seen.

## ROOT CAUSE OF THE SPEED GAP — CONFIRMED 2026-07-18 (Steam content_log analysis)

Read Steam's OWN `~/Library/Application Support/Steam/logs/content_log.txt` from a real Steam download of the same depot (1460472) at 13:57 today. Decisive findings:

1. **Same 6 hosts, not AU/Sydney.** The real Steam client got the EXACT same content servers GameLib's `getContentServers` returns (3 Auckland `*-akl-*` SteamCache + akamai/fastly/alibaba global CDN). cellID=22 is correct. So the gap is NOT a different/better server list and NOT a NZ→AU routing difference.
2. **Steam uses ALL 6 in parallel → ~397 Mbps aggregate** (per-host stats: edgx 68, tpwr1 65, tpwr2 62, akamai 73, alibaba **118**, fastly 10 Mbps). THAT is the ~2.5x. Every host — including the ones that fail ~100% for GameLib — works and is fast for the real client. `alibaba` (HTTP-only, port 80) is Steam's FASTEST yet fails 0/258 for us.
3. **`AuthenticateDepotID (<depot>) - Success!` appears ONLY on `type=CDN` hosts, NEVER on SteamCache hosts** (consistent across Apr/May/Jul log entries). The CDN edges require a depot-authentication handshake before serving chunks.
4. **steam-user has NO `AuthenticateDepotID`** — grep of `node_modules/steam-user/components/*.js` finds only `getCDNAuthToken` (the `usetokenauth`-gated, effectively-deprecated token path that TIMED OUT for us in cycle-6). So GameLib cannot authenticate to the 3 CDN hosts → they reject its chunk requests (~100% fail) → GameLib is confined to the 2-3 local SteamCache edges, while Steam parallels all 6.

**This SUPERSEDES the cycle-8 "self-inflicted rate-limiting" hypothesis — DISPROVEN:** the content_log shows those CDN hosts are healthy and fast for the real client; they reject US specifically for lack of `AuthenticateDepotID`, not because of request volume. (The local SteamCache hosts' intermittent failures in later GameLib runs remain possibly transient/time-of-day — a separate, minor thread; the uncommitted cycle-9 `err=N{...}` status logging can still confirm whether CDN failures are 401/403 as expected.)

**Implication for the fix:** the achievable, committed path (cycle-7 resilience) makes the LOCAL-cache download robust but cannot unlock the CDN hosts. Closing the full 2.5x gap requires implementing Steam's CDN `AuthenticateDepotID` depot-auth flow (real protocol reverse-engineering — SteamKit2/DepotDownloader implement it; steam-user does not). OPEN DECISION (user was clearing context, unanswered): (a) short capture to confirm CDN 401/403 then research-spike the AuthenticateDepotID flow; (b) go straight to that spike; (c) PARK CDN-parallelism as a known larger effort and ship cycle-7's local-cache resilience.

## RESEARCH SPIKE — CDN AuthenticateDepotID feasibility (2026-07-18, read-only, no source changes)

**Status of this spike:** answers the 4 research tasks from the open decision above. NO source files were touched. Session status remains `fix_committed_hardware_verification_parked` — this is documentation only, not a resolution, not a new fix cycle.

### Task 1 — What is `AuthenticateDepotID` at the protocol level?

Answer: **(a) — a CDN auth token (the same `getCDNAuthToken` concept steam-user already partially implements), appended to chunk/manifest requests.** It is NOT a separate HTTP handshake endpoint on the CDN host itself, and NOT tied to `getDepotDecryptionKey` (that key decrypts chunk *content* after download; the auth token only *authorizes the HTTP request* to a given CDN host for a given depot).

Confirmed by directly reading the installed `steam-user@5.3.0` source (`node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn.js`, read fully per the objective):
- `getCDNAuthToken(appID, depotID, hostname, callback)` (cdn.js:144-165) sends `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken` (`k_EMsgClientGetCDNAuthToken = 5546`, `node_modules/steam-user/protobufs/enums_clientserver.proto:1059`) and resolves `{ token, expires }` from `CMsgClientGetCDNAuthTokenResponse` (`body.token`, `body.expiration_time`).
- The ONLY two call sites of that token anywhere in cdn.js — `getRawManifest` (line ~251) and `downloadChunk` (line ~337) — both append the resolved token **directly to the URL with no separator inserted by the caller**: `` `${urlBase}/depot/${depotID}/chunk/${chunkSha1}${token}` ``. This only works if `body.token` itself already carries a leading `?`/`&` (a full query-string fragment) — confirming the token is a URL-appended authorization string, not a header, not a cookie, not a second handshake request.
- Both call sites gate the token fetch on `if (contentServer.usetokenauth == 1)` — i.e. steam-user's OWN reference implementation treats this as a **per-content-server, directory-advertised opt-in flag**, not something requested unconditionally for every host (this exactly matches this session's own cycle-6/cycle-7 history: cycle 6 fetched it unconditionally and broke the app; cycle 7 correctly reverted to gating on `usetokenauth`).
- Nothing in cdn.js ever does a THIRD request (no `/authdepot/`-style endpoint call to the CDN host over HTTP) — the "auth" is entirely a CM-side token fetch, then a client-side URL append. Ruled out: (b) separate CDN-host HTTP handshake, (c) tied to decryption key, (d) no-CM-needed.

### Task 2 — Reference implementation cross-check (SteamKit2 / DepotDownloader)

Confirmed via web research (SteamKit2 GitHub source + changes.txt, DepotDownloader's `Steam3Session.cs`/`ContentDownloader.cs`):

- **Modern real clients (and modern SteamKit2) do NOT use the old `SteamApps.GetCDNAuthToken` / legacy `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken` message steam-user implements.** SteamKit2's own changelog states plainly: *"`SteamApps.GetCDNAuthToken` was moved to `SteamContent.GetCDNAuthToken` due to a Steam change"* and *"a new `SteamContent` handler was added"* — i.e. Valve relocated this capability off the old handler at some point after steam-user's `cdn.js` was written, and steam-user's implementation was never updated to follow.
- DepotDownloader's `Steam3Session.cs` calls `steamContent.GetCDNAuthToken(appid, depotid, server.Host)` and caches the result in a `ConcurrentDictionary<(uint depotId, string host), ...>` — **the exact same per-(depotId, host) granularity** steam-user's own internal `_contentServerTokens[depotID + '_' + hostname]` cache already assumes, and the exact same granularity this session's own `depot/cdnAuth.ts` `CdnAuthTokenCache` (cycle 6/7) was already built around. No redesign of the cache key is needed.
- SteamKit2's `CDNClient.AuthenticateDepot`/`AuthenticateDepotAsync` (the `Steam3.CDNClient`/legacy fork's "authdepot" HTTP-POST-with-encrypted-app-ticket flow found in older forks) is a **different, older, pre-SteamPipe mechanism** for the legacy binary "Content Server" (CS) protocol — not the modern SteamPipe HTTP CDN (`*.steamcontent.com`/`steampipe.akamaized.net`/Alibaba edges) this session is downloading from. This is a documented dead end for this investigation: it explains why the *name* "AuthenticateDepot" exists in SteamKit2's history, but it is not the mechanism the real Steam client's `content_log.txt` "AuthenticateDepotID... Success!" line is using for a SteamPipe download in 2026. The real client's per-CDN-host token flow is the modern `SteamContent`/`ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken` unified RPC (task 3 confirms this directly against the installed dependency's own bundled protobufs).

### Task 3 — The cycle-6 contradiction, resolved

**Confirmed with direct evidence from the installed dependency itself** (`node_modules/steam-user/protobufs/steammessages_contentsystem.steamclient.proto`, grepped and read fully): steam-user@5.3.0 ALREADY bundles the modern protobuf schema for exactly this operation, registered on a unified RPC service, sitting unused right next to the sibling call steam-user's own `getManifestRequestCode` already correctly uses:

```proto
service ContentServerDirectory {
	...
	rpc GetManifestRequestCode (...) returns (...);   // steam-user's cdn.js DOES call this, via _sendUnified — WORKS
	rpc GetCDNAuthToken (.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request)
	                returns (.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Response);  // steam-user's cdn.js does NOT call this — dead code path
	...
}

message CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request {
	optional uint32 depot_id = 1;
	optional string host_name = 2;
	optional uint32 app_id = 3;
}
message CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Response {
	optional string token = 1;
	optional uint32 expiration_time = 2;
}
```

This is an **exact structural match** for what `getCDNAuthToken`'s existing (broken/legacy) implementation already expects (`body.token` / `body.expiration_time` — identical field names to the old `CMsgClientGetCDNAuthTokenResponse`), and the request fields (`depot_id`, `host_name`, `app_id`) are identical in spirit to what steam-user already sends via the old EMsg.

**The contradiction is fully reconciled:** steam-user's public `getCDNAuthToken()` method sends the OLD `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken` (5546) — a legacy, pre-"SteamContent-handler-move" message. Per SteamKit2's own changelog, Valve moved this capability to a new unified service (`SteamContent`/`ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken`) at some point; the old EMsg is either unanswered or unreliable for modern SteamPipe content, which is exactly consistent with this session's cycle-6 observation (`getCDNAuthToken` timing out) and with steam-user's own maintainer comment inside `getContentServers`'s reduction (`//usetokenauth: '1'`, commented out — "I'm not sure that any servers use token auth anymore," a comment written from the perspective of the *old* mechanism, not the new one). The real Steam client's `AuthenticateDepotID... Success!` in `content_log.txt` reflects it successfully calling the *modern* unified RPC — the same one steam-user already bundles the compiled protobuf schema for (via its `getManifestRequestCode`-adjacent sibling), but never wired up for CDN auth tokens. This is NOT option (d) ("no CM needed") — it still requires exactly one CM round-trip, just via `_sendUnified(...)` (`EMsg.ServiceMethodCallFromClient`) instead of the legacy `_send(EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken, ...)`.

Confirmed `_sendUnified(methodName, methodData, callback)` (`node_modules/steam-user/components/03-messages.js:726`) is a **plain, non-private prototype method** already called dozens of times elsewhere in steam-user's own codebase (e.g. every method in `chatroom.js`) and by steam-user's own `getManifestRequestCode`/`getContentServers`-adjacent code — fully callable externally on a connected `SteamUser` client instance without any fork.

### Task 4 — Feasibility verdict

**VERDICT: (i) IMPLEMENTABLE, without forking steam-user.** This is a small, additive change — not SteamKit-level protocol reimplementation — because steam-user already ships everything except one connecting line of code.

**Outline of the exact call/flow to add:**
1. In `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/cdnAuth.ts` (already exists, built cycle 6/7, currently dormant/`usetokenauth`-gated), replace the token-FETCH transport inside `CdnAuthTokenCache`'s underlying `getCDNAuthToken` call — instead of calling steam-user's dead public `client.getCDNAuthToken(appID, depotID, hostname)`, call the client's own `_sendUnified` directly:
   ```ts
   client._sendUnified('ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1',
     { app_id: appId, depot_id: depotId, host_name: hostname },
     (body, hdr) => {
       // same eresult-check + { token, expires: new Date(body.expiration_time*1000) } shape
       // steam-user's OWN getManifestRequestCode already uses this exact pattern
     });
   ```
   This mirrors steam-user's own (working) `getManifestRequestCode` implementation line-for-line, just swapping the unified method name and request/response field names — both already verified against the installed dependency's own bundled `.proto`/generated JSON.
2. Everything downstream is ALREADY BUILT and does not need to change: the per-(depotId,host) cache, proactive/reactive refresh, concurrent-fetch coalescing, graceful-degradation-on-failure, redacted logging (`cdnAuth.ts`, cycle 6), and the `fetchChunk`/`hostMeta` URL-append plumbing (`decompress.ts`, cycle 7) that already knows how to append a token to a chunk URL when `usetokenauth` is set.
3. **One additional, currently-unproven change is likely needed**: this session's own captured directory RAW dumps (cycle 4/5) showed `usetokenauth` absent/unset for every host observed in this region, including the `type=CDN` hosts (alibaba/akamai/fastly) — matching steam-user's own comment that no server has actually advertised `usetokenauth=1` in practice. But `content_log.txt`'s own evidence (this session's prior finding) shows the real client's `AuthenticateDepotID` fires **on every `type=CDN` host, unconditionally** — not gated on a directory flag that appears to never be set. This suggests cycle 7's existing `fetchChunk` gate (`meta?.usetokenauth && cdnAuth`) should likely be widened to `(meta?.type === 'CDN' || meta?.usetokenauth) && cdnAuth` — i.e. gate on host **type**, not the (apparently-always-false) `usetokenauth` flag. `hostMeta`'s side-channel map (cycle 7) already threads `httpsSupport`/`usetokenauth` per host from the same raw directory response; adding `type` alongside them is the same low-risk, additive pattern, not a redesign.

**Effort estimate:** LOW-MODERATE (roughly comparable to one of this session's own cycles 5-7, each ~1 day of focused work: cycle 6/7 already did the hard, novel parts — cache design, URL-append plumbing, security-redacted logging, test coverage). Remaining work is: (a) ~20-30 lines swapping the transport inside `cdnAuth.ts`, (b) a ~2-line gate-condition change in `decompress.ts`/`depot.ts` to key off `type` instead of `usetokenauth`, (c) unit tests mirroring the existing `cdnAuth.test.ts`/`depotPrimitives.test.ts` patterns (mocking `_sendUnified` instead of `client.getCDNAuthToken`), (d) one short hardware capture (cycle 9's `err=<N>{<reason>:<count>}` breakdown is already in place, uncommitted, and would directly show whether the type=CDN hosts move from `403`/timeouts to `ok>0` once a real token is attached).

**Confidence:** MEDIUM-HIGH that this closes the identified mechanism gap — the evidence is unusually direct for a "spike": the exact request/response protobuf is already compiled into the installed dependency, the exact working call pattern already exists in the same file (`getManifestRequestCode`), and two independent authoritative sources (SteamKit2's own changelog, DepotDownloader's real source) confirm modern Steam moved this capability to precisely the unified RPC steam-user already bundles but never wired up. Confidence is not HIGH (rather than certain) because of three unverified assumptions: (1) `_sendUnified` will actually resolve (not reject/timeout) for `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken` specifically — no reason to expect otherwise given it resolves fine for the sibling `GetManifestRequestCode` call in the exact same session, but unconfirmed until tried; (2) the `usetokenauth`→`type` gate-condition change (item 3 above) is inferred from `content_log.txt`'s behavior pattern, not yet re-derived from a fresh directory capture cross-referenced against a live token fetch; (3) the token, once obtained, might require more than a bare URL append for these specific global CDN edges (e.g. a cookie) — steam-user's own uniform URL-append convention is the best available evidence against this, but it's not independently confirmed for Akamai/Fastly/Alibaba specifically.

**Top risks:**
- CM-side ACL/version-gating on `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken` could reject calls from a client identifying as this codebase's steam-user session (unlikely — no evidence of this in either source, but not something a spike can rule out without trying).
- The `usetokenauth`→`type`-based gate gets the *trigger* right but the token could still fail to unlock a given CDN host if that specific edge additionally rate-limits or blocks based on other signals (region, request cadence) — this would look like partial success (token acquired, chunks still fail) and would need cycle 9's `reason` breakdown to distinguish from an outright wiring bug.
- If Alibaba/Akamai/Fastly turn out to need per-request re-authentication (not the 1-hour-cacheable token this session's `cdnAuth.ts` assumes), the existing proactive-refresh design would need retuning — a small, bounded follow-up, not a redesign.

**Recommended next step:** Do NOT go straight to a SteamKit-level protocol reimplementation (option (b) in the prior open decision) — the evidence gathered this spike shows that's unnecessary. Instead: (1) implement the `_sendUnified`-based transport swap in `cdnAuth.ts` (small, self-contained, no fork); (2) change the `fetchChunk`/`hostMeta` gate from `usetokenauth`-only to `type==='CDN' || usetokenauth`; (3) run one short hardware capture with cycle 9's already-uncommitted `err=<N>{<reason>:<count>}` diagnostic logging in place to directly observe whether the type=CDN hosts move from nomm-token failures to `ok>0`. This is a bounded, high-leverage follow-up fix cycle (cycle 10), not a multi-day protocol-research effort — steam-user already did the hard part (bundling the schema); it just never got wired up.

## Cycle 10 (2026-07-18) — IMPLEMENTATION: modern CDN-auth RPC wired up, gate widened to type=CDN, cycle-9 reason-extraction hardened

**Implements the RESEARCH SPIKE's recommended next step exactly (all 4 parts of the implementation directive).** Builds on committed cycle-7/8 (33c108a0) + uncommitted cycle-9 diagnostic. UNCOMMITTED — this changes real runtime behavior (fetches tokens for type=CDN hosts, none of which happened before) and per the objective must not be committed until a hardware validation run confirms it.

### What changed

**PART 1 — Transport swap (`depot/cdnAuth.ts`):** `CdnAuthTokenCache` no longer calls steam-user's public `getCDNAuthToken()` (legacy `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken`, moved server-side, goes unanswered — the cycle-6 regression). It now calls the client's own `_sendUnified` directly:

```ts
client._sendUnified(
  'ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1',
  { app_id: this.appId, depot_id: Number(depotId), host_name: host },
  (body, hdr) => {
    const eresult = hdr?.proto?.eresult   // EResult.OK === 1
    if (eresult !== undefined && eresult !== 1) { /* reject */ }
    if (!body?.token) { /* reject */ }
    // resolve({ token: body.token, expiresAt: body.expiration_time * 1000 })
  }
)
```

This mirrors steam-user's own (working) `getManifestRequestCode` `_sendUnified` call pattern (`node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn.js:281-303`) line-for-line — same method-name shape, same `(body, hdr) => check hdr.proto.eresult` structure. Method name (`ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1`) and request/response field names (`app_id`/`depot_id`/`host_name` -> `token`/`expiration_time`) were verified directly against the installed `steam-user@5.3.0`'s bundled `steammessages_contentsystem.steamclient.proto` (confirmed in the RESEARCH SPIKE, re-confirmed again this cycle before wiring — Pitfall 5 discipline). `expiration_time` (epoch seconds) maps to `expiresAt` (epoch ms) via `* 1000`, falling back to "already expired" (never trusted/cached) if missing or `<= 0`. `SteamUserDepotExtras` (depot.ts) gained the verified `_sendUnified` declaration, replacing the now-dead `getCDNAuthToken` declaration.

**PART 2 — Widened the token gate (`depot/decompress.ts` + `depot.ts`):** `ContentServerHostMeta` gained a `type?: string` field (`reduceContentServers` already sees `type` in the raw directory response — RawContentServer already declared it, cycle 4 — it just wasn't threaded into hostMeta before). New exported pure function `wantsCdnAuthToken(meta)` returns `meta?.usetokenauth === true || meta?.type === 'CDN'`, used at both of fetchChunk's use sites (the token-fetch call and the 401/403 invalidate gate) so they can never drift out of lockstep. This directly targets the RESEARCH SPIKE's own finding: this codebase's directory captures never see `usetokenauth` populated (steam-user's own `cdn.js` has it hard-coded commented out), but Steam's `content_log.txt` shows the real client's `AuthenticateDepotID` firing on every `type=CDN` host UNCONDITIONALLY. `type=SteamCache` hosts still never match either branch and stay token-less, exactly as they are for the real client. The `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` log's worst-hosts line now also renders `type=<CDN|SteamCache>` per host for direct hardware-run confirmation alongside the existing `scheme=`/`usetokenauth` fields.

**PART 3 — Regression guard (`depot/cdnAuth.ts`), the most important property this cycle adds:** `_sendUnified` itself has NO built-in timeout (unlike steam-user's public `getCDNAuthToken` wrapper, which the transport swap in PART 1 stopped calling entirely) — this cache now owns the timeout budget end-to-end via three layers, all additive to the existing per-depot+host cache/coalescing/graceful-degradation cycle 6 already built:
  1. **Bounded per-fetch timeout** — new `CDN_AUTH_TOKEN_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 3000` (a few seconds, not steam-user's own 10s+ wrapper, and well under fetchChunk's own `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000`). The CM round-trip races against a local `setTimeout`; whichever settles first wins, and a late real callback firing after the timeout already gave up is silently ignored (no double-resolve, no crash).
  2. **Negative-cache cooldown per (depot,host)** — new `CDN_AUTH_TOKEN_FAILURE_COOLDOWN_MS = 60000`. A failed or timed-out fetch for a key starts a cooldown; every other concurrent/subsequent `getToken()` call for that EXACT key short-circuits to `''` immediately with **zero network attempts** until the cooldown elapses — so a persistently-unreachable token endpoint can never re-pay the bounded timeout on every single chunk that lands on it across a large install.
  3. **Immediate token-less degradation** — `getToken()` NEVER throws; a failure/timeout always resolves to `''`, letting fetchChunk's existing retry/host-rotation/hostHealth mechanism (which then sees that host 403 and deprioritizes it) decide the outcome, exactly as cycle 6 already established.
  Concurrent callers for the SAME depot+host during an in-flight fetch remain coalesced onto one round-trip (cycle 6's existing `pending` map, untouched).

  **Test proving the guard is airtight** (`cdnAuth.test.ts`'s "PART 3" describe block + a dedicated `depotPrimitives.test.ts` fetchChunk-level test): a `_sendUnified` mock that NEVER calls back is exercised with `jest.useFakeTimers()` — advancing to `CDN_AUTH_TOKEN_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS - 1` leaves the promise unsettled; advancing past it resolves to `''`. A separate test proves a hanging fetch for `stuck-host` never blocks a concurrent `getToken()` for a different host. A separate test proves 20 consecutive calls during the cooldown window make exactly 1 total network attempt. A `depotPrimitives.test.ts` test proves a hanging token fetch degrades and the WHOLE chunk download (not just the cache in isolation) still completes once the bounded timeout fires, well under `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`.

**PART 4 — Hardened cycle-9's reason-extraction (`depot/decompress.ts`):** `attemptFailureReason` now checks a duck-typed `typeof err.status === 'number'` FIRST, instead of `err instanceof ChunkHttpError`. `ChunkHttpError` still sets `status` as a plain instance property, so every existing throw site is unaffected — but the check is now immune to any class-identity mismatch that an `instanceof` check could fail on (a real risk once code crosses a bundle/module boundary), which is the exact failure mode a previous hardware run's generic `err=N{Error:N}` breakdown pointed to instead of the real HTTP status. Added a dedicated regression test (`depotPrimitives.test.ts`) that rejects with a plain, non-Error, non-`ChunkHttpError` object carrying only a `status` field and asserts `reason` is the numeric status, not the fallback `name` — proving the fix works independent of any class identity at all.

### Self-verification (cycle 10, 2026-07-18)

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Steam-specific suites: 16 suites, 640/640 pass (515 cycle-9 baseline + 125 net-new/updated across `cdnAuth.test.ts` [full rewrite: `_sendUnified`-based mock, method-name/field assertions, expiration_time->expiresAt mapping, PART 3's timeout/cooldown/no-cross-host-block/late-callback-ignored tests], `depotPrimitives.test.ts` [`makeFakeCdnClient`/two other client literals swapped to `_sendUnified`; new type=CDN-gate-fires test; new type=SteamCache-stays-token-less test; new PART-3 fetchChunk-level no-stall test; new PART-4 duck-typed-reason test], `depot.test.ts` [`makeFakeClient`'s `_sendUnified` fake; cycle-6 test's assertions updated to the modern RPC method name/fields; `reduceContentServers`'s existing `toEqual` fixed to include the new `type` field; new dedicated `type` threading test]). Full suite: 82 suites, 1536/1536 tests pass (1525 cycle-9 baseline + 11 net-new). The pre-existing, unrelated `readAcfState`/`pollInstallOnce` leaked-timer teardown warning fires identically to every prior cycle's confirmation — not introduced by this cycle. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression (untouched, confirmed via `git diff --stat` against those directories — empty). No change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched). No change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate. No change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard. The sha1 chunk-integrity gate (T-21-03, `decodeChunk`) completely untouched — this cycle only changes how/whether a token is fetched and how a failure reason is classified, never the decrypt/decompress/verify path. `hostHealth.ts`/`stallTracker.ts` byte-for-byte untouched (confirmed via `git diff --stat` — empty).

### Deliberately NOT changed this cycle

`hostHealth.ts`'s scoring/circuit-breaker/weightedload-prior logic (cycles 3/5); `fetchChunk`'s attempt-count/backoff/timeout mechanics (cycles 1/3, `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`/`CHUNK_FETCH_MAX_BACKOFF_MS` unchanged); URL-scheme parity (cycle 7, unchanged); `StallTracker` re-queue logic (cycle 7, unchanged); `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` -> whole-install `status:'error'` escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; the token-append VERBATIM convention (cycle 6, unchanged — the modern RPC's `token` field is still assumed to carry its own leading `?`, consistent with steam-user's own usage of the concept elsewhere; unconfirmed until a hardware run actually receives a real token from the modern RPC, since no request has ever reached it before this cycle).

### NOT yet verified

The actual real-world effect of calling the modern RPC against Steam's live CM — whether `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1` actually resolves (not rejects/times out) for a real session, whether the returned token format matches the verbatim-append assumption, and whether the type=CDN hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) flip from ~100% failure to `ok>0` once authenticated — all require a fresh hardware reproduction. Per the objective, this cycle's changes are **UNCOMMITTED**, pending that hardware validation run. Acceptance criteria for that run: (a) `[Timing] CDN auth token acquired` lines appear for type=CDN hosts (confirms the modern RPC resolves); (b) the `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` log's `type=CDN` hosts show `ok>0` instead of near-100% failure; (c) `type=SteamCache` hosts are unaffected (no token fetch attempted, still succeed as before); (d) aggregate throughput rises toward the real client's observed ~397 Mbps / parallel-multi-host pattern (content_log.txt finding); (e) if the token round-trip itself fails/times out, chunk downloads must NOT stall — the log should show at most one bounded-timeout wait per (depot,host) before the cooldown silences further attempts. Session status remains PARKED (not resolved) — do NOT mark this session resolved until that hardware run confirms the fix.

## Cycle 10 gap-closure attempt (2026-07-19) — ROOT-CAUSED the hardware TypeError, STOP-for-decision returned, no code changed

**Trigger:** cycle-10's uncommitted build ran on hardware and threw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'encode')` on every type=CDN host's token fetch. PART-3's fail-fast guard (a synchronous throw inside a `new Promise` executor auto-converts to a rejection — no timeout even needed to elapse) caught it and degraded to `''` every time; the download did NOT stall, confirming PART-3 works exactly as designed. This cycle's job was ONLY to find the correct method-name string and fix the RPC call.

### Evidence (cycle 10 gap-closure, 2026-07-19)

- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`node_modules/steam-user/components/03-messages.js:726-736`, `_sendUnified`): confirmed the exact mechanism — `let Proto = protobufs[methodName + (callback ? '_Request' : '')]; ... this._send(header, SteamUserMessages._encodeProto(Proto, methodData), callback)`, and `_encodeProto(proto, data) { return proto.encode(data).finish() }` (line 349-351). `Proto` for `methodName='ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1'` is `protobufs['ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1_Request']`. found: this key is `undefined` in the installed module (see next entry) → `undefined.encode` → the exact hardware TypeError, and it throws SYNCHRONOUSLY, before any network I/O — `_sendUnified` never even reaches `_send`/`this._connection.send(...)`. implication: no request was ever transmitted on any hardware run to date; the RPC has never actually been attempted against Steam's CM.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — action: wrote a throwaway node script extracting every `protobufs[...]` key literal from `node_modules/steam-user/components/03-messages.js` via regex (`/protobufs\[(EMsg\.\w+|'[^']+')\]/g`) — 294 total registered keys (this mirrors, and is more reliable than, `Object.keys(protobufs)` since `protobufs` itself is a private, unexported module-closure variable with no public accessor). found: filtering for `/CDN|ContentServerDirectory/i` yields exactly 4 entries: `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken`, `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthTokenResponse` (the LEGACY EMsg-based mechanism), and `'ContentServerDirectory.GetManifestRequestCode#1_Request'`/`'_Response'` (the sibling call, registered and working). **`'ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1_Request'` and `'...#1_Response'` are registered under NEITHER this nor any other service name anywhere in the file.** implication: this is not a wrong-string bug — the RESEARCH SPIKE's Task 3/4 assumption ("steam-user already bundles everything except one connecting line of code") is DISPROVEN for the encode/decode wiring specifically, even though the method-name STRING itself (`ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1`) is verified correct against the `.proto`.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`node_modules/steam-user/protobufs/steammessages_contentsystem.steamclient.proto:103-113`): confirmed the `.proto` DOES declare `service ContentServerDirectory { ... rpc GetCDNAuthToken (.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request) returns (.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Response); ... }` with the exact request (`depot_id`, `host_name`, `app_id`) and response (`token`, `expiration_time`) fields cycle-10's code already uses. found via a node script: `require('steam-user/protobufs/generated/_load.js').CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request`/`_Response` are real, fully compiled, encode/decode-capable protobufjs message classes (`typeof ... === 'object'`, `.encode`/`.decode` are functions) — the schema IS bundled in `steam-user@5.3.0`. implication: the message definitions exist and are usable; only the `protobufs` LOOKUP MAP that `_sendUnified`'s encode path (and, separately, `_handleMessage`'s response-decode path at line 611-614, `if (protobufs[handlerName]) { body = _decodeProto(...) }`) both depend on was never populated for this specific RPC by steam-user's maintainer. Both directions (request encode AND response decode) are affected — even a corrected method-name string alone would still throw on encode, and even a manually-pre-encoded request would arrive back as a raw undecoded buffer on the response side (line 612's `protobufs[handlerName]` check silently skips decoding, no throw, `body` stays an undecoded `ByteBuffer`/`Buffer`).
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn.js:144-165`, the LEGACY `getCDNAuthToken()`): confirmed it calls `this._send(EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken, {...}, callback)`, and `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken`/`EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthTokenResponse` ARE both registered in the protobufs map (lines 122-123) — so the legacy path's JS-level encode/decode wiring is fully intact and functional; it is Valve's SERVER that no longer reliably answers this legacy EMsg for modern SteamPipe content (per the RESEARCH SPIKE's SteamKit2-changelog citation: the capability moved server-side), not a client-side wiring defect. This sharpens the RESEARCH SPIKE's finding: the legacy mechanism is JS-complete-but-server-abandoned; the modern mechanism is server-live-but-JS-incomplete (missing map entries, confirmed above) — cycle 6's timeout and cycle 10's TypeError are two different failure modes with two different root causes, both ultimately traceable to steam-user@5.3.0 never having been updated for Valve's post-changelog handler move.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`node_modules/steam-user/components/03-messages.js:419-491`, `_send`; line 640-646, the `_jobs`/`jobCallback` response-dispatch path): traced the full transport `_sendUnified` itself uses — `_send(header, preEncodedBuffer, callback)` stores `callback` in `this._jobs` keyed by a new `jobIdSource`; on response, `_handleMessage` looks up that stored callback and invokes `jobCallback.call(this, body, header, cb)` where `body` is either the auto-decoded object (if `protobufs[handlerName]` exists) or the RAW undecoded `ByteBuffer`/`Buffer` (if not, silently, no error). Also confirmed `EMsg.ServiceMethodCallFromClient` itself is NOT registered in the protobufs map, so `_send`'s OWN internal encode step (line 435, keyed by numeric EMsg) is a no-op for this message type — it only passes through an already-encoded buffer, exactly as `_sendUnified` supplies it. implication: a manual bypass (skip `_sendUnified`, encode the request directly via the compiled `Schema.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request` class, call `client._send(header, encodedBuffer, callback)` directly, and manually decode the raw response body via `Schema.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Response.decode(...)` inside the callback) is technically viable and would not double-encode or corrupt the transport — but it requires reaching into `_send` (an undocumented, `@protected`-by-convention method) and replicating `_decodeProto`'s default-value-normalization logic (lines 360-410) ourselves for correct field semantics. This is a materially larger and riskier change than "swap a method-name string."
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/cdnAuth.ts:282-332`, `callGetCDNAuthToken`): confirmed `client._sendUnified(...)` is called synchronously inside the `new Promise((resolvePromise, reject) => {...})` executor (no try/catch needed) — per JS spec, a synchronous throw inside a Promise executor auto-rejects the promise. found: this is exactly why PART 3's guard caught the TypeError instantly (faster than its own 3s timeout even had to fire) and `fetch()`'s catch block (line 252 in `cdnAuth.ts`) degraded to `''` and started the 60s cooldown, matching the objective's own hardware observation ("download did NOT stall") precisely. implication: PART 3 is confirmed airtight against this exact failure mode, on real hardware, not just in tests — no change needed to PART 3.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts:196-203`, `attemptFailureReason`, PART 4): re-read the current (already-hardened, from cycle 10) implementation — duck-typed `typeof e?.status === 'number'` check first, matching `ChunkHttpError`'s plain `status` instance property. found: this ALREADY fixes the generic `{Error:N}` symptom (the objective's secondary note) — that symptom's likely cause was the PRE-cycle-10 `err instanceof ChunkHttpError` check (cycle 9) failing on a class-identity mismatch; cycle 10's duck-typed rewrite (with its own dedicated regression test using a plain non-Error object carrying only `.status`) is not itself broken and needs no further change. Also confirmed `cdnAuth.getToken()` never throws (always resolves, even on internal failure — see cdnAuth.ts's own design), so a token-fetch failure cannot itself be the source of a bare `{Error:N}` chunk-attempt entry; any recurrence on the NEXT hardware run (post RPC-fix) would need a fresh raw-error capture to diagnose, but no action is warranted now.

### Root cause (confirmed, this cycle)

Cycle-10's `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1` method-name string is **correct** per the installed dependency's own `.proto` (request fields `app_id`/`depot_id`/`host_name`, response fields `token`/`expiration_time` all verified byte-for-byte against `steammessages_contentsystem.steamclient.proto`). The bug is NOT a wrong string. The bug is that **steam-user@5.3.0 never wired this specific RPC's compiled message classes into its internal `protobufs` encode/decode lookup map**, for either direction — `_sendUnified`'s encode step and `_handleMessage`'s response-decode step BOTH depend on that same private map, and BOTH are unpopulated for this RPC (confirmed via exhaustive enumeration of all 294 registered keys: only the sibling `GetManifestRequestCode` and the unrelated legacy `EMsg.ClientGetCDNAuthToken*` are present). This exactly matches the constraint's own anticipated STOP condition: "If it exists under NO service name, STOP and report for a decision — do not force a broken call." There is no different, correct method-name string to substitute — the string cycle-10 used IS the right one; steam-user simply never finished wiring it up.

### Decision needed (STOP, per constraint — no code changed this cycle)

Two viable paths, neither implemented (awaiting decision):

- **Option A — Fall back (LOW risk, immediately actionable):** revert cycle-10 PART 1 (transport swap) and PART 2 (gate widened to `type==='CDN'`) back to cycle-7/8's hardware-verified baseline (`usetokenauth`-gated only, which per this session's own directory captures is never true in this region — so `cdnAuth.ts` reverts to dormant/inert, exactly as it was through cycles 7-9). Keep cycle-9/10's PART 4 reason-extraction hardening (pure observability, no behavior change, no risk). This PARKS the type=CDN 2.5x throughput gap as a known, larger effort (library-level, not a quick fix) and returns to the last state that WAS hardware-verified not to regress anything. No new hardware run strictly required to re-validate (though one is still advisable given cycle-9's diagnostic additions).
- **Option B — Implement the manual bypass (MODERATE risk, more effort):** keep PART 1's intent but replace the `_sendUnified` call with a manual encode (via `Schema.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request.encode(data).finish()`, requiring `steam-user/protobufs/generated/_load.js` directly) + a direct `client._send(header, encodedBuffer, callback)` call (bypassing only the missing-map-entry problem, using the exact same transport `_sendUnified` itself delegates to) + a manual response decode (`Schema.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Response.decode(rawBody)` inside the callback, handling both `ByteBuffer` and `Buffer` shapes). This reaches into steam-user's undocumented `_send`/`_jobs` internals (still a plain prototype method, not privately enforced, but marked `@protected` and unexported by the library's public API) and must replicate `_decodeProto`'s default-value semantics for `token`/`expiration_time` to be safe. Needs its own dedicated test coverage (mocking `_send`+`_jobs` instead of `_sendUnified`) and is a genuinely new, unverified code path — no sibling call in this codebase or in steam-user's own source already does this exact bypass, unlike PART 1's original (now-disproven) assumption that this mirrored `getManifestRequestCode` line-for-line.

**No code changed. cycle-10's files remain exactly as they were (still uncommitted, still contain the TypeError-triggering call) pending this decision.** Self-verification (tsc/jest) was not re-run since nothing changed since cycle-10's own self-verification (tsc clean; 640/640 steam suites; 1536/1536 full suite — see cycle 10 above).

## DECISION 2026-07-19: OPTION B (user-chosen) — implement the manual `_send` bypass

next_action (cycle 11): In `depot/cdnAuth.ts`, replace the broken `client._sendUnified('ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1', ...)` call inside `callGetCDNAuthToken` with a MANUAL encode/send/decode that bypasses steam-user's missing `_sendUnified` map entry (the compiled classes exist; only the lookup registration is missing):
1. Load the compiled request/response classes: `Schema.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request` / `_Response` (require steam-user's `protobufs/generated/_load.js` — confirm exact export path; the throwaway node probe already proved these classes exist with real `.encode`/`.decode`).
2. Encode: `const buf = Schema.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request.encode({ app_id, depot_id, host_name }).finish()`.
3. Send via the SAME transport `_sendUnified` delegates to: `client._send({ msg: EMsg.ServiceMethodCallFromClient, proto: { target_job_name: 'ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1' } }, buf, callback)`. (Confirm no double-encode — `_send` takes an already-encoded Buffer here.)
4. In the callback, decode: `Schema.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Response.decode(rawBody)` — handle both ByteBuffer and Buffer shapes; replicate `_decodeProto`'s default-value semantics for `token`/`expiration_time`; check `hdr.proto.eresult === 1`.
KEEP intact: PART-3 fail-fast guard (3s timeout + 60s negative-cache + degrade-to-'' + never-block — PROVEN airtight on the real hardware TypeError), PART-2 `type==='CDN'` gate, PART-4 status reason. Add dedicated tests mocking `_send`+`_jobs` (not `_sendUnified`). Then tsc + steam suites + full jest green, leave UNCOMMITTED, and request a hardware re-validation (relaunch → user Install → confirm `CDN auth token acquired` lines appear and akamai/fastly/alibaba flip err/403 → ok>0 with parallel multi-host throughput toward ~397 Mbps). Risk is bounded by the fail-fast guard: worst case it degrades to token-less = current behavior. Consider filing an upstream steam-user issue/PR to register the proto (clean long-term fix) as a follow-up.

## Cycle 11 (2026-07-19) — IMPLEMENTATION: manual `_send` bypass (Option B), self-verified, UNCOMMITTED

**Implements "DECISION 2026-07-19: OPTION B" exactly.** Builds on committed cycle-7/8 (33c108a0). Cycle 9/10's uncommitted diagnostic/RPC-wiring files (`depot.ts`, `depot/decompress.ts`, `depot/cdnAuth.ts`) are the base this cycle edits further — `depot/decompress.ts` itself is UNTOUCHED this cycle (PART 4's reason-extraction hardening from cycle 10 stands as-is). UNCOMMITTED — per the objective, behavior-changing CDN-auth code is not committed until a hardware validation run confirms it.

### What changed

**`depot/cdnAuth.ts` — the manual bypass (PART 1 replacement):** `callGetCDNAuthToken` no longer calls `client._sendUnified('ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1', ...)` (proven broken — see "Cycle 10 gap-closure attempt" above). It now:
1. Loads the compiled `CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request`/`_Response` protobufjs message classes via a new `loadCdnAuthTokenSchema()` — confirmed by direct probe (`node -e "require('steam-user/protobufs/generated/_load.js')..."`) that these classes are exported directly off that module's `module.exports` object with real, callable `.encode`/`.decode`, and cached module-scope after first load (mirrors `depot.ts`'s `loadContentManifestParser` loud-throw-on-missing-shape discipline, but **synchronous** `require()`, not `await import()` — see below for why).
2. Encodes the request buffer synchronously: `schema.Request.encode({ app_id, depot_id, host_name }).finish()` — confirmed via probe to return a real `Buffer`.
3. Sends it via `client._send({ msg: 151 /* EMsg.ServiceMethodCallFromClient */, proto: { target_job_name: 'ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1' } }, requestBuffer, callback)` — the exact same lower-level transport `_sendUnified` itself delegates to (confirmed by reading `03-messages.js:726-736`'s own implementation). Confirmed no double-encode: `_send`'s own encode branch only fires when `protobufs[emsg]` resolves for the raw EMsg number, and `EMsg.ServiceMethodCallFromClient` is NOT in that map either (confirmed in cycle 10's gap-closure evidence) — a pre-encoded `Buffer` body passes through `_send` untouched.
4. Decodes the raw response manually in the callback via a new `decodeCdnAuthTokenResponse`/`normalizeResponseBuffer` pair: normalizes the raw body (a `Buffer` OR a `bytebuffer` `ByteBuffer` instance, since `_handleMessage`'s own auto-decode is skipped for this RPC's response too — same missing-map-entry root cause) via duck-typed `.toBuffer()` detection (never `instanceof`, PART 4 discipline), then `schema.Response.decode(buf)`. Verified via probe that protobufjs's raw `decode()` gives `''`/`0` for a missing `token`/`expiration_time` field (not `_decodeProto`'s own `null`-normalization) — documented in code as behavior-equivalent for this module's existing falsy-checks (`!body.token`, `body.expiration_time && ... > 0`), since `''`/`0`/`null` are all equally falsy.

**`EMsg.ServiceMethodCallFromClient` is a hardcoded local constant (`151`)**, not a value import of the real `'steam-user'` package — confirmed against `@types/steam-user`'s own `EMsg` enum AND against `_sendUnified`'s own header construction. Every other steam-user reference in this store manager is `import type` only; a value import here would force `depotPrimitives.test.ts`/`depot.test.ts` (which inject fakes and never mock the real `'steam-user'` package) to start mocking it.

**Synchronous schema loading, deliberately NOT `await import()`:** unlike `depot.ts`'s `loadContentManifestParser`, `loadCdnAuthTokenSchema()` uses a plain synchronous `require()` (with an eslint-disable for `@typescript-eslint/no-require-imports`, verified this is the actual configured rule name — the repo's own `jest.config.js` disables a *different*, stale rule name (`no-var-requires`) for its own `require`, which surfaced as an "unused eslint-disable directive" warning until corrected). This was a deliberate correctness decision, not a style shortcut: making `callGetCDNAuthToken` `async` and `await`-ing the schema load (the initially-drafted approach, mirroring `depot.ts`'s convention) pushes the real `client._send(...)` call at least one microtask tick later than the pre-cycle-11 `_sendUnified` call, which fired synchronously. Several EXISTING tests assume synchronous timing (the concurrent-coalescing test's `expect(client._send).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)` immediately after 3 sync `getToken()` calls with no `await`; several `jest.useFakeTimers()`+`advanceTimersByTimeAsync` timeout/cooldown tests). Rather than risk subtle, hard-to-diagnose async-interleaving flakiness across ~6 timer-sensitive tests for no functional benefit (the schema file is pure/side-effect-free and safe to load synchronously), `callGetCDNAuthToken` was kept structurally IDENTICAL to its pre-cycle-11 shape (`_send` still called synchronously inside the `new Promise(...)` executor) — confirmed this preserves every existing test's timing assumption byte-for-byte (see Self-verification below).

**`CDNAuthTokenClient` interface (`cdnAuth.ts`) and `SteamUserDepotExtras` interface (`depot.ts`)** both swapped their `_sendUnified(methodName, methodData, callback)` declaration for `_send(header, body: Buffer, callback)`, matching the real `client._send` signature this module now depends on.

**New ambient type declaration** `src/common/typedefs/steam-user-cdn-auth-schema.d.ts` — mirrors the existing `steam-user-content-manifest.d.ts` pattern exactly (undocumented steam-user internal path, Pitfall 5, satisfies `tsc` for the `require()`/dynamic-import path without claiming steam-user won't change this file).

**New shared test fixture** `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/fixtures/cdnAuthSendFixture.ts` (`makeFakeSendClient`) — replaces the retired `_sendUnified`-based fakes in `cdnAuth.test.ts`/`depotPrimitives.test.ts`. Decodes the REAL, already-encoded request `Buffer` cdnAuth.ts's manual bypass sends (via the same compiled protobuf classes) and hands back a REAL, encoded response `Buffer` through the callback — exercising the actual wire format end-to-end rather than a pass-through object fake, deliberately, since both prior wiring bugs (cycle 6's timeout, cycle 10's TypeError) slipped past tests that mocked the transport too abstractly to notice a real encode/decode mismatch.

**Test updates:** `cdnAuth.test.ts` and `depotPrimitives.test.ts` — every hand-rolled `_sendUnified`-based fake client rewritten to `_send`, encoding/decoding real protobuf buffers (via the new fixture or inline real-schema calls for the handful of tests needing hand-rolled callback-timing control: the concurrent-coalescing test, the late-callback test, the hanging-stuck-host test). `depot.test.ts`'s `makeFakeClient` default `_send` fake updated the same way; the dedicated "threads a REAL CdnAuthTokenCache" test now asserts on the real header (`{ msg: 151, proto: { target_job_name: '...' } }`) + an `expect.any(Buffer)` body, then separately decodes that captured buffer via the real `Request` class to assert on `app_id`/`depot_id`/`host_name` — since cdnAuth.ts no longer hands steam-user a plain object at all, only an already-encoded `Buffer`.

### Evidence (cycle 11, 2026-07-19)

- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — action: `node -e "require('steam-user/protobufs/generated/_load.js').CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request/_Response"` — confirmed both classes are real, directly accessible off `module.exports`, with working `.encode(data).finish()` (returns a real `Buffer`, confirmed `Buffer.isBuffer(buf) === true`) and `.decode(buf)` (returns an object with direct-property access to `depot_id`/`host_name`/`app_id` or `token`/`expiration_time`). found: the RESEARCH SPIKE's claim that these classes exist with real encode/decode (already established in cycle 10's gap-closure) is confirmed at the exact call shape cycle 11's manual bypass needs. implication: the manual bypass is directly implementable without any further reverse-engineering.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — action: `node -e "require('steam-user/protobufs/generated/_load.js').CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Response.decode(Response.encode({}).finish())"` — confirmed protobufjs's raw `decode()` on a message with no fields set returns `{ token: '', expiration_time: 0 }` via direct property access (default values applied via the generated class's prototype), NOT `_decodeProto`'s own `null`-for-missing-field normalization. found: since every consumer of this cache's decoded response only ever checks `token`/`expiration_time` for falsy-ness, `''`/`0` and `null` are behaviorally equivalent here — confirmed by re-reading `getToken`'s/`callGetCDNAuthToken`'s existing checks (`!body.token`, `body.expiration_time && ... > 0`). implication: no need to replicate `_decodeProto`'s full `replaceDefaults` machinery — a bare `schema.Response.decode(buf)` is sufficient and correct for this module's actual usage.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`node_modules/@types/steam-user/index.d.ts:12`, `export = SteamUser`; `node_modules/@types/steam-user/index.d.ts:2943`, `export enum EMsg`) + a live probe (`require('steam-user').EMsg.ServiceMethodCallFromClient === 151`): confirmed the numeric value directly, and confirmed the real package is CommonJS (`export =`) with `EMsg` merged onto the exported namespace. found: importing this as a VALUE (rather than the `import type` used everywhere else in this store manager) would pull the real `'steam-user'` package into every test file that transitively imports `cdnAuth.ts` — `depotPrimitives.test.ts`/`depot.test.ts` currently inject fakes and never mock the real package. implication: hardcoded `EMSG_SERVICE_METHOD_CALL_FROM_CLIENT = 151` as a local constant (with a citation comment) instead — zero new runtime dependency surface for any test file.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — action: compiled a throwaway probe (`tsc --module commonjs --esModuleInterop`) of `await import('steam-user/protobufs/generated/_load.js')` to inspect TS's actual emitted interop code (`__importStar`) — confirmed it copies every own key of the CJS module (except `default`) onto the returned namespace object AND sets `.default` to the raw `module.exports` — so both `mod.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request` and `mod.default.CContentServerDirectory_GetCDNAuthToken_Request` would have worked, had the async/`await import()` approach been kept. found: this was a due-diligence check on the ORIGINAL (later-reverted) async design before discovering the timing risk documented above; not load-bearing for the final synchronous `require()` implementation, but confirms the interop concern that motivated re-checking timing in the first place was well-founded (an extra async hop WAS present in that design).
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (existing tests, before any edits): `cdnAuth.test.ts`'s "concurrent callers... coalesced" test asserts `expect(client._sendUnified).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)` SYNCHRONOUSLY, immediately after three back-to-back `cache.getToken(...)` calls with no `await` in between; several PART-3 tests use `jest.useFakeTimers()` + `jest.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(...)` around the bounded-timeout/cooldown windows. found: the FIRST implementation attempt (async schema load via `await import(...)`) broke this synchronous assertion — `client._send` was not yet called by the time the assertion ran, since the dynamic import's `Promise.resolve().then(...)` interop wrapper requires at least one microtask tick before `require()` even executes. implication: switched `loadCdnAuthTokenSchema` to synchronous `require()` and reverted `callGetCDNAuthToken` to its pre-cycle-11 non-`async` structure (schema loaded synchronously, then `_send` called synchronously inside the `new Promise(...)` executor, exactly as `_sendUnified` did) — re-ran the full test file and confirmed all 19/19 tests pass with zero timing-related changes needed beyond the `_sendUnified` → `_send` rename and buffer encode/decode.

1. **SUCCESS — cycle-11's manual `_send` bypass fixed the cycle-10 TypeError. It is GONE.** The RPC now encodes/sends and the response callback ACTUALLY FIRES: the warning's own stack trace is `SteamUser.<anonymous> (cdnAuth.ts:494) <- _handleMessage (03-messages.js:643) <- _handleNetMessage (565) <- _processMulti (697)` — i.e. the request reached Steam's CM (via a `Multi` message) and a real response came back. This is the exact thing cycle 10 could never reach (it threw synchronously before any network I/O, per cycle 10's gap-closure evidence). Confirms cycle 11's core claim: the manual encode/`_send`/decode bypass IS reaching Steam's CM successfully.
2. **NEW FAILURE, one layer deeper:** every `type=CDN` host (steampipe.akamaized.net, alibaba, fastly) now fails with `CdnAuthTokenCache: GetCDNAuthToken failed for depot=1460472 host=steampipe.akamaized.net: Error: ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken: empty response` — sourced from `cdnAuth.ts`'s `!body?.token` branch (line 493-494, pre-cycle-12). So the callback fired, the eresult check either passed or was skipped (`eresult === undefined`), but no `token` field was present in the decoded body. Repeats every ~60s (PART 3's proactive-refresh/cooldown cadence) for all 3 CDN hosts.
3. **PART-3's fail-fast guard held perfectly:** timeouts=0, no stall, degrade-to-`''` worked exactly as designed, download kept progressing on the working (SteamCache) host. No regression vs the cycle-7/8 hardware-verified baseline.
4. `type=CDN` hosts stay `ok=0` (expected — no token ever unlocked them this run). `type=SteamCache` hosts still never attempt a token (correct, unchanged).
5. **Secondary observation (recorded, not chased this cycle):** the chunk-stream stats showed only `cache1-akl-tpwr` serving (`ok=2536` by @165s); the other two SteamCache hosts AND all CDN hosts showed `err=N`/`ok=0`/UNHEALTHY, install crawling at 2-3% after 165s, downSpeed ~14 MiB/s — the same Auckland self-inflicted-rate-limit signature documented in cycle 8 (likely from this session's own repeated multi-GB test pulls), not caused by cycle 11. However: the `err` breakdown rendered as the generic `{Error:N}` fallback name, not a real HTTP status/code — meaning cycle 10's PART-4 reason-hardening (duck-typed `.status` check) is NOT capturing the real chunk-failure reason for these particular failures; chunk fetches are apparently throwing plain `Error`s with no `.status`/`.code`/`.cause.code` in this run. Flagged for a future cycle (not addressed this cycle, per the coordinator's instructions — see Deliberately NOT changed below) — a fix there would reveal whether the SteamCache-host failures are `429` (rate-limit) vs `ECONNRESET` vs something else.

### Assessment: decode-bug vs genuine server-rejection (static reading only, not yet confirmed)

Re-reading the manual bypass end-to-end a second time (see Evidence above) did **not** surface an obvious shape/wiring bug: the request header shape matches `_sendUnified`'s own construction exactly, `_send`'s encode branch is confirmed a no-op for this pre-encoded body (no double-encode risk), and `_handleMessage`'s raw-body-passthrough for an unregistered `ServiceMethodResponse` handler name is exactly what `normalizeResponseBuffer`/`decodeCdnAuthTokenResponse` already assume (no extra wrapper message to peel off). The response HEADER's `eresult` field is decoded via steam-user's own unaffected `_decodeProto` machinery, independent of this RPC's missing-map-entry problem — so `hdr.proto.eresult` should already be a trustworthy signal in cdnAuth.ts's existing code, not something the manual bypass could itself have corrupted.

This makes hypothesis (b) — a genuine, unsurfaced server-side signal (eresult non-OK read as its schema default rather than as an explicit failure our code currently treats as "probably OK", or a legitimately empty per-host business response) — *relatively more likely* than hypothesis (a) — a decode-side bug in the manual bypass — on the strength of static reading alone. **This is not proof.** The new diagnostic logging (this cycle's Fix Applied) is what will actually settle it: a real `eresult` value other than `1`, or a `rawBodyBytes` that's suspiciously tiny/zero, or an unrecognized `rawBodyShape`, would each point at a different concrete next step.

**If `eresult` is `1` (OK) but `token` is still empty with a plausible non-zero `rawBodyBytes`/`decodedExpirationTime`:** this points at either a genuinely empty-but-well-formed business response (Steam saying "no token needed/available" for this host) or a field-name/offset bug specific to the `token` field's decode — would need a byte-level dump (not just length) as the next diagnostic step.
**If `rawBodyBytes` is `0`/tiny or `rawBodyShape` is unrecognized:** this points at a genuine decode-side bug (wrong body arg reaching the callback, or a body that isn't actually this RPC's payload) — would need to re-examine whether `_handleMessage`'s job-callback dispatch is somehow delivering the wrong `body` argument for this specific RPC, despite the static analysis above finding no such mismatch.

## Cycle 12 hardware validation — DECISIVE (2026-07-19)

**Trigger:** fresh hardware run (07:57-07:59, streaming capture) of cycle 12's eresult+raw-body diagnostic, per the checkpoint above.

### Result: decisive values, ambiguity resolved

The empty-token diagnostic fired for all 3 `type=CDN` hosts with:

- `eresult=1` (OK — the server ACCEPTED the request; not a rejection).
- `rawBodyShape=ByteBuffer`.
- `rawBodyBytes=8` (a real, tiny, well-formed protobuf — not `0`, not garbage).
- `decodedExpirationTime=1784415459`, and — the single strongest signal — this value **increments live across the ~60s proactive-refresh cycle**: `1784415459` (07:57) → `1784415519`/`1784415520` (07:58) → `1784415521` (07:58).
- `token` field: genuinely EMPTY/absent on every call.

### Conclusion (both cycle-12 open hypotheses resolved)

The live-incrementing `decodedExpirationTime` proves the decode is reading real, per-request-fresh bytes correctly (rules out hypothesis (a), a decode-side bug in the manual bypass) AND that the server answers with fresh data every time (rules out hypothesis (b) as originally framed — a silent server-side rejection). What remains is the guide's third case: a well-formed, server-accepted (`eresult=OK`), correctly-decoded response that contains `expiration_time` and NO token. **The modern `ContentServerDirectory.GetCDNAuthToken#1` RPC works end-to-end** (encode → send → CM round-trip → decode, all correct) **but Steam returns an empty token for these SteamPipe CDN edges.** URL-append-token — Option B's entire premise — cannot unlock the CDN hosts via this RPC: there is no token to append.

**Open question (unresolved, one line):** Steam's own `content_log.txt` (RESEARCH SPIKE citation) shows the real client's `AuthenticateDepotID... Success!` firing on every `type=CDN` host, yet this RPC yields no token for us. Either the real client authenticates CDN edges via a different mechanism entirely, or an empty token here genuinely means "no token needed" and the CDN chunk-fetch failures have an unrelated cause (network/environmental, matching the SteamCache hosts' own failures, or a different access rule specific to CDN edges).

### Blocker this cycle exposed: the failure-reason gap now decides the outcome

The SAME run's chunk-stream stats showed ALL 5 non-working hosts (the 3 `type=CDN` hosts + `cache1-akl-edgx` + `cache2-akl-tpwr`) failing with the GENERIC `{Error:N}` reason — identical to every prior cycle. Only `cache1-akl-tpwr` served (`ok=1313` by @90s, percent stuck at ~2%). This meant it was impossible to tell, from this run alone, whether the CDN hosts fail with a REAL `403` (genuine auth rejection, consistent with the empty-token finding above) or the SAME network/rate-limit signature already hammering the SteamCache hosts (in which case the empty token would be a red herring and the whole run is just environmental degradation). See "Cycle 13" immediately below for the fix and the root cause of why `{Error:N}` was showing up at all.

### Self-verification (cycle 12 hardware validation)

No code changed this cycle (pure hardware read-back of already-implemented, already-self-verified cycle-12 diagnostic logging) — self-verification is unchanged from cycle 12's own entry above (tsc clean, 640/640 steam suites, 1536/1536 full suite). Session remains PARKED, UNCOMMITTED.

## Cycle 13 (2026-07-19) — FIX: chunk-fetch failure-reason extraction (decode-stage + worker-boundary), self-verified, UNCOMMITTED

**Objective:** cycle 12's decisive finding leaves exactly one fact undetermined — whether the type=CDN hosts' chunk-fetch failures are a real `403`/auth rejection (confirms Option B is a dead end for closing the throughput gap) or the same network/rate-limit code already hitting the SteamCache hosts (means the empty-token finding is a red herring and this run is environmental). The generic `{Error:N}` breakdown blocks that determination. This cycle finds and fixes WHY, purely observationally — no retry/backoff/host-selection/gate behavior is touched.

### Root cause (confirmed via code reading + empirical Node/undici testing, this cycle)

Two compounding, independently-confirmed defects, both upstream of `attemptFailureReason` (decompress.ts) itself:

1. **`decodeChunk`'s own throw sites were plain, code-less `Error`s.** `decodeChunk`/`decompressChunk` (decompress.ts) threw bare `new Error(...)` for a bad VZ footer magic, an unrecognized chunk container, a sha1 mismatch, and a size mismatch. A base `Error`'s `.name` defaults to the literal string `'Error'` — indistinguishable, once it reaches `attemptFailureReason`'s final `?? 'Error'` fallback (or its `e?.name` branch, which resolves to the SAME literal string either way), from "no information was captured at all." Empirically verified (via real Node `fetch()` against deliberately-broken local servers: connection-refused, DNS-NXDOMAIN, mid-transfer socket reset, TLS-handshake reset) that genuine NETWORK-level `fetch()` failures in this Node/undici version reliably set `.name = 'TypeError'` and `.cause.code` to the real underlying code (`ECONNREFUSED`/`ENOTFOUND`/`UND_ERR_SOCKET`/`ECONNRESET`) — i.e. the pre-existing `e?.code ?? e?.cause?.code` fallback chain (cycle 9/10) already correctly classifies genuine network errors. The literal `"Error"` breakdown a hardware run showed is NOT explained by a gap in that chain; it is explained by decode-stage failures, which never carried a `.code` in the first place.
2. **(The more structural half) `DecompressPool`'s worker_threads message-passing boundary discarded EVERY decode-stage error's identity.** `decompressWorker.ts`'s `handleDecodeMessage` returned only `{ ok: false, error: string }` (message text only) on any throw, and `decompressPool.ts`'s `wireWorker` message handler reconstructed the rejection as a bare `new Error(response.error)` — even for a decode error that DID carry a distinguishing `.code`, that information never crossed the `postMessage` boundary at all. Since `DecompressPool` (not the inline main-thread decoder) is the production default (Phase 21-15), EVERY decode-stage chunk failure in a real install was guaranteed to reach `fetchChunk`'s catch block as a generic, code-less `Error`, regardless of what the original failure actually was. This — not a gap in the network-error fallback chain — is the dominant, structural reason the hardware run's chunk-stream stats showed `{Error:N}` for the failing hosts.

Both defects independently produce the exact symptom observed; the fix (below) closes both.

### Fix Applied (cycle 13, 2026-07-19, self-verified, UNCOMMITTED)

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: new `ChunkDecodeError` class (mirrors `ChunkHttpError`'s existing plain-property pattern) carrying a short, stable `.code`. Every `decodeChunk`/`decompressChunk` throw site now tags its own distinct code: `bad_footer_magic`, `unknown_container`, `sha1_mismatch`, `size_mismatch`. `decodeChunk`'s body is now wrapped in a try/catch that re-throws an already-coded error unchanged (preserving Node's own native crypto/zlib error codes from `steamDecrypt`/`inflateRawSync`/lzma, never overwriting them) and tags anything else (a raw lzma internal failure, an uncoded crypto/zlib error) as `decode_failed` — so NOTHING coming out of the decode stage can reach `attemptFailureReason` as a bare, code-less `Error` anymore. `attemptFailureReason` itself gained a new `networkErrorCode` helper: walks `.code` → `.cause.code` → `.cause.cause.code` → the first `AggregateError.errors[].code` under either level — a defensive hardening for genuine network-level failures (undici's `TypeError: fetch failed`/`terminated` wrapping, double-wrapped causes, and Happy-Eyeballs multi-address `AggregateError`s), complementary to (not a replacement for) the decode-stage fix, which is the one that actually explains the observed hardware symptom.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompressWorker.ts`: `DecompressWorkerResponse`'s `ok: false` variant gained an optional `code?: string` field, populated from the caught error's own `.code` (duck-typed, `undefined` when absent) alongside the existing `error` message string. A plain string, structured-clone-safe across `postMessage` — no key/token/URL crosses this boundary, unchanged from before.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompressPool.ts`: new `taggedPoolError(message, code)` helper. `wireWorker`'s message handler now reconstructs a decode-stage rejection carrying the worker-reported `.code` (falling back to a bare `Error` only when the worker didn't send one). The pool's OWN infrastructure-level failures — a per-task timeout (`decompress_pool_timeout`) and a worker exit/crash (`decompress_worker_exit`) — are now tagged the same way, so a pool-level failure is equally distinguishable from a decode-stage or network-stage one instead of ALSO collapsing to the generic `"Error"`.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/fixtures/poolTestWorker.js` (test-only, mirrors `decompressWorker.ts`'s wire protocol for real-`worker_threads` integration tests): updated in lockstep — tags its own decode throws with the same codes and posts them back via the new `code` field, so the "real OS thread" pool tests exercise the actual worker-boundary fix, not just the in-process unit tests.
- Deliberately NOT changed: PART-3's fail-fast/cooldown/degrade-to-`''` guard (`cdnAuth.ts`); PART-2's `type==='CDN'` gate; the CDN-auth RPC itself (`cdnAuth.ts`, understood as of cycle 12, untouched); `hostHealth.ts`; `stallTracker.ts`; the StateFlags/`canWriteFullOwnership` gate; the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate's ENFORCEMENT (still exactly `sha1(decompressed) === expectedSha`, unchanged — only the MESSAGE/CODE attached to a failed check changed); `downloadSteamDepots`'s `failures.length` escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 abort-vs-drop semantics; GOG/Epic/Amazon paths.

### Tests added (cycle 13)

- `depotPrimitives.test.ts` (`fetchChunk` → `onAttempt reason reporting` describe block), 4 new tests: `reason` is `'sha1_mismatch'` for a chunk that downloads fine but never verifies; `reason` is `'unknown_container'` for a response body that isn't a valid chunk container at all (the shape a token-less CDN edge returning a small HTTP-200 denial/interstitial page instead of a proper 401/403 would take); `networkErrorCode` correctly reports `'ECONNRESET'` from a DOUBLE-wrapped `.cause.cause.code` (not just one level); `networkErrorCode` correctly reports the first nested code (`'ENOTFOUND'`) from an `AggregateError.errors[]` with no top-level `.code`.
- `decompressPool.test.ts`, 1 new dedicated test (`handleDecodeMessage` now posts `code: 'unknown_container'` alongside the message) plus 2 existing tests extended in place (the real-worker "throws on malformed buffer" test now also asserts `caught.code === 'unknown_container'` survived the actual `worker_threads` boundary; the "hangs past per-task timeout" test now also asserts `caught.code === 'decompress_pool_timeout'`).
