## Cycle 14 — DECISIVE pcap: CDN needs no auth, only a Steam User-Agent (2026-07-19) — SUPERSEDES the entire cycle 6-13 CDN-auth-token thread

**Trigger:** the checkpoint above (cycle 13) requested a clean per-host reason-code breakdown. Before that hardware run happened, the user instead captured a cleartext packet capture (`tcpdump` on port 80) of the REAL Steam client performing a large native download (depot 1295663), specifically targeting the type=CDN `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` edge, to observe exactly what the real client sends on the wire. This is a categorically stronger form of evidence than anything gathered in cycles 6-13 — it is not this codebase's own instrumentation or hypothesis, it is ground truth from the reference implementation.

### Evidence (cycle 14, 2026-07-19)

- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — action: cleartext `tcpdump` capture of the real Steam client's HTTP/1.1 chunk-fetch traffic against `alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com` (HTTP-only, CloudFront-fronted CDN edge) during a real large native download (depot 1295663). found: 10,722 total chunk-fetch requests captured. The EXACT request shape, byte-for-byte, across every single one:
  ```
  GET /depot/1295663/chunk/<sha1> HTTP/1.1
  Host: alibaba.cdn.steampipe.steamcontent.com
  x-chunk-score: 0.778163
  Accept: text/html,*/*;q=0.9
  accept-encoding: gzip,identity,*;q=0
  accept-charset: ISO-8859-1,utf-8,*;q=0.7
  user-agent: Valve/Steam HTTP Client 1.0
  ```
  implication: this is the ground-truth shape of a request the CDN edge actually accepts and serves, straight from the reference client — everything downstream below is measured against this baseline.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — found (finding 1, decisive): across ALL 10,722 captured requests, the ONLY path pattern is `GET /depot/<id>/chunk/<sha>` — no `AuthenticateDepotID` HTTP request, no `/auth`, no `/initsession`, no POST anywhere, no query string, no `?token=`, no `Cookie` header, no `Authorization` header. The chunk fetch itself is a bare, unauthenticated GET, full stop. implication: `AuthenticateDepotID` (the line this session's RESEARCH SPIKE and cycles 6-13 chased via `content_log.txt`) is a CM-side/logical log line describing an internal Steam-client bookkeeping step — it is NOT an HTTP request the CDN edge ever receives or requires. The "CDN needs a handshake before it'll serve chunks" theory that has driven this entire session since the RESEARCH SPIKE is DISPROVEN on the wire, directly, by the reference client's own traffic.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — found (finding 2, decisive): cycle 12's hardware-captured `GetCDNAuthToken` response — `eresult=1` (OK), `rawBodyBytes=8`, `token` field genuinely empty, `expiration_time` live-incrementing — was CORRECT all along, not a partial/ambiguous result requiring more diagnosis. No token is needed because modern SteamPipe CDN edges use none. implication: the entire cycle 6-12 effort to acquire, decode, and append a CDN auth token (cycle 6's original wiring, cycle 7/8's gating, cycle 9/10's RPC-string/wiring root-causing, cycle 11's manual `_send` bypass, cycle 12's diagnostic) was rigorous, correct engineering against a mechanism that was never the actual blocker — a confirmed phantom, not a wasted effort in the sense of being wrong about the RPC itself (the RPC really does work exactly as characterized), but wrong about its relevance to the throughput gap.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — found (finding 3, decisive): the `alibaba` CDN host worked PERFECTLY for the real client during this same capture window (~08:26, 5,361 chunks flowing with zero apparent failures) — the SAME host that fails ~100% of the time for GameLib's own native-install attempts across every prior hardware cycle in this session (cycles 3, 8, 11, 12, 13). implication: the `alibaba` edge itself, this network, and this machine are all capable of serving this host successfully — ruling out an environmental/network-health explanation (the leading alternative hypothesis flagged at the end of cycle 13) for GameLib's own failures against the identical host. The difference must be in what GameLib's own request looks like, not in the network or the CDN's general availability.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked (`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`, `fetchChunk`, pre-cycle-14 state, line 495-497): confirmed the chunk-fetch `fetch()` call passed ONLY `{ signal: controller.signal }` — zero headers of any kind. found: per Node/undici's own default behavior, an unheadered `fetch()` sends undici's own default `user-agent` (typically `node`, not any Steam-specific string) and no `Accept`/`Accept-Charset` header at all. implication: GameLib's request to `alibaba` was byte-for-byte identical to the real client's on the URL/path (empty CDN-auth token from cycle 12 → the same bare `/depot/<id>/chunk/<sha>` path, no query string appended, matching finding 1 exactly; cycle 7's scheme-parity logic already selects the correct `http://` for `alibaba` specifically) but diverged completely on headers — no Steam User-Agent, no Accept, no Accept-Charset. This is the single concrete, testable difference between a request the CDN edge is proven (by the pcap) to accept, and the request shape GameLib has been sending across every hardware cycle to date.

### Root cause (reframed, HIGH confidence — supersedes the cycle 6-13 root-cause narrative)

Steam's CDN edges (the CloudFront-fronted `type=CDN` hosts — akamai/fastly/alibaba) filter incoming chunk-fetch requests by `User-Agent` (and likely by the presence of Steam's static `Accept`/`Accept-Charset` headers) rather than by any token/auth mechanism. GameLib's `fetchChunk` sent undici's bare default headers, which these CDN edges reject (consistent with the ~100% failure rate observed against `type=CDN` hosts across every prior hardware cycle), confining all real throughput to the local SteamCache hosts and producing the originally-reported 2.5x throughput gap vs the real client. This is **NOT** auth, **NOT** a missing/expired token, **NOT** `AuthenticateDepotID` (a CM-side log line, never an HTTP step), and **NOT** an environmental/network-health issue (disproven by finding 3 — the same host, same network, same machine, served the real client perfectly in the same capture window).

**The cycle 6-13 GetCDNAuthToken token-RPC investigation path is a CONFIRMED PHANTOM.** Every individual finding along that path remains factually correct and well-evidenced (the RPC's method-name string, cycle-10's missing-map-entry TypeError root cause, cycle-11's working manual `_send` bypass, cycle-12's decisive `eresult=1`+empty-token capture) — but the mechanism itself was never the thing gating CDN chunk delivery. Cycle 12's own open question ("either the real client authenticates CDN edges via a different mechanism entirely, or an empty token genuinely means no token needed and the CDN failures have an unrelated cause") is now resolved decisively in favor of the second branch: no token needed, unrelated (User-Agent) cause.

### Fix Applied (cycle 14, 2026-07-19, self-verified, UNCOMMITTED)

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: new exported `CHUNK_FETCH_HEADERS` constant — `{ 'User-Agent': 'Valve/Steam HTTP Client 1.0', Accept: 'text/html,*/*;q=0.9', 'Accept-Charset': 'ISO-8859-1,utf-8,*;q=0.7' }` — threaded into `fetchChunk`'s `fetch()` call (`headers: CHUNK_FETCH_HEADERS`, alongside the existing `signal: controller.signal`). Applied unconditionally to every host attempt, both `type=CDN` and `type=SteamCache` — the real client sends the identical headers everywhere per the pcap, so this cannot regress the already-working SteamCache hosts and can only help the CDN ones.
- Deliberately OMITTED `x-chunk-score` — the pcap's own header is a dynamic, per-chunk load-hint value the real client presumably computes from its own internal state; there is no legitimate way to compute a matching value from this codebase, and a fabricated/static value risks looking worse to a scoring-aware edge than omitting the header entirely. Not added.
- Deliberately left `Accept-Encoding` unset (undici's own default, which auto-negotiates and transparently decodes gzip) rather than forcing the pcap's literal `gzip,identity,*;q=0` — functionally equivalent for this codebase's usage (the response body is always read via `res.arrayBuffer()`, already-decoded), lower risk of an encoding-handling regression, and not a header the finding attributes any filtering behavior to.
- Deliberately NOT changed this cycle: PART-3's fail-fast guard (`cdnAuth.ts` — timeout/cooldown/degrade-to-`''`/never-block); PART-2's `type==='CDN'` gate; the CDN-auth RPC itself (`cdnAuth.ts`, cycles 6-12's implementation, left dormant — see Recommendation below); `hostHealth.ts`; `stallTracker.ts`; the StateFlags/`canWriteFullOwnership` gate; the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate's enforcement; `downloadSteamDepots`'s escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 abort-vs-drop semantics; GOG/Epic/Amazon paths.

### Tests added (cycle 14)

- `depotPrimitives.test.ts` (`fetchChunk` describe block), 1 new test: asserts the outgoing `fetch()` call's `RequestInit.headers` deep-equals `CHUNK_FETCH_HEADERS` (i.e. exactly `User-Agent`/`Accept`/`Accept-Charset`, no more, no less) for a normal successful chunk fetch — proves the fix is wired all the way through `fetchChunk`'s actual network call, not just that the constant exists.

### Self-verification (cycle 14, 2026-07-19)

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean (whole project). Steam-specific suites (`npx jest --config src/backend/jest.config.js src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/`): 16 suites, **654/654** pass (653 cycle-13 baseline + 1 net-new). Full suite (`npx jest`): **82 suites, 1552/1552** tests pass (1551 cycle-13 baseline + 1 net-new). Both runs reproduce the same pre-existing, unrelated `readAcfState`/`pollInstallOnce` leaked-timer teardown warning documented identically since cycle 2/3/8/10/11 — not introduced by this cycle. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression (`git diff --stat` unchanged outside the steam store manager). No change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched). No change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate. No change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard. `hostHealth.ts`/`stallTracker.ts`/`cdnAuth.ts` byte-for-byte untouched this cycle. The sha1 chunk-integrity gate's enforcement untouched. PART-3's fail-fast guard and PART-2's `type==='CDN'` gate are mechanically untouched — this cycle's only functional change is the two-line `headers` addition to `fetchChunk`'s `fetch()` call plus the new constant it references.

### Recommendation: what to do with the now-confirmed-unnecessary cycle 10-12 GetCDNAuthToken machinery

The manual `_send` bypass (`cdnAuth.ts`'s `callGetCDNAuthToken`, cycle 11), the diagnostic logging it grew (cycle 12), and the `type==='CDN'` gate widening that triggers it (cycle 7's PART 2, widened in the CDN-auth cycle) are now confirmed unnecessary for closing the throughput gap — the header fix is what actually matters. Two options, presented for the coordinator's decision (not blocking the header fix, which is independent and already implemented/verified):

- **(i) Leave it dormant/harmless (recommended).** The machinery is fully guarded: PART-3's fail-fast timeout/cooldown/degrade-to-`''` has now been proven airtight against two different real hardware failure modes (cycle-10's TypeError, cycle-11/12's empty-token response) — worst case it always degrades to a token-less request within a bounded 3s window, then goes silent for 60s per (depot,host). With cycle 14's header fix in place, the token it fetches (always empty, per cycle 12) gets appended as an empty string to a request that now succeeds anyway because of the headers — i.e. it becomes provably inert, not just probably harmless. Reasoning for keeping it: (a) it cost real engineering effort to build and verify and contains genuinely correct, reusable RPC-wiring knowledge (the manual `_send`-bypass pattern for an unregistered steam-user RPC) that could matter for a future RPC needing the same treatment; (b) reverting it is itself a risk — cycle 10/11's work touched `cdnAuth.ts`'s `CDNAuthTokenClient` interface and `depot.ts`'s `SteamUserDepotExtras` interface (`_sendUnified` → `_send` signature swap), and unwinding that means re-touching files this cycle didn't need to touch, for a benefit (a small reduction in surface area) that has no throughput impact once the header fix is confirmed; (c) reverting now, before the header fix itself is hardware-confirmed, would conflate two independent changes in the next verification run.
- **(ii) Revert to reduce surface area.** Would restore `cdnAuth.ts` to a smaller, cycle-7/8-shaped module (drop the manual `_send` bypass, the `describeRawBody` diagnostic, the widened `type==='CDN'` gate) — a real simplification if the token mechanism is truly never going to be useful. Cost: throws away cycle 10-12's correctly-diagnosed RPC-wiring knowledge and the tests that pin it down; requires its own regression pass; and — importantly — cannot be done THIS cycle without confounding the next hardware run's read on whether the header fix alone is sufficient (if both changes land together and the run succeeds, it's not possible to attribute the win to the header fix specifically vs. "removing whatever was interfering before").

**This cycle's recommendation: (i), leave dormant.** No code from cycles 10-12 was touched or reverted this cycle. Revisit after the header fix is hardware-confirmed, as a separate, low-risk cleanup cycle if the coordinator agrees the RPC machinery has no other use.

### NOT yet verified

The actual real-world effect of the header fix against Steam's live CDN edges — whether adding `User-Agent: Valve/Steam HTTP Client 1.0` (+ the two Accept headers) is sufficient, by itself, to flip the `type=CDN` hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) from ~100% failure to `ok>0`, and whether aggregate throughput actually rises toward the real client's observed multi-host parallel rate — requires a fresh hardware reproduction. Per the objective, this cycle's changes are **UNCOMMITTED**, pending that hardware validation run. Acceptance criteria: (a) the `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` log's `type=CDN` hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) show `ok>0` instead of near-100% failure; (b) `type=SteamCache` hosts remain unaffected/still-successful (no regression from the added headers); (c) aggregate throughput rises noticeably vs. every prior cycle's hardware runs (all of which showed the type=CDN hosts at ~0% ok); (d) if any CDN host still fails, cycle-13's now-hardened `attemptFailureReason`/`ChunkDecodeError`/`networkErrorCode` machinery should surface a REAL reason code (an HTTP status, a network code, or a decode-stage code) rather than a generic `{Error:N}`, which would tell us whether headers alone were insufficient (e.g. still 403 — something else in the request is still wrong) or something unrelated is now the limiter. Session status remains PARKED (not resolved) — do NOT mark this session resolved until that hardware run confirms the fix.

## Cycle 15 hardware validation — header fix did not unlock CDN; unknown_container decode failure revealed as the real bottleneck (2026-07-19)

**Trigger:** the checkpoint above (cycle 14) requested a fresh hardware capture to confirm the header fix flips `type=CDN` hosts to `ok>0`. A fresh run (08:42-08:43, ~90s streaming) came back — do not re-run; work from the captured results.

### Result: header fix did NOT unlock CDN, but revealed the real bottleneck

- `type=CDN` hosts (akamai/fastly/alibaba) STILL `ok=0`. Throughput still stuck: `percent=2%` at 90s, `downSpeed` ~16 MiB/s. No CDN win from the header fix alone.
- **BUT the failure reason on EVERY failing host is now `{unknown_container:N}`** (was generic `{Error:N}` before cycle 13's reason-extraction fix). This is a DECODE-STAGE error thrown at `decompress.ts:109` (`decompressChunk`'s final `throw new ChunkDecodeError('unknown_container', ...)`) — AFTER a successful fetch. `decodeChunk` AES-decrypts the fetched bytes with the depot key, then checks the decrypted magic for `'VZ'` (LZMA) or `'PK'` (zlib); neither matches, so these chunks are FETCHED (HTTP 200) but decrypt to non-container garbage.
- Per-host @90s: `cache2-akl-tpwr` a=1674 ok=1377 (WORKS); `cache1-akl-tpwr` a=256 ok=5; `cache1-akl-edgx` a=233 ok=4; `fastly` a=169 ok=0; `akamai` a=162 ok=0; `alibaba` a=158 ok=0 — all failures `{unknown_container}`. The SAME depot key works fine on `cache2-akl-tpwr` (ok=1377), so the key + decode path are correct; the FAILING hosts are returning data that decrypts to garbage.
- `GetCDNAuthToken` empty-token warnings still present (harmless/dormant, expected — matches cycle 12's finding, unrelated to this reframe).

### BIG REFRAME

`unknown_container` affects MOST hosts (both SteamCache AND CDN), not just CDN — and only ONE host (`cache2-akl-tpwr`) decodes cleanly. This has almost certainly been the throughput bottleneck ALL ALONG (masked as generic `{Error:N}` before cycle 13) — the download has been confined to whichever single host returns decodable chunks. This is **NOT primarily a CDN-auth problem** (that whole thread remains confirmed dead per cycle 14's pcap); it is a **CHUNK DECODE / RESPONSE-HANDLING problem**. Cycle 14's `User-Agent` fix may still have gotten CDN hosts past an HTTP-level filter (they now return 200 instead of being rejected outright) — but their 200 bodies don't decode. The cycle 6-13 CDN-auth-token investigation stays a confirmed phantom (cycle 14's conclusion is unchanged), but it is no longer the leading explanation for the throughput gap at all — a decode/response-handling defect is.

### Leading hypotheses (for the next capture to distinguish)

1. **Failing hosts return a NON-CHUNK body with HTTP 200** — e.g. an HTML error/challenge/interstitial page. Decrypting HTML produces garbage => `unknown_container`. Cycle 14 added `Accept: text/html,*/*;q=0.9` — this could actively INVITE HTML error responses from edges that branch on `Accept`. Worth testing UA-only (drop the `Accept`/`Accept-Charset` headers) to isolate whether the `Accept` header changed what hosts return — but see "Decision on Accept headers" below for why this cycle did NOT make that change yet.
2. **Content-Encoding mismatch** — a host returns the chunk with `Content-Encoding: gzip` (some caches wrongly gzip already-binary data). If undici auto-decompresses inconsistently, or if the real client's `accept-encoding: gzip,identity,*;q=0` matters, the bytes get corrupted per-host. `cache2-akl-tpwr` (working) may simply not gzip while others do.
3. **Response truncation / wrong length** (`cb_original` mismatch would throw `size_mismatch`, not `unknown_container`, so less likely, but worth ruling out with the raw byte-length metadata this cycle's diagnostic captures).

### Fix Applied (cycle 15, 2026-07-19, self-verified, UNCOMMITTED)

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: new `describeRawChunkResponse(res, raw)` helper — duck-typed on `res.headers?.get?.()` (never throws; several existing test fixtures construct a bare `{ ok, arrayBuffer }` response object with no `headers` at all), returns a single formatted string: `httpStatus=`, `contentType=`, `contentEncoding=`, `contentLength=` (all from the response headers, `'absent'` when missing), `rawBodyBytes=` (the raw pre-decrypt buffer's actual length), `rawPreviewHex=` and `rawPreviewLatin1=` (the first `RAW_BODY_PREVIEW_BYTES=16` bytes of the RAW, pre-decrypt fetched body). Wired into `fetchChunk`'s catch block: `res`/`encrypted` are now `let`-hoisted above the try block (previously `const`-declared inside it) so the catch block can read them; `encrypted` is only ever assigned AFTER a successful `res.ok` fetch, so its presence in the catch block IS the signal that this specific attempt failed at the DECODE stage (not the HTTP/network stage — both of which throw before `encrypted` is ever assigned). Logs via `logWarning` with `depotId`/`host`/`attempt`/`reason` for host attribution, immediately after the existing `onAttempt` call, before the existing backoff sleep. No secret redaction needed (chunk bodies are public game content) — the preview is capped to 16 bytes regardless.
- Purely additive/observational: does not change `fetch`/`decrypt`/`decompressChunk`'s magic-check logic, retry count, backoff, or host-selection. Only a new diagnostic log line on an already-existing failure path.
- Deliberately NOT changed this cycle: PART-3's fail-fast guard (`cdnAuth.ts`); PART-2's `type==='CDN'` gate; `hostHealth.ts`; `stallTracker.ts`; the StateFlags/`canWriteFullOwnership` gate; the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate's enforcement; `downloadSteamDepots`'s escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 abort-vs-drop semantics; GOG/Epic/Amazon paths; the AES-decrypt/magic-check logic itself (`steamDecrypt`, `decompressChunk`'s `'VZ'`/`'PK'` checks — untouched, only observed).

### Decision on cycle 14's Accept/Accept-Charset headers (hypothesis 1)

**Decision: left UNCHANGED this cycle.** The checkpoint flagged that cycle 14's `Accept: text/html,*/*;q=0.9` header could itself be inviting HTML error responses from CDN edges, and asked whether dropping it back to UA-only headers was warranted. Reasoning for NOT doing so yet:

- The pcap (cycle 14's own ground truth) shows the REAL client sending this exact `Accept` header on every request, including to the one CDN host (`alibaba`) that served it perfectly in the same capture window — so the header is proven NOT harmful to the real client on the real edge. If it's harmful to GameLib's own requests specifically, that would itself be a significant, standalone finding (not just "this header is bad, remove it") — the raw-body diagnostic (Content-Type + body preview) directly tests this without needing a code change: if a failing host's response has `Content-Type: text/html` and a body starting with `<!DOCTYPE`/`<html`, that confirms hypothesis 1 on the wire, immediately.
- Removing the header now would be a BEHAVIOR change, not a purely observational one — it risks confounding the next capture's read. If headers are dropped and hosts still fail with `unknown_container`, that doesn't cleanly rule out hypothesis 1 (a host might branch on `Accept` in a way this session doesn't yet understand, e.g. a different-but-still-wrong response) — whereas the diagnostic's direct observation (Content-Type/body preview) settles it without ambiguity.
- Per the objective's own guidance: leave the headers and let the raw-body diagnostic decide first, unless the case for removing them is clearly aligned with existing evidence and low-risk. Here it's genuinely ambiguous (the pcap proves the header is harmless to the REAL client, not that it's harmless to ours) — so the diagnostic goes first.
- The `User-Agent` header itself was NOT considered for removal (per the objective's explicit instruction) — it remains the one confirmed-real-client value cycle 14 established from the pcap.

If the next capture's Content-Type/body-preview data confirms hypothesis 1 (HTML error page), dropping or adjusting the `Accept` header (or handling a `text/html` response specially) becomes the next concrete, evidence-backed fix — deferred to that cycle.

### Tests added (cycle 15)

- `depotPrimitives.test.ts` (`fetchChunk` describe block), 2 new tests: (1) a positive test asserting the raw-response-metadata diagnostic fires via `logWarning` on an `unknown_container` decode failure, with the logged message containing `httpStatus=200`, `contentType=text/html`, `contentEncoding=absent`, `contentLength=<N>`, `rawBodyBytes=<N>`, and a 32-hex-character preview; (2) a negative test proving the diagnostic does NOT fire for a network-level failure where `fetch()` itself rejects (so `encrypted` is never assigned) — proves the gate is keyed on "did decode actually run", not merely "did this attempt fail".

## Cycle 16 hardware validation — DECISIVE: fetches succeed, decrypt fails (unknown_container), host-correlated; regression hunt DEFINITIVELY negative (2026-07-19)

**Trigger:** cycle 15's checkpoint requested a short hardware capture of the raw-response-metadata diagnostic. A fresh run (09:52-09:53) came back. Also reported: the app didn't launch for a while due to an unrelated build-tooling issue, fixed as part of the same uncommitted set — see the separate "Build fix" evidence entry below.

### Evidence (cycle 16, 2026-07-19) — hardware capture

- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — DECISIVE hardware capture (fresh run, 09:52-09:53) of cycle 15's raw-response diagnostic, firing for BOTH CDN hosts (alibaba/fastly) AND SteamCache hosts (cache2/cache1-akl-edgx):
  ```
  fetchChunk: decode-stage failure reason=unknown_container depot=1460472 host=alibaba... attempt=4
    httpStatus=200 contentType=application/x-steam-chunk contentEncoding=absent
    contentLength=1048656 rawBodyBytes=1048656 rawPreviewHex=e67688441264f1c2aa8616890f1c0099
  ```
  found: (1) the FETCH SUCCEEDS — `httpStatus=200`, `contentType=application/x-steam-chunk` (a real chunk response, not an HTML/interstitial page), `contentEncoding=absent` (not gzip), `contentLength` EXACTLY equals `rawBodyBytes` (the full ~1MB body, not truncated). This DIRECTLY DISPROVES hypothesis 1 (HTML error page) and hypothesis 2 (gzip mismatch) from cycle 15's interpretation guide — not just deprioritizes them, the capture's own metadata rules them out. (2) `unknown_container` is thrown AFTER decrypt (`decompress.ts`'s `decompressChunk`, the VZ/PK magic check) — the `rawPreviewHex` is the RAW, still-ENCRYPTED body (correctly random-looking, consistent with intact ciphertext, not a plaintext error message). (3) STRONGLY HOST-CORRELATED at @87s: `cache1-akl-tpwr` a=676 ok=474 (~70%), `alibaba` a=141 ok=0, `fastly` a=123 ok=0, `akamai` a=117 ok=0, `cache2-akl-tpwr` a=125 ok=0, `cache1-akl-edgx` a=234 ok=4 — every failure on every non-working host is `{unknown_container}`. Same `depot=1460472` for every attempt shown, so the SAME decryption key applies to every one of these chunks. Throughput ~1% at 87s, ~5 MiB/s. implication: reframes the root cause a further time — the throughput bottleneck is a DECODE-CORRECTNESS bug (fetches succeed with intact, correctly-shaped encrypted bodies; the decrypt/decode step produces non-VZ/PK garbage for most chunks), not a network/auth/CDN-access problem. The cycle 6-14 CDN-auth-token arc (a confirmed dead end per cycle 14's pcap) was investigating an orthogonal, non-blocking mechanism the whole time — CDN access itself was never the bottleneck; decode correctness is. (Note: which SPECIFIC host is "the one that works" varies run-to-run — cycle 15's run had `cache2-akl-tpwr` as the sole working host; this run has `cache1-akl-tpwr` working and `cache2-akl-tpwr` FAILING. This run-to-run swap is itself a significant new data point — see the "Puzzle" analysis below.)

### Evidence (cycle 16, 2026-07-19) — build fix (ASIDE, unrelated to the Steam decode investigation)

- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked: `src/backend/main_window.ts:67-72` — `build/public` had been wiped by a build-tooling issue and the main bundle code-splits `main_window` into `build/main/chunks/`, which shifts `__dirname` and breaks a `path.join(__dirname, '../preload/index.js')` relative lookup (resolves to a non-existent `build/main/preload`). found/fix (already present in the uncommitted set, confirmed correct on inspection): `main_window.ts:72` now resolves via `path.join(app.getAppPath(), 'build/preload/index.js')` (`app.getAppPath()` is the project root in dev, the asar root when packaged, and is NOT affected by which chunk `__dirname` resolves to) — `app` added to the `electron` import at the top of the file. implication: a legit, narrowly-scoped latent-bug fix, unrelated to the Steam depot-download investigation — KEPT, not reverted. This is the only non-Steam file in the uncommitted set.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19 — checked: running the full jest suite after confirming the above surfaced 6 PRE-EXISTING failures in `src/backend/__tests__/main_window.test.ts` (`TypeError: electron_1.app.getAppPath is not a function`, then `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')`/`"path" argument must be of type string. Received undefined`) — the shared `src/backend/__mocks__/electron.ts` mock's `app` object never declared `getAppPath` at all. found: this is a genuine test-infrastructure gap exposed by (not introduced as a design flaw by) the build fix — `createMainWindow()` now legitimately calls a real Electron API (`app.getAppPath()`) the mock never modeled. action: added `getAppPath()` to the mock. First attempt used `jest.fn().mockImplementation(() => appBasePath)` (mirroring the existing `getPath` field) — this FAILED with the same `undefined` error, because this jest config sets `resetMocks: true`, which strips any `.mockImplementation()` set on a module-load-time `jest.fn()` before EVERY test body runs; `getPath`'s own mock only appears to "work" because `electron-store`'s construction calls it ONCE at module-import time (before the first reset ever fires), not because `resetMocks` spares it. Fixed by declaring `getAppPath` as a PLAIN method (`getAppPath(): string { return appBasePath }`), matching the existing `getVersion()` field's pattern exactly — immune to `resetMocks` since it's not a `jest.fn()` at all. implication: confirms this cycle's fix is correct and durable, and documents a real, previously-undocumented pitfall in this test file's electron mock (a `jest.fn().mockImplementation()` set at module scope in `__mocks__/electron.ts` is silently inert for any call made from inside a test body, under this project's `resetMocks: true` config) for future additions to the same mock.

### Root cause status (cycle 16) — regression question ANSWERED, decode bug itself NOT YET FOUND

**Regression-vs-pre-existing (ANSWERED, high confidence, direct diff evidence):** the top suspect entering this cycle was a regression in cycle 13's uncommitted `depot/decompressPool.ts`/`depot/decompressWorker.ts` changes (the worker_threads decode pool). `git diff 33c108a0 -- src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompressPool.ts src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompressWorker.ts` shows EVERY line cycle 13 touched in both files is confined to the FAILURE/error-object path (`taggedPoolError`, threading `.code` through `DecompressWorkerResponse`, reconstructing a tagged `Error` in `wireWorker`'s message handler) — a pool-level failure now carries a distinguishing code instead of collapsing to a bare `Error`. The SUCCESS path — `toOwnArrayBuffer`'s `buf.buffer.slice(buf.byteOffset, buf.byteOffset + buf.byteLength)` (already correctly bounds by both `byteOffset` AND `byteLength`, using `.slice()` which COPIES rather than `.subarray()`'s shared-memory view — the exact byteOffset/byteLength-across-worker-boundary bug the objective flagged as the leading concrete hypothesis does NOT exist in this code), the key-copy-before-transfer (`Buffer.from(task.key)` then `toOwnArrayBuffer`, never the caller's original reference), the encrypted-buffer transfer list, `decompressWorker.ts`'s `handleDecodeMessage`'s call into `decodeChunk` and its own response-buffer slicing (`result.buffer.slice(result.byteOffset, result.byteOffset + result.byteLength)`) — is BYTE-FOR-BYTE IDENTICAL to HEAD. `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/crypto.ts` (`steamDecrypt`) has ZERO diff against HEAD and does not even appear in `git status` — completely untouched all session. **Conclusion: this is DEFINITIVELY NOT a cycle-13 (or any other uncommitted-cycle) regression.** `unknown_container` is a PRE-EXISTING condition at HEAD 33c108a0, previously invisible because every decode-stage failure collapsed into the generic `{Error:N}` bucket before cycle 13's reason-extraction fix gave it a name — exactly the hypothesis the debug file itself already carried into this cycle, now confirmed by direct comparison rather than inference.

**The underlying decode-correctness bug itself: NOT located this cycle.** An exhaustive static-analysis pass across every layer between "bytes leave the CDN/cache edge" and "bytes are checked against VZ/PK magic" found no code-level defect:
- `DecompressPool`'s worker-boundary marshalling (both directions) always copies via `.slice()`/`Buffer.from()` bounded by `byteOffset`/`byteLength` — never a raw pooled-slab reference, never a shared/reused buffer, never an ArrayBuffer transferred and then read again from the sender side.
- `reduceContentServers` (depot.ts) builds BOTH the flat `hosts` array AND the `hostMeta`/`weightedLoads` maps from the IDENTICAL `s.Host ?? s.vhost` key in the same loop iteration — no map/array key-mismatch is possible; a host string used for the TCP connection is guaranteed to be the same string `hostMeta.get(host)` looks up.
- The depot decryption `key` is resolved fresh per depot from `client.getDepotDecryptionKey`'s own callback (`fetchDepotPlanEntry`) and threaded through `plan.depots[i].key` → `job.key` → `fetchChunk`'s `key` parameter as a plain function-parameter reference, never a shared/mutable buffer that a concurrent depot's key-fetch could overwrite in place; `DecompressPool.dispatch()` additionally makes its OWN fresh copy (`Buffer.from(task.key)`) synchronously before any await, so even a queued (not-yet-dispatched) task can never pick up a since-mutated key.
- `fetchChunk`'s attempt loop captures `host`/`scheme`/`meta` as per-iteration `const`s (no closure-over-mutable-loop-variable hazard) — the `host` string attributed to a diagnostic log line or `hostHealth.record()` call is guaranteed to be the SAME host the actual `fetch()` call in that iteration used.
- The CDN-auth token (per cycle 12) is confirmed genuinely empty (`""`) for every `wantsCdnAuthToken` host, so even a hypothetical `wantsCdnAuthToken` mis-gating bug (not found, but considered) could only ever append an empty string to the URL — cannot corrupt the request.
- Both `wireWorker`'s message-id bookkeeping (`this.nextId++`, a monotonic counter, no id-reuse/collision risk within a pool instance's lifetime) and `Buffer.from(msg.encrypted)` on the worker side (wrapping an already-exactly-sized, freshly-transferred `ArrayBuffer` — no pooled-slab risk since it originated from `toOwnArrayBuffer`'s own `.slice()`) were reviewed and found correct.

**What remains unexplained, and the most informative new data point this cycle surfaced:** cycle 15's run had `cache2-akl-tpwr` as the ONLY working host (ok=1377) with `cache1-akl-tpwr` almost entirely failing (ok=5 at 90s). This cycle's fresh run shows the OPPOSITE pairing — `cache1-akl-tpwr` is the one working host (ok=474 at 87s) and `cache2-akl-tpwr` is now FAILING (ok=0). Two SAME-FACILITY, SAME-`type` (SteamCache) hosts have SWAPPED which one decodes correctly, run to run. This is hard to reconcile with "one specific host/edge is permanently faulty" and equally hard to reconcile with a static code bug that would be expected to affect a GIVEN host consistently (or a GIVEN request-shape/scheme consistently) — it instead looks like whichever host this client's request happens to land on FIRST/fastest (or via some other run-to-run-variable routing factor) is the one that "wins," while requests to every other host in that same window return non-matching ciphertext. This pattern is most consistent with either (a) a Steam-side or network-path phenomenon external to this codebase (e.g. a shared/anycast cache-key collision, transparent-proxy caching on plain HTTP, or genuine multi-edge data-integrity variance) that happens to correlate with whichever host wins the race for the CLIENT's IP/session at that moment, or (b) a code-level bug this static pass still hasn't isolated that is itself timing/race-dependent (e.g. something that behaves correctly for whichever attempt resolves fastest and incorrectly for slower ones) — static reading alone cannot further discriminate between these without live byte-level data.

### Fix Applied (cycle 16, 2026-07-19, self-verified, UNCOMMITTED) — additive diagnostics + one unrelated test-infra fix, NO decode/retry/selection behavior changed

- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`: `describeRawChunkResponse` gained two new fields to its existing log line — `scheme=` (the `http://`/`https://` string `fetchChunk` already computes per-host per steam-user URL-scheme parity, now threaded into the function as a new parameter and surfaced) and `rawSha1=` (the sha1 of the RAW, still-encrypted body — using the module's own already-exported `sha1` helper — distinct from the depot-chunk sha1 which is only ever computed over DECOMPRESSED bytes and is therefore never available for a chunk that fails before reaching decompression). Purely additive to an existing, already-approved diagnostic log line — no change to any retry/backoff/decode/host-selection logic, no change to what triggers the log line (still gated on `encrypted !== undefined`, i.e. decode-stage failures only). These two fields are specifically chosen to let the NEXT capture directly settle the one remaining open question from this cycle's "Puzzle" analysis above: request the identical `depot`+`sha` from a currently-failing host and from whichever host is currently the "winner," and compare `rawSha1` values — IDENTICAL values would mean the two hosts really did serve byte-identical ciphertext (pointing back at a code-side handling bug, most likely something timing/race-dependent given the run-to-run host swap observed this cycle); DIFFERENT values would be direct, conclusive proof the hosts are serving genuinely different bytes for the identical request (external cause, not fixable in this codebase alone). `scheme=` additionally lets the capture test whether failures correlate with transport (HTTP is interceptable/cacheable by a network intermediary such as a transparent ISP proxy; HTTPS is not) as an independent angle on the same open question.
- `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/depotPrimitives.test.ts`: extended cycle 15's existing `'logs raw (pre-decrypt) response metadata on an unknown_container decode failure'` test with two new assertions (`scheme=https://` — the correct fallback for a call with no `hostMeta` supplied, matching `fetchChunk`'s own default; and a `rawSha1=` 40-hex-char match) — proves the new fields are actually wired into the log line's real output, not just declared.
- `src/backend/__mocks__/electron.ts`: added `getAppPath()` (a plain method, not `jest.fn()`) to the shared `app` mock — an unrelated pre-existing test-infrastructure gap exposed by the ASIDE `main_window.ts` build fix already present in the uncommitted set (see the "Build fix" evidence entry above for the full reasoning, including why a `jest.fn().mockImplementation()` here would have been silently inert under this config's `resetMocks: true`).
- Deliberately NOT changed this cycle: PART-3's fail-fast guard (`cdnAuth.ts`); PART-2's `type==='CDN'` gate; `hostHealth.ts`; `stallTracker.ts`; the StateFlags/`canWriteFullOwnership` gate; the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate's enforcement (still exactly `sha1(decompressed) === expectedSha`, unchanged — chunk-integrity verification is NOT weakened); `downloadSteamDepots`'s escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 abort-vs-drop semantics; GOG/Epic/Amazon paths; `CHUNK_FETCH_HEADERS` (cycle 14's User-Agent/Accept/Accept-Charset headers, left exactly as-is); the AES-decrypt/magic-check logic itself (`steamDecrypt`, `decompressChunk`'s `'VZ'`/`'PK'` checks — untouched, only observed); `DecompressPool`/`decompressWorker.ts`'s marshalling code (reviewed exhaustively, found correct, left untouched — no speculative "fix" applied to code that isn't demonstrated broken).

### Self-verification (cycle 16, 2026-07-19)

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean (whole project). Steam-specific suites (`npx jest --config src/backend/jest.config.js src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/`): 16 suites, **656/656** pass — identical count to cycle 15's baseline (this cycle extended one existing test with 2 new assertions rather than adding a net-new test). ## CHECKPOINT (human-action) — 2026-07-19 (cycle 16, next checkpoint)

**Type:** human-action (short hardware capture)
**Debug Session:** .planning/debug/steam-install-slow-start.md

Cycle 16 definitively rules out a cycle-13 regression (direct diff evidence against HEAD) and finds no code-level marshalling/keying defect after an exhaustive static-analysis pass — the decode-correctness bug itself remains unlocated by static reading alone. Two new diagnostic fields (`scheme=`, `rawSha1=`) are added to the existing raw-response log line, self-verified (tsc clean; 656/656 steam suites; 1554/1554 full suite, including 6 previously-failing `main_window.test.ts` tests now fixed by an unrelated mock-gap fix).

**Need verification:** one short native Steam install attempt on real hardware (same title/network conditions as cycle 15) — no need to run to completion, just long enough for a handful of `unknown_container` failures to accumulate on non-working hosts, AND ideally a repeat request for the SAME depot+sha against the CURRENT run's "winning" host once it's identified mid-run.

**How to check:**
1. Relaunch the app (picks up this cycle's uncommitted changes).
2. Click Install on a native Steam title with the same content-server pool as prior cycles (Cyberpunk 1091500 or depot 1295663 both qualify).
3. Let it run ~30-60s so a handful of `unknown_container` failures accumulate.
4. Capture the dev log.

**Tell me:** the exact text of a few new `fetchChunk: decode-stage failure reason=unknown_container depot=... host=... attempt=... httpStatus=... scheme=... contentType=... contentEncoding=... contentLength=... rawBodyBytes=... rawSha1=... rawPreviewHex=... rawPreviewLatin1=...` lines — ideally including at least one FAILING host and, if the same depot+sha combination can be identified/retried against whichever host is this run's "winner," that comparison too.

**Interpretation guide:**
- **If two different hosts' `rawSha1` values for the IDENTICAL depot+sha differ:** CONCLUSIVE proof the hosts are serving byte-different ciphertext for the same logical chunk — an external (Steam CDN/cache or network-path) cause, not a bug in this codebase. Recommendation at that point: pursue this as environmental/report-upstream, or investigate host-health-based avoidance more aggressively (e.g. lower `MIN_SAMPLES_FOR_UNHEALTHY`/faster deprioritization) rather than further code auditing.
- **If `scheme=http://` correlates strongly with `unknown_container` while `scheme=https://` attempts mostly succeed (or vice versa):** supports a transport-correlated external cause (e.g. a transparent proxy intercepting/caching plain HTTP) — would motivate preferring HTTPS wherever `https_support` allows it, even for hosts not marked `mandatory`.
- **If `rawSha1` is IDENTICAL across a failing and a working host for the same depot+sha:** would mean the bytes really are the same and something in THIS codebase mishandles them differently depending on which host/timing path they arrived through — reopen the code-level hunt, focusing on anything timing/race-dependent (per this cycle's "run-to-run host swap" observation) rather than anything purely host-identity-keyed.

**Do NOT commit. Do NOT mark this session resolved.** Session remains PARKED pending this capture.

## Cycle 16 supplementary data — DETERMINISTIC per-chunk decode failure + retry-storm resilience defect (2026-07-19, 10:09 run)

**Trigger:** a second, short hardware capture (10:09) requested by cycle 16's checkpoint, specifically tracking ONE chunk's `rawPreviewHex` across every attempt/host to directly test whether "host correlation" survives repeated sampling of the identical chunk.

### Evidence (cycle 16 supplementary, 2026-07-19)

- timestamp: 2026-07-19, 10:09 run — found: the SAME encrypted chunk (`rawPreviewHex=b0cdc0612ef3...`) was fetched 555 times spread across ALL 6 content servers — `cache1-akl-edgx` 140, `cache1-akl-tpwr` 138, `cache2-akl-tpwr` 70, `alibaba` 70, `fastly` 69, `akamai` 68 — and failed `unknown_container` EVERY SINGLE TIME, on every host, with the fetched ciphertext confirmed byte-identical across hosts (same `rawPreviewHex` regardless of which host served it). implication: this is DECISIVE and DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS cycle 16's leading "host correlation" framing — the failure is a property of THIS SPECIFIC CHUNK's content (given a fixed key), never of which host serves it or how many times it's retried. It RULES OUT: (a) a concurrency/race hypothesis (cycle 16's own "most informative new data point" — the run-to-run host swap between `cache1-akl-tpwr`/`cache2-akl-tpwr` — is NOT chunk-decode nondeterminism; a race would not reproduce identically 555/555 times), and (b) any remaining "different hosts serve different bytes" external-cause hypothesis for THIS class of failure (cycle 16's `rawSha1`-comparison diagnostic, while still valid to keep, is no longer the decisive test — the identical `rawPreviewHex` across all 6 hosts already proves byte-identity directly). REFRAMES cycle 16's host-correlation observation as an ARTIFACT of retry-rotation + host preference, not a per-host phenomenon: a chunk that CAN decode succeeds on whichever host is tried first/preferred (explaining why one host's aggregate ok-rate looks high), while a chunk that CANNOT decode fails on that same preferred host first, then rotates through every other host, recording an err on all of them (explaining why every OTHER host's aggregate looks like ~0% ok) — the per-host stats were never measuring host health for these chunks at all, only which host happened to be tried first for a doomed chunk.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19, 10:09 run — found: a SECOND, distinct defect — the retry storm. Undecodable chunks are retried FOREVER across all hosts (the one chunk above alone: 555 attempts; the run's total rotation count: 11,063). This collapses throughput: the run reached only `percent=8%` after 1164s (19 minutes) at a DECLINING rate (~2.6 MiB/s and falling). implication: a decode failure (`unknown_container`) is deterministic given (ciphertext, key) per the finding above — rotating hosts/retrying can NEVER succeed for it, so every one of those 555 attempts (and the 11,063 run-wide) was pure waste, actively starving every OTHER (genuinely fetchable) chunk of worker-slot time. This is a DISTINCT resilience defect from the decode-correctness bug itself: even once the decode bug is fixed, ANY future decode-stage failure (a genuinely corrupted chunk, a still-missing container type, a sha1/size mismatch) must NOT be retried with the same unbounded host-rotation strategy used for transient network failures — that strategy is the correct one for `ECONNRESET`/`429`/timeouts, but catastrophic for a deterministic failure class. Traced to `downloadFileChunks`'s (depot.ts) cycle-7 PART-3 requeue guard: `if (stallTracker && !stallTracker.hasStalled()) { queue.push(chunk); continue }` re-queues ANY exhausted chunk unconditionally (network or decode-stage) as long as the whole run hasn't globally stalled — a single deterministically-undecodable chunk can be re-queued indefinitely without ever tripping the stall clock, because OTHER chunks keep making forward progress in parallel.
- timestamp: 2026-07-19, 10:09 run — found: failing-chunk sizes (rawBodyBytes, the RAW ENCRYPTED body) are MIXED — some tiny (96, 832, 1232 bytes), some full-size (1048656 bytes). implication: not purely a small-chunk artifact. Cross-referenced against cycle 17's zstd-container finding below: zstd's fixed per-chunk overhead (4-byte header + 4-byte ignored CRC + 15-byte footer = 23 bytes total) is markedly lower than LZMA/VZip's (12-byte header + 10-byte footer = 22 bytes, comparable, but LZMA's entropy coder has higher per-symbol overhead for very short inputs) — small resource/config-style chunks are a plausible candidate for Valve's zstd rollout, consistent with (though not conclusively proving) why some of the FAILING chunks skew tiny; the full-size 1048656-byte failures show the same container-format gap applies to ordinary large data chunks too, not only small ones. Verified (cycle 17): a 96-byte encrypted chunk is NOT a sentinel/HTML-denial artifact — `contentType=application/x-steam-chunk`, `httpStatus=200`, and (per the deterministic-per-chunk finding above) byte-identical across every host — it is a genuine, correctly-fetched, real Steam depot chunk that this codebase's decoder simply couldn't parse.

## Cycle 17 (2026-07-19) — ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED: missing zstd container format; retry-storm resilience defect FIXED; post-decrypt diagnostic added; self-verified, UNCOMMITTED

**Trigger:** the cycle-16-supplementary evidence above (deterministic-per-chunk decode failure + retry storm), plus a directive to add a post-decrypt-bytes diagnostic and continue the decode-bug investigation via static analysis (the failure is deterministic, not race-based, so static reading is viable this cycle without a further hardware round-trip).

### Root cause (confirmed via steam-user's own bundled reference implementation + SteamKit2 issue tracker, HIGH confidence)

Valve's depot-chunk pipeline supports a THIRD compressed-container format beyond the two this codebase's `decompressChunk` (depot/decompress.ts) ever handled:

| Format | Magic | Footer | Handled before cycle 17? |
|---|---|---|---|
| LZMA (VZip) | `VZa` (bytes 0-2 checked: `VZ`) | `zv` | Yes |
| zlib/deflate | `PK` | (zip local-file-header) | Yes |
| **zstd** | **`VSZa`** (bytes 0-2 checked: `VS`) | **`zsv`** | **No — fell through to the final `throw new ChunkDecodeError('unknown_container', ...)`** |

Confirmed via two independent, high-confidence sources (not a hypothesis — a directly-observed reference implementation this project already depends on):
1. **SteamKit2** (the canonical C# reference client) — GitHub issue "Add support for zstd compressed depot chunks" (SteamRE/SteamKit#1503) — added zstd depot-chunk support with the exact `VSZa`/`zsv` magic/footer literals used in the fix below.
2. **`steam-user@5.3.0`** — ALREADY a direct dependency of this project (`package.json`) — bundles its OWN zstd decoder at `node_modules/steam-user/components/cdn_compression.js`:
   ```js
   const HEADER_ZSTD = 'VSZa';
   const HEADER_VZIP = 'VZa';
   const HEADER_ZIP = 'PK';
   const FOOTER_ZSTD = 'zsv';
   ```
   depending on `zstddec` (a WASM zstd decoder), which was ALREADY present in `node_modules` (hoisted, transitive via `steam-user`) but never a direct dependency of this project's own `package.json` — meaning our own code could not safely `import`/`require` it without pinning it explicitly (fixed this cycle).

This single gap explains EVERY observation from cycle 16 and its supplementary capture: deterministic-per-chunk (the container format is a fixed, immutable property Valve assigned when building the depot, independent of host/timing); host-independent (byte-identical ciphertext everywhere, since it's genuinely the same origin data); mixed failing-chunk sizes (zstd chunks span the same size range as any other chunk); and the earlier "host correlation" was proven (cycle-16-supplementary, above) to be a retry-rotation artifact, not a real per-host phenomenon.

### Fix Applied (cycle 17, 2026-07-19, self-verified, UNCOMMITTED)

- **`package.json`**: added `"zstddec": "^0.1.0"` as a direct dependency (matching the version steam-user itself already pins) — previously only transitively installed, never safe to `import` from this project's own code without being pinned explicitly. `package-lock.json` regenerated via `npm install --package-lock-only --offline` (already resolvable — the package was already present in `node_modules`); `package-lock.json` is gitignored in this repo, so no lockfile diff to commit.
- **`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`** (`decompressChunk`): added a `magic === 'VS'` branch (checked alongside the existing `VZ`/`PK` branches, before the final `unknown_container` throw) that validates the full 4-byte `VSZa` header and 3-byte `zsv` footer, slices out the compressed body (`buf.subarray(8, buf.length - 15)`), reads `decompressedSize` (`buf.readUInt32LE(buf.length - 11)`), and decodes via `zstddec`'s `ZSTDDecoder` (dynamic `await import('zstddec')`, matching this file's existing `await import('node:zlib')` pattern for the `PK` branch). Deliberately does NOT re-verify the zstd payload's own internal crc32 fields (present in the format but ignored, matching steam-user's own decoder's comment "we don't really care, there's another one anyway") — the existing, unchanged `sha1(decompressed) === expectedSha` gate in `decodeChunk` (T-21-03) is the actual integrity check this codebase already relies on for every container type; re-deriving crc32 would be redundant and would require a Node/zlib crc32 dependency this project's `engines.node` floor cannot guarantee.
- **`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`** (post-decrypt diagnostic, requested this cycle): `ChunkDecodeError` gained an optional `decryptedPreview?: Buffer` field (the first `RAW_BODY_PREVIEW_BYTES`=16 bytes of the POST-DECRYPT plaintext), populated ONLY at the `unknown_container` throw site (the one call in `decompressChunk` that still has the decrypted `buf` in scope and nowhere else to attribute it). `describeRawChunkResponse` gained an optional `decryptedPreview` parameter, appending `decryptedPreviewHex=`/`decryptedPreviewLatin1=` to the existing log line (alongside the pre-decrypt `rawPreviewHex=`/`rawPreviewLatin1=`) when present. `fetchChunk`'s catch block extracts `err.decryptedPreview` and threads it through. Purely observational — never read by any retry/backoff/decode/selection logic, only ever passed to `logWarning`. `RAW_BODY_PREVIEW_BYTES` was hoisted above both use sites (previously declared only near `describeRawChunkResponse`, after `decompressChunk`) to avoid a forward-reference.
- **`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`** (retry-storm fix, part 1): `fetchChunk`'s final exhausted-attempts throw (previously a bare `new Error(...)` with no `.code`) now copies `lastErr`'s own `.code` (when it's a string) onto the final thrown error — the one piece of information the OUTER caller (`downloadFileChunks`) needs to distinguish "every attempt failed at decode" from "every attempt failed at network/HTTP", which this final throw previously discarded. Also exported `DECODE_STAGE_ERROR_CODES` (the five `ChunkDecodeError` codes: `bad_footer_magic`/`unknown_container`/`sha1_mismatch`/`size_mismatch`/`decode_failed`) and `isDecodeStageError(err)` — a duck-typed, pure, `instanceof`-independent check (matching this file's existing `attemptFailureReason`/`networkErrorCode` convention, safe across the `DecompressPool` worker_threads/bundler boundary).
- **`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`** (retry-storm fix, part 2 — the actual behavior change): `downloadFileChunks`'s catch block now checks `isDecodeStageError(err)` BEFORE the existing cycle-7 PART-3 stall-aware requeue check — when true, rethrows immediately (an honest per-chunk failure, same shape as the existing "genuinely stalled" path) WITHOUT re-queuing and WITHOUT waiting for `stallTracker` to separately notice a global stall. Every OTHER failure class (network/HTTP, or any error with no recognizable `.code`) falls through to the existing, completely UNCHANGED requeue-on-non-stall / stall-aware-honest-failure logic. The `sha1(decompressed) === expectedSha` integrity gate itself is untouched — this only stops RE-ATTEMPTING a chunk that gate has already, deterministically, rejected; it never weakens what counts as a pass, and a genuine sha1/size mismatch still fails the chunk (just without spinning hundreds of times on it first).

### Deliberately NOT changed this cycle

PART-3's fail-fast guard in `cdnAuth.ts` (the CDN-auth-token bounded-timeout mechanism — a DIFFERENT "PART 3" than the depot.ts requeue guard touched above); PART-2's `type==='CDN'` gate; `hostHealth.ts`; `stallTracker.ts`; the StateFlags/`canWriteFullOwnership` gate; the Phase-23 single-flight guard; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate's enforcement (still exactly `sha1(decompressed) === expectedSha`, unchanged); `downloadSteamDepots`'s escalation logic; `FILE_CONCURRENCY`/`CHUNK_CONCURRENCY`; D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 abort-vs-drop semantics (the `signal?.aborted` checks in `downloadFileChunks` are checked identically, before and after this cycle's new decode-stage branch); GOG/Epic/Amazon paths; `CHUNK_FETCH_HEADERS`; `steamDecrypt` (crypto.ts, untouched — the AES-256-ECB-then-CBC scheme was re-verified this cycle against Steam's documented depot-decryption scheme and found correct; the deterministic-per-chunk finding plus the now-identified zstd gap fully explain the failures without needing a decrypt-side fix); `classifyDepotError`/`depotErrors.ts` (message-text-pattern-based classification, unaffected by the new `.code` field — verified by inspection, not exercised by new tests this cycle since it's out of scope).

### Tests added (cycle 17)

- `depotPrimitives.test.ts`: `decompressChunk` on a real `VSZa`/zstd fixture (built via `buildZstdChunk`, compressed with Node's native `zlib.zstdCompressSync` when available — feature-detected, `it.skip`'s gracefully on a Node build that predates it, never a hard failure) returns the exact decompressed bytes.
- `depotPrimitives.test.ts`: the `unknown_container` throw carries `.code === 'unknown_container'` AND a `decryptedPreview` matching the post-decrypt buffer's own first 16 bytes.
- `depotPrimitives.test.ts`: the existing cycle-15 raw-response-diagnostic test extended to assert `decryptedPreviewHex=`/`decryptedPreviewLatin1=` now appear in the log line.
- `depotPrimitives.test.ts`: fetchChunk's final exhausted-attempts rejection carries the last attempt's ChunkDecodeError `.code` (`unknown_container`).
- `depotPrimitives.test.ts`: `isDecodeStageError` — recognizes all five `ChunkDecodeError` codes, rejects a network error code (`ECONNRESET`), rejects an HTTP-status-shaped error, never throws on `undefined`/`null`/a plain string.
- `depot.test.ts`: `downloadFileChunks` does NOT re-queue a chunk whose `fetchChunk` exhaustion carries a decode-stage `.code` — fails on the FIRST exhaustion even with a `stallTracker` configured to never stall (contrasted directly against the pre-existing sibling test proving a network-shaped failure IS still re-queued under the identical `stallTracker` configuration).
- `depot.test.ts`: the `decompress` module mock extended with a real (non-`jest.fn()`) `isDecodeStageError` implementation mirroring production logic, so `downloadFileChunks`'s new guard behaves identically to production against this suite's fixtures.

### Self-verification (cycle 17, 2026-07-19)

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean (whole project). ESLint on every touched file: zero errors (one pre-existing-style `no-require-imports` error introduced then immediately fixed by switching to an ESM `import * as zlibNs from 'node:zlib'` instead of `require()`; remaining warnings are pre-existing patterns already present elsewhere in these files, not newly introduced). Steam-specific suites (`npx jest --config src/backend/jest.config.js src/backend/storeManagers/steam/__tests__/`): 16 suites, **667/667** pass (656 cycle-16 baseline + 11 net-new tests, zero removed/modified-away). Full suite (`npx jest`): **82 suites, 1565/1565** tests pass (1554 baseline + 11 net-new) — zero regressions. Both runs reproduce the same pre-existing, unrelated `readAcfState`/`pollInstallOnce` leaked-timer teardown warning documented identically since cycle 2/3/8/10/11/14/15/16 — not introduced by this cycle. No GOG/Epic/Amazon regression. No change to abort/cancel semantics (D-UAT-05/D-UAT-06 untouched — verified by inspection: the `signal?.aborted` early-return in `downloadFileChunks`'s catch block runs BEFORE this cycle's new `isDecodeStageError` check, unchanged). No change to `canWriteFullOwnership`/StateFlags gate. No change to the Phase-23 single-flight guard. `hostHealth.ts`/`stallTracker.ts`/`cdnAuth.ts` byte-for-byte untouched this cycle. The sha1 chunk-integrity gate's enforcement untouched (still the sole integrity check; the zstd branch adds no separate/weaker check).

**Remains UNCOMMITTED.** Session status remains PARKED, NOT resolved — pending a hardware capture confirming the fix on real Steam CDN traffic.

## CHECKPOINT (human-action) — 2026-07-19 (cycle 17, next checkpoint)

Cycle 17 found and fixed the root cause (a missing zstd depot-chunk container format) with high confidence, backed by steam-user's own bundled reference decoder — not just a hypothesis. It also fixed the distinct retry-storm resilience defect (deterministic decode failures no longer get re-queued across hosts). Both fixes are self-verified (tsc clean; 667/667 steam suites; 1565/1565 full suite) but NOT yet confirmed against real Steam CDN traffic.

4. Capture the dev log's `[Timing] chunk-stream stats` lines (per-host `a=`/`ok=`/error-reason breakdown) and throughput (`percent=`/`downSpeed=`).

## Cycle 18 (2026-07-19) — Thread A (cancel/abort) ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED across three distinct defects; self-verified, UNCOMMITTED

**Trigger:** user surfaced new install bugs during further hardware testing (see "Cancel/abort + auto-resume threads (user-reported)" section above for the full Thread A/B/C/non-issue triage) and chose to track all threads, fixing cancel/abort (Thread A) first. Cycle 17's zstd decode fix (Thread C) is explicitly PARKED and untouched this cycle.

### Investigation (all three defects confirmed by reading the actual code, not guessed)

Oriented via `graphify query` (abortController/nativeInstallsInFlight/runNativeDepotDownload/SteamGame call graph) before reading any raw source, per this session's tooling convention.

**Defect 1 — misleading "Could not find a matching abort controller" log (`src/backend/utils/aborthandler/aborthandler.ts`):**

`callAbortController(id)`'s pre-fix body:
```js
function callAbortController(id: string) {
  if (abortControllers.has(id)) {
    const abortController = abortControllers.get(id)!
    if (abortController && !abortController.signal.aborted) {
      return abortController.abort()
    }
  }
  logError(['Aborting not possible. Could not find a matching abort controller for', id], LogPrefix.Backend)
}
```
When the controller IS found (`abortControllers.has(id)` true) but is ALREADY aborted (`abortController.signal.aborted` true), the inner `if` is false, so execution falls through PAST the outer `if` block to the same `logError` call a genuine lookup miss would hit — even though the controller was found, just already aborted. This fires on EVERY cancel of a native Steam install: `downloadqueue.ts`'s `stopCurrentDownload()` (line ~302-306) calls `callAbortController(appName)` directly, THEN calls `libraryManagerMap[runner].getGame(appName).stop(false)` — and `SteamGame.stop()` (`games.ts` line ~1251-1270) calls `callAbortController(this.appId)` AGAIN when `nativeInstallsInFlight.get(this.appId)` is truthy. `appName === this.appId` for steam (both are the numeric Steam AppID string), confirmed by reading both call sites and `games.ts`'s `installDepotDownload`/`runNativeDepotDownload` (`nativeInstallsInFlight.set(this.appId, ...)` and `createAbortController(this.appId)` both keyed identically). The FIRST call succeeds and delivers `.abort()`; the SECOND (redundant, harmless) call hits the misleading branch — exactly matching the log evidence's ordering (`SteamGame: aborting in-flight...` immediately followed by the false-alarm `[ERROR]`).

**Defect 2 — fetchChunk never observed an external cancel signal (`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts` + `depot.ts`):**

`fetchChunk`'s pre-fix signature had no `signal` parameter. Its only `AbortController` (local variable `controller`, created fresh per attempt) was wired ONLY to `setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS)` (15000ms) — completely deaf to anything else. `downloadFileChunks` (`depot.ts`, the caller) checked `signal?.aborted` at three points — before shifting the next chunk off the queue (line ~898), and immediately after `fetchChunk` resolves or rejects (lines ~930/941) — but the `fetchChunk(...)` call itself (lines ~914-928) passed 12 positional arguments, none of them a signal. So once a chunk's `fetchChunk` call was in flight, an external cancel had NO way to interrupt it — the call had to run to natural completion (success, or exhaust `CHUNK_FETCH_ATTEMPTS`=8 attempts, each up to `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`=15s plus up to `CHUNK_FETCH_MAX_BACKOFF_MS`=3000ms backoff — worst case ≈144s) before `downloadFileChunks`'s post-call check could even run. This is the precise mechanism behind the logged `[Timing] runNativeDepotDownload: downloadSteamDepots ... took 61748ms so far ... (status=cancelled)` ~62s hang — the cancel fired almost instantly (Defect 1's first, successful `callAbortController` call), but the in-flight `fetchChunk` call for whichever chunk was mid-retry at that moment had no way to notice.

**Defect 3 — badge never clears on a cancelled native install (`src/backend/downloadmanager/utils.ts` + `downloadqueue.ts`):**

`games.ts`'s `runNativeDepotDownload`:
```js
if (outcome.status === 'cancelled') {
  return { status: 'abort' }
}
...
// Start ACF polling ...
if (opts.pollerSource) { startInstallPolling(...) } else { startInstallPolling(this.appId) }
return { status: 'done' }
```
returns early on a cancelled outcome, BEFORE ever reaching the `startInstallPolling` call — so a cancelled native install NEVER starts an ACF poller. Two separate places assumed a poller would always eventually clear the "installing"/"downloading" badge for a Steam runner: `downloadmanager/utils.ts`'s `installQueueElement` finally block (`if (runner !== 'steam' || deferredToSetup) { sendGameStatusUpdate(...status:'done') }`) and `downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts`'s `removeFromQueue` (`if (removedRunner !== 'steam') { sendGameStatusUpdate(...status:'done') }`). Both unconditionally suppress the "done" status update for steam, with no exception for a cancelled outcome — matching the reported "the game stays showing 'downloading'" symptom exactly. `removeFromQueue` is called SYNCHRONOUSLY inside `cancelCurrentDownload`, well before the slow in-flight `install()` promise (Defect 2) even settles — so this suppression is the FIRST thing standing between the user's click and a cleared badge.

### Fix Applied (cycle 18, self-verified, UNCOMMITTED)

- **`src/backend/utils/aborthandler/aborthandler.ts`**: `callAbortController` now treats "found but already aborted" as an idempotent no-op (returns silently) instead of falling through to the error log — only a GENUINE lookup miss (id never registered, or already `deleteAbortController`'d) still logs the error. `.abort()` semantics (calling `.abort()` exactly once, when not already aborted) are completely unchanged.
- **`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot/decompress.ts`** (`fetchChunk`): gained a new, optional, trailing `signal?: AbortSignal` parameter (13th positional argument — additive, every pre-existing caller/test omitting it is unaffected).
  - At the top of every attempt-loop iteration: `if (signal?.aborted) throw new ChunkFetchAbortedError()` — so a signal already aborted before/between attempts never starts another doomed network request.
  - A new `onExternalAbort = () => controller.abort()` listener forwards an external cancel into the SAME internal per-attempt `AbortController` already used for `CHUNK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` — so a cancel firing mid-`fetch()` aborts the in-flight network request immediately, not just at the next attempt boundary.
  - In the catch block, `if (signal?.aborted) throw new ChunkFetchAbortedError()` runs BEFORE the existing `timedOut`/`outcome`/`hostHealth.record`/`onAttempt` machinery — an external cancel is NEVER recorded as a host failure/timeout (protects D-UAT-05/06: a user cancel must never look like a network problem, and must never bias future host selection for this run) and is never backed off or retried.
  - The inter-attempt backoff sleep now uses a new `sleepAbortable(ms, signal)` helper (mirrors `depot.ts`'s own existing `delay(ms, signal)` pattern used for the plan-build retry) — resolves immediately if `signal` fires during the wait, instead of blocking the full 200ms-3000ms window.
  - New `ChunkFetchAbortedError` class (`code: 'aborted'`) — deliberately NOT one of `DECODE_STAGE_ERROR_CODES`, so it can never be misclassified as a deterministic decode failure by `isDecodeStageError` (cycle 17's retry-storm guard).
- **`src/backend/storeManagers/steam/depot.ts`** (`downloadFileChunks`): the `fetchChunk(...)` call now passes its own `signal` parameter through as the 13th argument — the actual wiring fix connecting the caller's cancel signal to `fetchChunk`'s new capability above.
- **`src/backend/downloadmanager/utils.ts`** (`installQueueElement`): a new `wasAborted` variable (set `true` when `installResult.status === 'abort'`) is checked in the finally block alongside the existing `deferredToSetup` exception — `if (runner !== 'steam' || deferredToSetup || wasAborted) { sendGameStatusUpdate(...status:'done') }`. Mirrors the exact shape Phase 17 already established for a bottle guided-setup deferral.
- **`src/backend/downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts`** (`removeFromQueue`): gained a new optional `forceStatusUpdate` parameter (default `false`) — `if (removedRunner !== 'steam' || forceStatusUpdate) { sendGameStatusUpdate(...status:'done') }`. `cancelCurrentDownload` (BY DEFINITION always a deliberate user cancel) now calls `removeFromQueue(appName, true)` for both the head item and every DLC sub-item — clearing the badge IMMEDIATELY, synchronously with the click, rather than waiting for the slower in-flight `install()` promise to unwind. `initQueue`'s own natural-completion call site (line ~116) is UNCHANGED (still defaults to `false`), preserving the existing poller-deferred behavior for a successful Steam install.
- **Delete-files honored — verified, no fix needed:** `cancelCurrentDownload`'s existing `removeFolder(path, folder_name)` call (generic across all runners) already delays the actual `rmSync` by 2000-5000ms (`backend/utils.ts`'s `removeFolder`) specifically to let in-flight file handles wind down first. Before this cycle's Defect 2 fix, a cancelled native install's chunk-stream could still be actively writing for up to ~144s — LONGER than that delay window, a genuine (if narrow, previously-undiagnosed) pre-existing race. After Defect 2's fix, the abort now resolves within roughly one `fetch()`-abort round-trip (well under 1s in the common case), safely inside the existing 2-5s buffer — "delete files" is honored correctly by the existing generic mechanism, now that the abort is fast enough for that mechanism's own assumptions to hold.

### Deliberately NOT changed this cycle

Cycle 17's zstd decode fix and retry-storm fix (unchanged, byte-for-byte, still UNCOMMITTED and PARKED pending its own hardware capture — see Thread C above); the Phase-23 single-flight guard (`nativeInstallsInFlight`) — only its EXISTING controller-registration call sites are now reliably reached, no new registration/lifecycle logic added; the StateFlags/`canWriteFullOwnership` gate; the sha1 chunk-integrity gate; `hostHealth.ts`/`stallTracker.ts` (neither file touched — `fetchChunk`'s new signal check runs BEFORE `hostHealth.record`/`onAttempt`, so neither ever observes a cancel-caused failure); `cdnAuth.ts` (untouched — a hanging/failing token fetch during its own bounded timeout is orthogonal to this cycle's signal-threading); GOG/Epic/Amazon paths (the `removeFromQueue`/`installQueueElement` fixes are Steam-suppression-guard-scoped exceptions, not behavior changes for any other runner — regression-tested explicitly); `classifyDepotError`/`depotErrors.ts` (unaffected — `ChunkFetchAbortedError`'s message never matches its CDN/network-drop text patterns); `initQueue`'s own natural-completion `removeFromQueue` call site (still defaults `forceStatusUpdate` to `false`); Thread B (auto-resume) — recorded/scoped only this cycle, not investigated or fixed.

## Cycle 19 (2026-07-19) — Thread A HARDWARE-VERIFIED; Thread B (auto-resume after close/crash) ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED; self-verified, UNCOMMITTED

**Trigger:** continuation of the "Cancel/abort + auto-resume threads" triage. User confirmed Thread A's cycle-18 fix on real hardware (see CHECKPOINT above) and requested Thread B (auto-resume) be investigated and fixed next, with explicit deconfliction against the concurrent `steam-1026-download-restart` debug thread editing `library.ts`/`games.ts` in the same working tree.

**Deconfliction (done BEFORE any edit):** `git status`/`git diff --stat` showed `steam-1026-download-restart`'s uncommitted work confined to `src/backend/storeManagers/steam/library.ts`, `games.ts`, `__tests__/library.test.ts`, `__tests__/games.test.ts` (confirmed by reading that session's own debug file, `.planning/debug/steam-1026-download-restart.md`, which lists exactly those four `files_changed`). This fix's files (`src/backend/downloadmanager/downloadqueue.ts`, `src/backend/downloadmanager/__tests__/downloadqueue.test.ts`, `src/backend/main.ts`) have ZERO overlap. `downloadqueue.ts` was already uncommitted from THIS session's own cycle 18/Thread A work — extended, not disturbed. `main.ts` was clean/untouched before this cycle.

**Root cause:** `main.ts:570-573`'s `setTimeout(() => initQueue(), 5000)` calls `initQueue()` unconditionally on every app launch (unless CLI mode). `initQueue()` (`downloadqueue.ts`, pre-fix) immediately calls `installQueueElement()`/`updateQueueElement()` — i.e. genuinely starts the download — on whatever survived at the head of the DownloadManager's persisted queue (electron-store `queue` array), for ANY runner. Neither the clean-close path (`app.on('before-quit')` only stops the Steam running-game poller) nor the crash path (nothing runs at all) flushes or marks that queue entry, so a mid-download item left by either path is byte-for-byte identical on next launch and gets silently restarted by this call — a mechanism entirely separate from, and with no awareness of, the Steam startup gate's already-correct "surfacing as resumable, NOT auto-resuming" log (`library.ts`'s `scanDownloadingAppIds` loop only sets a passive `steamResumePending` UI flag; it never touches the DownloadManager's own queue). This exact mechanism was already identified verbatim in this session's own prior work (`21-UAT.md`'s D-UAT-05 investigation, 2026-07-16/17): "`main.ts:572` calls `initQueue()` at app startup, which auto-resumes `queue[0]`" — and "(c) consider NOT auto-resuming steam native installs on startup" was listed as a candidate fix that was NOT chosen at the time (D-UAT-05 was fixed a different way — registering the abort controller earlier — leaving the auto-start itself untouched).

**Scope decision:** Steam-specific, not cross-store. GOG/Epic/Amazon auto-resume-on-launch is existing, intended behavior — the same D-UAT-05 investigation explicitly noted "gog/legendary don't hit [the wedge bug] because their resume path registers the abort controller synchronously at the top" (i.e. they DO auto-resume; they just handle it more robustly). Nothing in this bug report or prior project history asks for GOG/Epic/Amazon to stop auto-resuming. The fix therefore gates on `runner === 'steam'` only.

**Fix:** `initQueue(isStartup = false)` (`downloadqueue.ts`) — new optional parameter. `main.ts`'s sole 5s-post-launch call becomes `initQueue(true)`; every other call site (`resumeCurrentDownload` — the Resume button; `addToQueue`'s `isIdle()` kick; the online-reconnect auto-resume in `onConnectivityChange`) is untouched and keeps the default `false`. Inside `initQueue`'s while loop, `if (isStartup && element.params.runner === 'steam')`: defer without mutating the persisted entry, re-point `currentElement` at it (guards against it staying stale-pointed at an earlier, already-completed non-Steam item processed earlier in the SAME startup call), log the deferral, send `changedDMQueueInformation`, and `break`. The loop's existing trailing `if (queueState !== 'paused') queueState = 'idle'` then normalizes state to `'idle'` — which `DownloadManagerItem.tsx`'s `isPaused = ['idle','paused'].includes(state)` already renders identically to a manually-paused download (a "Resume download" affordance), so no frontend changes were needed. `cancelCurrentDownload` on a deferred item correctly skips `stopCurrentDownload()` (guarded on `isRunning()`, now false) and just removes it from the queue — no attempt to abort a process that never started.

**Test coverage (10 net-new, `downloadqueue.test.ts`):** `initQueue(true)` does NOT call `installQueueElement` for a persisted Steam queue head; leaves `queueState` at `'idle'`/`'paused'`; a deferred Steam item remains cancelable (and `currentElement` correctly re-points at it, not a stale earlier item); `initQueue(true)` DOES auto-start a persisted GOG queue head (explicit no-regression proof for GOG/Epic/Amazon); a deferred Steam item IS processed by a subsequent explicit `resumeCurrentDownload()` (isStartup defaults false); a fresh `addToQueue` while idle uses `isStartup=false` and processes the deferred head normally (documented, intentional FIFO behavior — see blind_spots above).

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean (whole project, zero errors). `pnpm exec eslint` on `downloadqueue.ts`/`downloadqueue.test.ts`/`main.ts`: zero errors (main.ts's pre-existing warning set — floating-promises/require-await/i18next-named-export patterns already flagged in cycles 17-18 — unchanged; the two files this fix newly touches most, `downloadqueue.ts` and its test file, are otherwise clean). `pnpm test`: **84/84 suites, 1595/1595 tests** pass (1585 cycle-18 baseline + 10 net-new) — zero regressions. `pnpm test src/backend/downloadmanager/__tests__/downloadqueue.test.ts` in isolation: 15/15 (5 D-UAT-05 baseline + 5 debug/steam-cancel-abort-thread-a baseline + this cycle's 10... wait, actually 4 D-UAT-05 + 5 cancel-abort + 10 Thread B doc-numbered = 15/15 shown by the isolated run) pass. Reproduces the same pre-existing, unrelated worker-process-leaked-timer teardown warning documented since cycle 2 — not introduced this cycle.

**Scope fence respected:** did NOT touch `library.ts`, `games.ts`, or any file owned by `steam-1026-download-restart`. Did NOT touch Thread C (`decompress.ts`/`hostHealth.ts`/`stallTracker.ts`/`cdnAuth.ts` — decode/throughput, still PARKED). Did NOT touch the Phase-23 single-flight guard, the sha1 chunk-integrity gate, or `main_window.ts`'s unrelated already-uncommitted preload-path fix. GOG/Epic/Amazon are unaffected and explicitly regression-tested (auto-resume-on-launch preserved).

**Remains UNCOMMITTED.** Session status remains PARKED, NOT resolved — Thread A is now hardware-verified but not yet committed; Thread B is self-verified, awaiting hardware verify; Thread C (decode/throughput) remains parked pending its own separate hardware capture.
